mr president, commissioner andor, our good colleague mr cercas is making a very simple proposal. he's telling you to get off the bleachers and get on the field and play ball. the crisis has resulted in the need for business restructuring. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a good example of the need to ensure that the directive is applied in a uniform manner. the commission is the one who has to deal with the difficult ones. i repeat, in difficult times it is the european commission which must intervene. we are asking for the rights of workers to be respected and at the same time for competitiveness to be promoted.
mr president, i too would like to welcome this important initiative. the young people today are the greatest victims of the crisis and youth unemployment, as a major problem, now requires direct and dynamic interventions. in my country, greece, 56% of young people are out of the labour market, with particularly painful consequences both for their own lives and for the functioning of society as a whole. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the young europeans do not become a lost generation. this is why there should be leverage to pressure governments, the social partners and other stakeholders to adopt targeted actions without, however, operating at the expense of other policies of a social nature. i would therefore like to ask, in a period of widespread cuts in national, social and educational budgets, what specific ways and measures can you take to ensure that the youth guarantee is financed and implemented effectively in the member states, particularly those which are governed by memoranda?
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, gloom, austerity, unemployment and depression are over europe. unemployment is not just an economic issue, it is psychiatric in origin. we're driving a whole new generation into a mental anomaly. i therefore welcome, and cannot help myself, the initiative to discuss this matter. the commissioner, the plans for the youth guarantee schemes must go ahead. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area. it is necessary to understand that the austerity measures over europe and the unemployment of educated young people are creating a new scientific proletariat that will drown us.
madam president, in the light of all the interesting things that have been said by my colleagues, i would like to add that the situation is urgent. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this subject. not just from their own country, but from europe, with what that means for the competitiveness of our economy. it is therefore very important that we see that these measures must be taken immediately, bearing in mind that we already have programmes for young people, we already have programmes to make a substantial and serious evaluation so that we can draw the best conclusions to use them and avoid mistakes and the squandering of resources that have not paid off. i would also like to stress that unless there is a broader framework for development in the countries of the south, a balance between the countries of the european union, we will not be able to create the conditions for sustainable growth and stability which will bring investment, employment and retention of young people in their countries and in europe.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. madam president, the european solidarity fund was launched immediately after the 2002 floods, but climate change and other human interventions have led to an increase in the frequency and intensity of natural disasters. in 2005, the council blocked changes to the fund. this time the council must accept the commission's proposal, with the european parliament's amendments, to improve the functioning of the fund, especially in view of the increase in the frequency and intensity of natural disasters in europe. from 1980 to 2011, total damage in the european area from hydrological, geophysical and extreme events reached €455 billion, while natural disasters caused 110,000 deaths. insurance covered only 27% of the cases, and does not usually cover many of the catastrophic events. the fund should therefore play an active role in the effective recovery of areas and social life in the event of natural disasters, but the fund's contribution should be made in a way that improves policies and practices for preventing natural disasters and mitigating their consequences. the best way, of course, and the most economical way to deal with natural disasters is prevention. but the process also needs to be improved. to clarify the cases that can be supported by the fund, because 60% of applications were rejected in the previous period; to simplify administrative, bureaucratic procedures; to improve the time needed for financial resources to reach the member states from the european institutions and from the member states to the beneficiaries of compensation in the regions.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to join in the voice of all those who say that the solidarity fund is one of the main and most successful instruments of expression and solidarity available to the european union. in recent years the fund has been used on a number of occasions to provide aid to member states affected by disasters. however, some problems have been identified in the process of mobilising and implementing it, with the consequence that available resources are delayed in reaching the affected areas. a delay that creates problems in providing help at a time when that help is needed more than at any other time. that is why our socialist group in the european parliament supports a more flexible, efficient and simplified fund, easy and quick to mobilise, which will enable the european union to respond better to major disasters, providing timely relief for citizens in need. this must be the essence of the solidarity which our union offers its citizens.
mr president, the common thread running through all three reports is the allocation of public money to the large business groups in order to mobilise their accumulated capital and overcome the capitalist crisis at the expense of the workers. in particular, they call on the local administration to enter into partnerships with the european commission which will determine the eligibility of projects and the way funds are allocated not to meet the increased needs of the people which are particularly large today, such as public, free transport, seismic and flood protection, public education infrastructure, etc., but, instead, to new profitable areas for capital, such as waste management, the commercial exploitation of historical and cultural monuments, the liberalisation of energy production and distribution. the reports promote the anti-people europe 2020 strategy which already absorbs 63% of the community's resources in greece. 40% of the resources go back to the big monopoly groups and the remaining 60% goes to the greek oligarchy to intensify capitalist restructuring. the sharpening of geographical and social contradictions in the capitalist system is a matter of law.
in writing. the lisbon treaty introduced the territorial dimension as an objective of cohesion policy to exploit the strengths of all regions, manage concentration, improve regional connectivity and develop cooperation. the role of territorial development in cohesion policy is therefore considered to be of major importance. to further strengthen the role of territorial development in future cohesion policy, common rules and close cooperation are necessary for all five funds under the general regulation. the implementation of territorial strategies should also be the responsibility of local bodies and authorities, with the aim of ensuring that economic and social needs are met at the level closest to the citizen. moreover, providing opportunities for member states, regional and local authorities to use more than one european funding programme to support projects will improve the efficiency of european funding, enhance simplification and provide potential beneficiaries with easier access to funding. finally, the integrated territorial investment proposals can provide an opportunity for cities to meet their specific financing needs, and it is of particular importance that the instrument can be used in non-urban areas if it meets local needs.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
the next item on the agenda is the one-minute speeches (rule 150).
mr president, a new report on behalf of the international monetary fund published on 3 january 2013 admits that the forecasting models used to promote crisis response policies contain significant overestimations of fiscal multipliers. the commission report estimates that any multiplier above 0.5, especially for greece, even in healthy fiscal periods causes debt to rise. but the fiscal multiplier for greece is estimated at 1 and maybe 1.7. this has the effect that restrictive policies lead not to a reduction but to a dramatic increase in debt, making it unsustainable. following these wrong forecasting models and decisions for forcible fiscal adjustment, the greek debt crisis will cost european citizens 240 billion euros by 2016. for the greek people, however, it results in more than doubling unemployment, collapse of the real economy, even viable businesses, and poverty for a significant proportion of the population. by 2016, 65 billion euros will have been taken from the greek people. but a stagnation at 2011 levels, not a worsening of the recession, could reduce debt by 21%. the commission must therefore contribute to the political dialogue in greece on an alternative, balanced and effective plan for getting out of the crisis.
the debate is closed.
the next item is the report by charles tannock, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on the eu strategy for the horn of africa (2012/2026 (ini)) (a7-0408/2012).
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank you for this opportunity to talk on behalf of vice-president/high representative ashton about the strategy in the horn of africa. i would also like to thank charles tannock for his valuable report. the situation in the horn has evolved since the approval of our horn of africa strategy in november 2011, including the appointment of the eu special representative for the horn of africa, mr alexander rondos. we must, of course, do our utmost to secure the progress achieved and we must prevent the region from being affected by the developments in the sahel. the turning point for the region has been the changes in somalia. we have a new and more representative government, voted in by a new parliament. al-shabaab is a diminished force, thanks in no small part to our support to the african union. piracy, as was stated in the report, has been reduced. our work on the political and security sectors has helped pave the way for more effective investment by us in construction and development. that is what high representative ashton means by a comprehensive approach. by providing the support to our mission and maintaining atalanta to secure the high seas from piracy, by training the future officer corps of the somali army through the eu training mission, by financing nestor to create maritime policy capacity and by supporting rule of law programmes in more parts of somalia, the eu has laid the foundations for a somalia that can be at peace. we have created the space for politics to play their part. now we are opening the path for development activities to resume and we are helping make the entire region of the horn of africa more secure. we are also pleased to announce too that, following the high representative’s invitation, the new president of somalia, hassan sheikh mohamud, will be in brussels at the end of the month to meet with the foreign affairs council and with parliament. it is also worth noting that natural resources are changing the economies of the region and that this is of course a challenge for african countries. several of them have discovered oil and gas resources. if it is not hydrocarbons, as in sudan, uganda, kenya or even somalia, it is water, which ethiopia has in such abundance and with which it could potentially supply hydroelectricity to the entire region. we must now work with the region to turn these resources into something that adds strategic value to the security and prosperity of the whole region. we must intensify our investment in all regional projects – road, electricity, water management, inter-regional trade – and we believe that many in the region are hiding behind the excuse – real or perceived – of external threat. if we work on eliminating that threat we eliminate the rationale for abuse. we are pleased that the external action service and the eu special representative for the horn of africa have already engaged through the african union and with the intergovernmental authority on development in order to develop such a regional policy. ethiopia and eritrea need to come to a resolution of their differences. if both rise to the challenges and responsibility of finding common ground, they can make a major contribution to regional stability. eritrea must find ways to demonstrate that it wishes to engage constructively with the region and eliminate the stifling restraints on its own population. i listened very carefully to the statement made by olle schmidt on the unfortunate imprisonment of mr dawit isaak, and i will convey that information to high representative ashton. we will monitor closely the kenyan elections with a message that the key actors should not squander the country’s potential but pay careful attention to their responsibility to their country, their neighbours and the international community. the two sudans, we hope, will continue in their recent efforts to improve their relations. relations between the two sudans remain very delicate, but the summit between presidents kiir and al-bashir this month reaffirmed the commitment of both to respect and implement the addis ababa agreement of last september and work to resolve the outstanding issue of abyei and other issues. the report by mr tannock welcomes and underscores the need for an approach based on tackling security and stability concerns, enforcing the rule of law and ensuring due process, whilst addressing the underlying causes. indeed, we need to act on both. development will only occur if there is trust within and among the countries of the region, and we must act regionally and combine our economic, diplomatic, security and humanitarian activities in such a way that the interests of our taxpayers are effectively served and the regions’ populations feel the beneficial impact on their daily lives.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.
the next item is the report by mrs catherine grèze, on behalf of the committee on development, on the development aspects of intellectual property rights for genetic resources: the impact on poverty reduction in developing countries (2012/2135 (ini)) (a7-0423/2012)
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i wish on behalf of the commission to thank the european parliament, and ms grèze in particular, for the report on the development aspects of intellectual property rights on genetic resources. i would also like to thank the committee on development as a whole, as well as the committee on internal trade and the committee on legal affairs for their views. the commission believes that the eu has been playing an important role in advancing regulatory action in this field, both domestically and internationally. this would, as you rightly state, enhance the effectiveness, inter alia, of the fight against poverty. the impressive development of modern bioscience and the rapid growth of scientific research on the genetic base of life have created growing expectations vis-à-vis the commercial value of biodiversity. it is now recognised that research and innovation relating to biotechnology play a significant role for development and can also help in the fight against poverty. however, we have also seen increasing concerns from developing countries relating to the misappropriation of genetic resources and ways of ensuring that the benefits stemming from intellectual property rights based on genetic resources from biodiversity reach developing countries. the union cares about development, recognises the concerns of developing countries and works towards creating positive synergies between the convention on biological diversity and different international bodies and instruments, such as the wto agreement on trade-related aspects of intellectual property, wipo, fao and the international treaty on plant genetic resources for food and agriculture. the new treaty under the convention on biological diversity, the nagoya protocol on access to genetic resources and a fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from their utilisation, which the international community finally managed to negotiate in 2010, should ensure legal certainty for users of genetic resources and improve transparency on transactions involving such resources. this in turn should help the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity and those who depend on ecosystems in the source countries. the commission has already proposed legislation for the ratification and implementation of the nagoya protocol in the eu. it provides for legal certainty for european users and increases the possibilities for transactions on genetic resources. at the same time, the proposal for a regulation establishes a credible system to ensure that users are in compliance with the legal requirements of the countries that provide such resources, which – as your report highlights – is a concern for developing countries. in our view, however, the compliance system should avoid excessive implementation costs and other burdens, especially for public research laboratories and smes. the eu also plays a very active role in helping to finalise the mechanisms needed for the entry into force of the nagoya protocol. the commission supports the establishment of the nagoya protocol clearing-house mechanism and considers close cooperation with other regions and countries essential for its effective implementation and for reaching a shared understanding of all pending issues relating to its smooth application. in order to help the least developed countries in particular, the eu and a number of member states are supporting other parties in ratifying and implementing the protocol. one important example is the multi-donor abs capacity development initiative that supports stakeholders from acp countries. the commission regards this as an important initiative and therefore contributes significantly. in addition a number of other international legal tools need to be further developed. the union is heavily involved in discussions with the wto and wipo. finally, in bilateral relations with third countries, and in line with eu policy coherence for development, we work towards the adoption of a balanced solution for intellectual property rights in order to prevent negative impacts on agriculture, health and poverty eradication. in summary, the commission acknowledges the call by the european parliament for coherent global governance systems that offer prospects for development to poorer countries. we agree that an important path towards this objective is an intellectual property rights system that prevents biopiracy and promotes innovation, the transfer and dissemination of technology to mutual advantage, and prosperity for stakeholders, providers, holders and users of genetic resources and associated traditional knowledge. the commission looks forward to further cooperation with the parliament, including the committee on development, on this very important challenge.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon.

in writing. about 70% of the eu population now lives in urban centres. in practice, therefore, much of the economic activity, employment opportunities, education, training and innovation centres are based and created in them. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. in this own-initiative resolution, which i voted in favour of, the european parliament calls on the commission and the member states to show greater determination. the efforts to develop urban networks cannot, however, be confined to the economy. growth means green space, culture, volunteering, sports, and opportunity. the new programming of cohesion policy should collectively respond to these challenges. the opportunities for financing from the corresponding community fund must be balanced between them, creating conditions for the overall emergence of the centres and not just the cores, leading other neighbourhoods, as has happened in athens and other greek urban centres, to degradation, marginalisation and underdevelopment.
in writing. − more than two thirds of the european population live in urban areas. cities are the sources of problems, but are also places where solutions can be found. the urban fabric is a fertile breeding ground for science and technology, culture and innovation. on the other hand, problems such as unemployment, discrimination and poverty can also be concentrated and inflated in cities. similarly, it is where the effects of the economic crisis are most strongly felt and the challenges of reducing the impact of climate change, job creation, well-being and quality of life are played out. i voted in favour of this report because i believe that it will be the development of our cities that will determine the future of economic, social and territorial development.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on urban upgrading as a contribution to economic development, as local development at the urban level should be an integral part of cohesion policy, as it is the level closest to a very large proportion of citizens living in the european union. i agree with the rapporteur who proposes a new integrated approach to urban policies and the promotion of a holistic approach to the problems of the urban fabric. i also think that the proposal to strengthen the instrument of participatory planning with local and regional authorities, by creating the right conditions for the establishment of partnerships, is particularly important. in short, a coherent urban policy should be a priority as the majority of european union citizens live in cities and urban regions and all european union policies must take this reality into account. the effectiveness of cohesion policy, as one of the most important policies of the european union, is based on an effective urban policy, which makes a decisive contribution to development.
in writing. this own-initiative report contains important examples and good practices currently in place or under consideration for implementation in european regions with a view to growth and job creation. territorial cohesion has now been added to economic and social cohesion as an objective of the treaty (article 174) and therefore requires greater mobilisation of local bodies and authorities to ensure that policies are taken close to the citizen and with immediate and tangible effects. however, this also requires greater involvement of local actors, transparency of programmes and clarity of their objectives. particularly for countries such as greece, which in the past have had problems absorbing and utilising resources from the community funds, further development and better design of territorial development programmes, and of the structural funds in general, for the new financial period (2014-2020) can prove particularly beneficial. the report i voted for is moving in that direction.
in writing. i voted against the vaughan report because i believe that there are differences (e.g. on accessibility) between the eu regions which are constantly widening due to the difficult economic conditions for many member states and budgetary cuts. i agree with the rapporteur that the budget for cohesion policy after 2013 must be maintained at least at its current level in order to ensure that support continues to be provided to areas in need of economic and social regeneration.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report which demonstrates the importance of the role of territorial development in the context of cohesion policy. to this end, common rules and close cooperation are necessary for all five funds under the general regulation. the implementation of strategies at local level should also be the responsibility of local bodies and authorities, with the aim of ensuring that economic and social needs are met at the level closest to the citizen. furthermore, i agree with the rapporteur who argues that providing opportunities for member states, regional and local authorities to use more than one european funding programme to support projects will improve the efficiency of european funding, enhance simplification and provide potential beneficiaries with easier access to funding. the new instruments introduced to strengthen the local dimension of cohesion policy, to increase the strategic focus of territorial cooperation and to introduce a more coordinated approach at all levels of governance are therefore considered to be of major importance.
in writing. the solidarity fund, which has now completed its 10 years of existence, is perhaps the most characteristic political and economic instrument of practical cooperation and mutual assistance between the eu member states. having so far provided financial support to compensate for damage caused by 49 disasters, mainly floods and fires (it should be recalled that it has also been mobilised in greece for these reasons), its successful course requires its preservation. however, conceptual clarifications regarding the eligibility of the disasters where it occurs are necessary. in view of the new financial period 2014-2020, the fund regulation should therefore be updated. in any case, however, the european parliament makes it clear in this report, which i voted in favour of, that these changes should not reduce the effectiveness and the ability of the fund to intervene, but should instead make it even clearer that europe will stand by its citizens in the face of a difficult and emergency situation.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the regulation governing the solidarity fund needs to be adapted and some shortcomings that have become apparent in its 10 years of operation need to be addressed.
in writing. the solidarity fund is the main instrument available to the european union for implementing the principle of solidarity enshrined in the treaty, and its success is beyond doubt. since its creation in 2002, the fund has been mobilised 49 times, with 23 member states requesting its mobilisation and the amount allocated amounting to some eur 3 billion. however, some problems have been observed in the implementation of the fund, in particular with regard to definitions, scope, meeting the criteria for applications and timely delivery of funding. i therefore strongly support any positive measure aimed at improving the mobilisation and implementation of the solidarity fund, such as the minimisation of red tape. our aim should be a more flexible and simplified fund that is easily and quickly mobilised and allows the european union to respond better and more effectively to major disasters and to provide timely relief for citizens in need. the european union is not a political union, but a political union of the people.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it sees in a law of administrative procedure an opportunity to reinforce the legitimacy of the union and, at the same time, to give citizens and legal persons clearer rights and more legal certainty in their relations with the union’s administration.
in writing. the kke votes against the extraordinarily complex report, the new anti-working class measures included for the relocation of enterprises and the safeguarding of the interests of the monopolies. the report considers it an inalienable right of enterprises to make whatever restructuring they deem necessary for their profitability, such as redundancies, the generalized application of temporary flexible employment, welfare, intensification of the exploitation of the working class, protection and safeguarding of competitiveness, the profitability of monopolies, and the protection of the environment. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are not undermined by the excessive deficit of the member states. the commission has already proposed training programmes for redundancies, which will certainly benefit the companies. with unprecedented audacity for the unemployed, they propose measures of psychological support! it is now imperative that the working class and the popular strata strengthen the anti-monopoly - anti-capitalist struggle, to open the way for the overthrow of the power of capital, for radical changes in the level of power and the economy, which will be based on the satisfaction of the modern needs of the working people and not the monopolies.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the amendment of the directive. its adoption is necessary in order to bring the legislation into line with the treaty of lisbon by conferring on the commission the power to adopt delegated acts as well as implementing acts. this directive regulates the classification, packaging and labelling of preparations, i.e. mixtures or solutions consisting of two or more substances (chemical elements and their compounds) and remains in full conformity with the united nations system of harmonised commodities. the increased delegation of competences at transnational level ensures better and more efficient controls, while creating a safer environment for citizens with regard to the hazard and safety of the relevant preparations.

the rapporteur. - the the commission's proposal for a regulation on the protection of the environment is based on the principle of the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment. the proposal for a regulation now before the house is a further amendment or recast of the system introduced in 1993 and subsequently amended several times, most recently in 2006. the agricultural production of the aegean islands should be geared to promoting synergies between the local agricultural economy and tourism development, as well as the preservation of the natural and cultural wealth of the area. the dispersion of the islands is a barrier to supply and trade, dramatically increasing transportation costs. the existing transport networks are characterised by inadequacies and structural weaknesses, creating conditions of 'dual insularity', as the smaller islands are supplied via other main islands. the small size, the inaccessibility both externally and internally, the declining and ageing population, the lack of basic raw materials and public goods (water, electricity, fuel), as well as the geophysical and climatic conditions (rocky soil, fragmented agricultural holdings, and drought and strong winds respectively) make up the highly peculiar environment of the small aegean islands; this is why special measures are needed. addressing the persistent and structural difficulties of the aegean islands' agricultural sector requires a systemic approach, a thorough analysis of needs and a clear strategic orientation. the concept of agricultural activity, in its most modern sense, is inextricably linked to sustainable economic, social and ecological development. in addition to the particular difficulties which characterise the agricultural production of the small aegean islands, these islands are also the recipients of external (illegal) migratory pressures. the aid scheme for the smaller aegean islands cannot, in its present form, respond individually to the above challenges, nor unfortunately to the extension of its territorial scope, i.e. to include the islands of crete and euboea. we look forward with great interest to the next legislative initiative from the european commission to proceed with a substantial reform of the programme. in conclusion, i would like to thank the danish and cypriot presidencies of the council, the european commission and the shadow rapporteurs of the other political groups for the fruitful and constructive cooperation we have had.

do our legal systems in our member states work or not? if they work, then nobody, no member state, has to be afraid of ratifying the convention of the council of europe. yes, we need action. we need action, for instance, so that all the good work which has long been carried out in the daphne programme can continue. are we going to take a stand if some people want to cut funding to initiatives within the daphne programme? i myself have not reduced the money available to the programme and i have added money from the justice budget. we need to take action to train our justice practitioners so that when they are confronted with violence – and they will be confronted, as even if we speak up and even if we pass another resolution, there will be violence – they will to know how to deal with it. we need those who perpetrate this violence to understand that they cannot go unpunished and the victims to know that they will have a helping hand. so, yes, we do need action. to all those who said that we do not need action only inside the european union but also outside it: yes, i agree. that this is why my colleagues, baroness ashton, ms georgieva and mr piebalgs, who are responsible for approving the disbursement of funds for projects which help women, have joined forces to speak up. lady ashton is doing it regularly in her bilateral meetings, and ms georgieva and mr piebalgs are helping victims. mr piebalgs is building up a society in third countries where women can have their rights. yes, that is action. you also asked me about supplementary action. you asked me for a strategy and you asked me for a directive on violence with minimum standards. did you listen to what ms pietikäinen has just said? all the specific cases are crimes – it is a scandal that they happen. but we need to reach out to the victims in order to help them, because by helping the victims we can show that we do not leave anybody who has been a victim of crime alone, no woman is left helpless. i think that will be the answer given by this parliament, and we can work together on this both in this house and outside. another very important thing which each of you can do in your member state is to say to your national media that yes, there is a scandal here. but the main scandal is that the laws that we have all been deciding together are improperly implemented and that the legal professions are not properly trained to implement the laws in such a way that there are no more women saying: yes, i was ill-treated; yes, i was raped; yes, i was punished although i did nothing wrong, and nobody helped me. no, we have the laws in order to help them, so help me implement the laws in the member states.

on behalf of the efd group. mr president, the text of mrs rodust's proposal is very satisfactory and there are also reasonable amendments which i believe will be voted on tomorrow, so that we can arrive at a new fisheries policy for europe for the next ten years. according to available data, 75% of the union's fish stocks are overfished, and more than 60% of european waters are fished beyond their maximum sustainable yield. the overcapacity of the fleet, the lack of control, the lack of reliable scientific data and the failure of the member states to comply with scientific advice for a number of years are some of the problems we have to solve. the european union loses around €2 billion in potential revenue every year because it cannot manage its fisheries sustainably. the introduction of maximum sustainable yield, as adopted by the committee on fisheries, will, i believe, bring significant economic, environmental and social benefits. the proposal to ban waste is also generally considered to be a good one, since no one wants to waste resources, especially in the difficult economic circumstances we are living in. however, we must be careful about the socioeconomic impact of this measure. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament's budgetary powers are not undermined, and that the budgetary authority is able to take action in the event of a dispute between the commission and the council.
mr president, our european fishermen can no longer make ends meet. there is not enough fish in our seas, and tomorrow’s vote is our last chance to change this. i fully support the commissioner’s proposal on msy and the discard ban. however, madam commissioner, there was a key measure missing from your proposal. with your help, and a lot of work from colleagues, we managed to get parliament’s proposal through, namely the establishment of fish stock recovery areas. this will allow us to restore levels of fish and increase the income of fishermen throughout the european union by increasing both the quantity and the size of fish caught. these areas will be identified and co-managed by local fishermen. a unique sense of ownership of the sea and its resources and a real form of rationalisation will be established. i want to call upon all members of this parliament to give this measure some teeth and vote for amendment 253. this is the main novel idea that parliament is introducing in this regulation. give fish and fishermen a chance. vote for amendment 253.
in writing. the committee voted in favour of the report and against the compromise proposal with the council, which was tabled at the last minute (although it had already been agreed) by the rapporteur of the european parliament, because it worsened the existing regulation and even the commission's proposal. not only does it keep the funds for aid at the level of the previous decade, but it increases the amount of aid for the supply of products, to the detriment of aid for agricultural production. it thus favours monopoly trading enterprises and harms the poor and middle-class farmers and small businessmen and artisans of the islands. at the same time, it adds registration requirements, certificates and other bureaucratic procedures, which effectively exclude small farmers and professionals, in favour of large companies, and gives more powers to the european commission to determine, in the name of compliance with eu rules, the direction of aid. the political representatives of the monopolies in the european parliament have once again played the well-known game of deceiving the workers. once again it is being shown that the cause of the dramatic increase in all the people's problems - and the problems of the workers, the islanders - is the anti-people policy of the eu, over time, of governments, of capital and of the current three-party government, which crushes the people to keep the profits of the monopolies intact.
in writing. small and mediumsized enterprises in europe account for 98% of all enterprises, while in greece before the crisis this figure was 99%. the economic crisis, however, has revealed that smes are a very vulnerable group which is particularly exposed to situations where the flow of small loans and credits is interrupted. in the same context, this category of enterprises is dramatically affected by delays in payments to them. in fact, due to stricter capital requirements, creditors often require additional personal guarantees in addition to standard bank guarantees, making the financial environment for smes stifling. the present report, which i voted in favour of, identifies these threats and, in addition to welcoming the 'action plan to improve access to finance for smes' adopted by the commission, makes further proposals, such as the creation of additional alternative financing mechanisms and the activation of household and business savings, for example by introducing tax incentives.
the question is "blue card". as you know very well, and as we all know here, mr langen, greece has taken full responsibility for the great economic difficulties it is facing and for the last three years has been implementing a very tough programme - unfortunately mainly - of fiscal adjustment and not so much of structural reforms. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the european union's financial instruments for the period 1994 to 1999 is a good one, but it is not enough to say that the commission is not prepared to take account of the fact that the member states are not yet in a position to take account of the need to ensure that the financial instruments are used effectively.
madam president, commissioners, we all agree in theory that we need a growth-friendly fiscal adjustment, but we disagree on how to put it into practice... i think there is a question of inconsistency in words and deeds. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have countries implementing fiscal adjustment programmes and suffering from deep recession and huge unemployment. we know their numbers. i don't know if we know what these numbers mean to people. but, commissioner rehn, these countries are excluded from the analysis of macroeconomic imbalances in the context of the six-month period. under the country's name is just one phrase: "implement the program". i would therefore like to stress that this excludes both dialogue and efficiency checks and exacerbates the problem of democratic legitimacy and accountability. i would therefore urge you to include the countries in the programme in the analysis process of the european semester.
mr president, the european semester is at the heart of a new governance of coordination and cohesion. in recent years we have all become aware of the interdependence of our economies and of our shared responsibility for the common effort towards stability and growth. the european semester brings the representatives of european citizens in national parliaments closer to the european vision, common policies, the need for the proper and complementary management of european and national resources. it is also a way of bringing us, the european institutions, closer to national realities. the recommendations issued for each country must therefore take account of the uneven economic and social conditions currently prevailing in europe, which have been widened by the crisis. our experience has shown today that the great challenge is how to achieve a better recipe in the balance of policies for fiscal consolidation and growth with jobs. the social consequences of this continuous search for a development balance without results are serious. it is bad for the credibility of european citizens towards the union; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. economic indicators must be complemented by social indicators in order to be able to propose sustainable policies. the european semester has the challenge of proving effective. we cannot make the mistakes of multipliers and bad stabilizers that have been applied in countries with a fiscal adjustment programme like greece. the european union cannot allow such mistakes. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject.

ladies and gentlemen, the comments are the words of those who make them.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report approving the commission proposal. the european employment strategy provides more coherent and precise guidance to member states in defining national reform programmes. its main points are to increase labour market participation and reduce structural unemployment, develop a skilled workforce that responds to labour market needs, promote quality jobs and lifelong learning, improve the performance of education and training systems at all levels and increase participation in higher education, and promote social inclusion and combat poverty. however, in order to achieve the above objectives, member states, in particular those that are in financial straits and cannot mobilise resources for employment, such as greece, should make full use of the tools and financial instruments offered in particular through the european social fund. greece is already mobilising around eur 750 million to tackle youth unemployment; however, there are still a number of resources that should be mobilised before the completion of the current multiannual financial framework ending in 2013.
in writing. i voted against the parvanova report despite the improvements it has made in some areas to the unacceptable proposal for a directive from the european commission. i find the following points particularly problematic: i am sorry to say that the house today rejected the double legal basis (articles 114 and 168) by retaining only article 114 and thereby undermining the responsibility of the member states for their national health insurance schemes, which is their responsibility under article 168. the european commission shall impose penalties in the event of failure to meet the deadlines for reimbursement of expenditure. this is contrary to the principle of subsidiarity and poses a major problem for insurance funds. it undermines the ability of the pricing and reimbursement authorities to fulfil their mission to protect public health and adds unnecessary administrative and financial burdens to public health systems that will jeopardise their viability and patients' access to safe medicines. 3. member states shall be prohibited, in the context of pricing and reimbursement decisions, from reassessing the elements on which the marketing authorisation was based, including the efficacy, quality or safety of the medicinal product. unfortunately, the amendments tabled by my group to delete these provisions have been rejected.
in writing. the general objective of this proposal is to modernise the legislative framework in order to clarify the procedural obligations of the member states to prevent obstacles to trade in medicinal products. it is recalled that the pricing and reimbursement of medicinal products is the sole responsibility of the member states; however, minimum provisions should be laid down to ensure legal certainty and transparency for the competent national authorities in relation to the manufacture of medicinal products and the placing on the market of generic medicinal products. the european parliament, with this report, which i voted in favour of, supports the approach proposed by the european commission to provide for double and differentiated deadlines for the invoicing and reimbursement of medicines, while at the same time providing an incentive for the implementation of health technology assessment which will support the definition of safe and effective health policies that put patients at the centre and aim to achieve optimum price.
in writing. the committee voted against the commission's proposal and report on the revision of the common fisheries policy (cfp) because it aims to restructure the fishing industry more intensively by further destroying small-scale fisheries - which has already largely been done with the current cfp - and by strengthening large fishing fleets and aquaculture monopolies. the ban on waste is conducive to monopolistic concentration. the landing obligation and the increase in selectivity are intended to reduce traditional fishing in order to encourage the development of aquaculture enterprises. the eu and its member states' governments can use the findings on discard levels, overfishing, poor compliance, overexploitation of fish stocks, impacts on small coastal communities, etc., as a pretext to accelerate the eradication of coastal and inland fisheries. the development of sustainable fisheries to meet the needs of the people requires the socialization of monopolies. popular power and economy, which, through centralised planning of fisheries production and the leverage of the fisheries production cooperative, will ensure the people's nutritional needs in fishery products, in accordance with the fishing possibilities available, with respect for the requirements of fish stocks, ecosystems and the environment.
ladies and gentlemen, we will now take the explanations of vote for three more reports: the report by mr gallagher on the conservation of fishery resources; the resolution on the elimination and prevention of all forms of violence against women and girls; and the report by ms liotard on the european innovation partnership on active and healthy ageing. all the other explanations of vote will be taken tomorrow. i shall start with the gallagher report.
in writing. the aim of this report is to protect public health and the environment through measures to reduce the noise level of motor vehicles. i voted in favour of the report which, in order to achieve this, proposes and introduces a new test method for measuring the noise level of these vehicles and noise limit values for different categories of light, medium and heavy vehicles. the european parliament also adds two important provisions which were not included in the commission's original proposal. first, the introduction of a vehicle noise labelling system to promote low noise vehicles and to enable consumers to make an informed choice before buying a vehicle. secondly, the introduction of a road classification system which would enable member states and national and local authorities to monitor and assess more effectively the quality of their road surfaces with a view to reducing the impact on the general noise levels.
in writing. the european parliament's committee on budgets has voted against the two reports on increasing the eu's contribution to projects financed by the reactive funds of the eu's migration policy for eu member states which are in a 'memorandum', such as greece. these are the funds that implement all the reactionary political decisions to strengthen the repressive mechanisms, the eu programmes and the governments of capital over time and the three-party nd-pasok-dimar government, such as the evros bridge, the concentration camps for migrants, the guarding of the borders by frontex forces, the mass deportations of migrants to their countries of origin, even when their lives are in danger. the aim of the increase in eu co-financing is to ensure that the repression against migrants continues unabated. as the explanatory memorandum to both legislative proposals states in detail: ... renovation of detention centres in poor condition and improvement of technical equipment necessary for effective border surveillance. ... member states facing significant economic challenges can carry out migration management tasks. the eu wants controlled immigration, immigration - hostage to supply capital with cheap labour.
in writing. this decision supports the major efforts made by greece in recent times to combat illegal immigration. and it is important that, in cooperation with the rapporteurs of the other political groups, the european commission and the council, we have moved ahead and completed the process very quickly so that the country can immediately benefit from the new provisions. by increasing the community funding rates, greece will be able to support projects that until now have required greater national involvement and which, due to the economic crisis, have been impossible to finance on its own, such as the renovation of detention centres and the improvement of technical equipment necessary for effective border surveillance. it is encouraging that our partners are increasingly recognising the need to show solidarity with member states such as ours, which often bear a disproportionate burden on behalf of the eu as a whole in dealing with illegal immigration flows. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

mr president, in order to look at the kurdish problem, we must look at the human rights situation in turkey as a whole, because it is an integral part of it. after all these years it is clear that this conflict between the kurdish people and the turkish government must be brought to an end. the fact that mr erdoğan has taken the initiative of direct talks means that it has become clear that the solution to this problem can only be found through political dialogue and not through violence. the situation in turkey is not encouraging in this respect. and that's because many kurdish prisoners are still being held in prisons for long periods of time and without trial. the continuing arrests we see every day are not helping to create a common ground. on the other hand, the murders of three pkk activists in paris may reinvigorate an already fragile climate. the point is that the talks must move forward and the solutions proposed must take account of the common interest as well as the dignity and future of the kurdish people. the basic rights of the kurds must be respected. turkey is in the process of profound change, at a great crossroads. it is up to the individual to decide whether he or she wants to become a modern democratic society with respect for the fundamental rights and freedoms of its citizens. it is up to it to resolve this longstanding issue which is of great importance not only to itself but also to the stability and peace of the wider region.
mr president, i hope that the turkish government's recent opening up on the kurdish question and the ending of a conflict which has cost thousands of lives since 1984 is sincere. given that the kurds constitute 15 to 20% of turkey's population of 75 million, it is clear that the resolution of the kurdish issue is of major geopolitical importance for security and stability in the region. this is reinforced when we consider that the kurdish question involves the territories of four countries, all of which are currently in turmoil. we condemn the recent murder of three kurdish activists in paris. this heinous crime raises concerns about the prospects for negotiations. we are also concerned about the large-scale arrests of kurdish elected representatives and prominent dissidents. but we also condemn any act of indiscriminate violence. we believe in a peaceful solution to the problem and support a political solution to the kurdish question through democratic dialogue, constitutional reform and non-discriminatory justice.
the commission's proposals are not only a good way of dealing with the problem of the kurdish people, but also a way of dealing with the problem of the kurdish people. of course the problem is old, it hasn't come up now. it has caused many thousands of casualties on the kurdish side and on the turkish side. it is therefore right and just to seek a solution through discussion and consultation. it is true that turkey has made some progress in recent years, but this progress does not yet correspond to european standards of democracy. the turkish government's attempt to open a dialogue with the kurdish side must be supported by our own side. the council and the european commission and parliament must support this. the references made each year in the parliament's report by mrs oomen-ruijten are clear. and this is the direction. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force. i think we all support this effort, we are all deeply saddened by what happened in paris, by the murder of the three women activists, which was probably motivated by other things, namely to undermine the dialogue that has begun between the kurdish side and the turkish state. it is a fact, mr president, that in turkey certain things are not recognized. it has been said before that turkey does not recognise a member state of the union. this does not mean that we do not want close relations with turkey and progress on its accession. but it must also - and this is the message we must send - continue the dialogue. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training.
madam president, cop after cop the negotiations do progress, but it is clear that the pace of progress is slower than we need if we are to be realistically optimistic about a global agreement for 2015. we need to speed up, both in the eu and internationally. in the eu we have made a mistake and are only just now starting to realise it. we have spoken to our people too much about the actions we are taking and too little about what other nations are doing. we have created a fake conviction that only the eu is taking action and all the others are sticking to their old policies. this is now becoming the excuse for inaction within the eu. we urgently need to end this misinformation and to tell the truth, which is that every year new legislation is being adopted and implemented in almost every country in the world regarding climate change. i urge the commissioner to take the lead in communicating this message to our people. it will seriously help us speed up the pace of negotiations and give the global climate agreement a chance.

president of the european investment bank. − mr president, it is a real pleasure and honour for me to be here to report to you about 2011 and to have a look into the future of the bank and the activities which, in particular together with the european commission, we are proposing for 2013 and beyond. we feel – and we are – accountable to european citizens for the activities of the bank. that means that we are accountable to the european parliament as well. therefore this is a very good opportunity for me to thank you for the support we have received from the european parliament over all these years, in particular in the very difficult time which we had to go through and which we are still going through. the crisis is not over yet. we are seeing the first good signs that we might be seeing light at the end of the tunnel, but we still need to perform in a very difficult situation. that means that the european investment bank with its instruments can, together with the commission and the european institutions, make a difference. first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr mauro, for a very thorough and well balanced report on the activities of the european investment bank in 2011. i also thank the shadow rapporteurs and the many other members of this parliament who have been involved in this report and have cooperated so well with the eib. in terms of the internal activities of the eib and outside it, we consider the european parliament our main ally and i thank you for that. 2011 is a long time ago, but nevertheless it is important to go back to that year to understand the fundamental changes that happened at the eib in 2012. those changes prepare us for the years to come – at least the next three years – as outlined in our corporate operational plan which was decided by the board in december 2012. in 2011 the eib was scaling down its business after the huge extraordinary effort that the bank had made following the lehman brothers crisis. consequently the bank had to reduce its lending volumes after those critical years. this was a decision made by the board of governors and the finance ministers of the european union. but, as we all know, the lehman brothers crisis was followed by a deep sovereign debt crisis. so, at the request and with the support of our shareholders, we charted a new course for the bank. we positioned it for another three-year phase of increased funding and lending operations. to put this into figures, we plan to lend around eur 60 billion in additional money over the next three years, bringing the annual lending for 2013-2015 to a volume of about eur 65 billion to eur 70 billion per year. enhancing our activities was in accordance with the macroeconomic convictions of our shareholders, as well as of the management of the bank. this was made possible because of the decision of our shareholders to strengthen our capital base by increasing the paid-in capital by eur 10 billion. this has created the conditions that will enable us to carry out this task over the next three years. i thank the european parliament for its support and i also thank the members of the national parliaments who supported this, because we needed unanimity in 27 member states and, in many member states, we needed the support of the national parliaments in budgetary decisions. this was given on the last day of 2012 – so it was not a given thing that it would work in 2012. i am very grateful for your support. so where do we stand now? for me there is no doubt that the eib, the eu bank, is making an extremely important contribution to overcoming the crisis. but allow me to make a personal comment. although we are observing the first signs showing that the worst might be over, for me this crisis will not be over until there are signs that the situation is also easing on the labour markets in those countries that have been hit hardest by this crisis. however, what we are seeing is that some stability has been achieved and also most definitely a number of successes. we are operating together with other european institutions. we are not the european central bank, which is taking care of the liquidity of our monetary system. we are not the esm, which is so important for dealing with the debt situation. but the european institutions are giving a coherent answer to the crisis, together with the commission and the eurogroup. this is important as it also shows the outside world that europe is acting in a coherent way. so the key message here is clear. we are proud to be a part of this coordinated european approach. we have a special role. we are not a bailout bank, we are not the bank of the programme countries or the bank of the eurozone, we are the bank of the 27 member states of the european union. this is a key nuance. economic conditions in the investment environment vary from country to country and we need to provide the right instruments to ensure the maximum impact of eib activity in lending, leveraging eu budgetary funds with our loans or technical advice. besides that, our business is designed on four pillars, more or less equally distributed between strategic infrastructure projects, investments in research and development, climate action activities and the support of small and medium-sized enterprises. to ensure all this, our performance must be differentiated, creative on the tools and, at the same time, conservative on the substance and on the risks involved. we need to convince the private capital markets to provide us with the necessary means which we are then able to lend out to our final recipients. so the eib pays even more attention than before to the quality of the projects that we finance. the quality of our portfolio and excellent hedging of the risks it is to take are vital to our own and to our investors’ survival. your report mentions on a number of occasions the second of the three pillars of activity – lending, or combining loans with eu budgetary funds. in recent years, the commission and the eib have developed joint risk-sharing instruments to improve access to loan finance. this enables us to be selective in countries where other investors have already left the markets or are heavily reducing their business. let me therefore take the opportunity to thank the european commission for its excellent cooperation on these joint measures. i look forward to further enhancing this cooperation. it allows me also to make a comment on the capacity of structural funds. the eib is ready to step up its lending activities and its lending efforts to co-finance these funds and allow the member states the maximum possible intake of this cash to kick-start their economies, but we would ideally like to see some changes to the current rules to allow for the redeployment of structural and cohesion funds to ensure their best possible utilisation. more flexibility would give us more opportunities. just a final word on the external dimension of our activities. in the present situation it is important that we concentrate on the work within the european union to restart growth, employment and innovation. let us not forget innovation. but we are also the financial arm of the european union outside. we are ready to perform this in this field. we are limited so far to 10% of the business volume, but we know that also for the external dimension of the european union, in the neighbourhood policy and in the accession countries, but also far beyond these territories, we are aware of the support of the european parliament in these activities and i thank you for that as well.

role the eib has to play in supporting the europe 2020 objectives. in this context, we particularly welcome the capital increase recently approved by the eib governors, which will generate eur 60 billion of additional lending over the next three years. while the commission recognises the importance of the aaa rating of the bank and the need to properly manage portfolio risks, it is critically important that the eib deploys fresh funds to high-value-added projects across the 27 member states, including vulnerable and programme countries for which funds are not available from other sources, on reasonable terms. for example, eib activity in 2012 has been decisive in providing much-needed support in greece and portugal. the bank provided substantial amounts and support for school facilities throughout greece, as well as for development of environmentally friendly energy production. small- and mid-capitalisation businesses play a key role in the greek and portuguese economies, and eib funding has helped them gain access to credit, despite the difficult market conditions. we also welcome parliament’s support for deepening the cooperation between the eib and the commission in developing innovative financial instruments such as loan-grant blending and risk-sharing mechanisms. these instruments are very efficient tools for combining and leveraging the limited resources from the eu budget, the eib and other european institutions to promote growth and employment in europe. therefore the commission has proposed to considerably expand their use under cosme, horizon 2020 and the connecting europe facility. moreover, to further improve the impact of the eu intervention, we should continue exploring synergies between eib financing and structural funds. a clear example is the pilot phase of the project bonds initiative. the scheme is up and running and managed by the eib. the eu budget contribution of eur 230 million is expected to raise around eur 4.5 billion in project costs. the project bonds initiative has two objectives: to revive project bond markets, and to help the promoters of individual infrastructure projects to attract long-term private-sector debt financing. the project pipeline is good, with both ten-t and ten-e infrastructure projects, all of which could be allocated in 2013, following the finalisation of the tendering procedures. the positive feedback from market participants provides confidence about this new way of financing infrastructure projects in the eu and, more generally, on the use of innovative instruments in support of the europe 2020 strategy. we consider that a major challenge for the eib in the years to come will be to refine its business model in light of the new market circumstances, notably the deleveraging of the private banking sector, the limited investment budget available to member states, and the growing relevance of public investment banks. in this context, the eib should use its catalytic capacity to attract resources from the capital markets to support eu priority investments. at the same time, it should reinforce and streamline its cooperation with public investment banks in member states. we share the eib’s view that one-size-fits-all is not an option, in particular in the current circumstances. in order to generate growth, the eib has to adapt its lending programme to the needs and necessities of each member state while at the same time, focusing on eu added value. consequently, the eib investment strategies have to be closely aligned with the priorities identified in the annual growth survey and the european semester of economic governance. as regards the eib’s external activity, a substantial level of eib involvement in the pre-accession and neighbourhood countries is essential and in line with the eu’s political and strategic priorities. the eib’s investment activity in these regions is still of fundamental importance to facilitate the accession process and the eu support to its neighbourhood, while other, more distant, regions will also benefit from support from other international financial institutions. as you may be aware, the commission is currently preparing the proposal for the future mandate and the next financial framework, which should be submitted to parliament and the council in the coming months.

in writing. the european union is experiencing perhaps the world's greatest demographic crisis alongside the economic crisis. the number of people aged 65 and over is expected to almost double from 85 million in 2008 to 151 million in 2060. in parallel, the eu population is expected to fall from 495 million to 416 million by 2060, while the elderly dependency ratio will increase from 25% to 62%, with implications for social security funds and welfare budgets. the report, which i voted in favour of, seeks to create the conditions necessary to ensure that the individual is able to mature and age in an active and healthy way. this means that member states should prepare for the new conditions today, for example by creating living conditions in the city or in the countryside that are suitable for the health and well-being of the ageing population, by creating age-friendly communities, services and products and, obviously, by changing the patterns of urbanisation in europe. it is encouraging that this debate at european level is starting relatively early; however, action should be taken quickly as the conversion of entire spatial data takes time, especially in the midst of an economic downturn.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the establishment of guidelines for the administrative budgets of the eu institutions at a time when the protracted crisis continues to cast a shadow on this debate. in the past years we have made considerable savings in the administrative expenditure of the european parliament and the other institutions. we are continuing with the same determination this year, with particular emphasis on strengthening cooperation between the institutions and sharing best practices that can reduce operating costs without having to sacrifice the quality of the services provided. as regards the budget of parliament, i would argue that the structural and organisational reforms which have led to considerable savings should be continued without jeopardising parliament's legislative activity. finally, we must not forget the peculiarities of 2014, as it is the year in which the european elections will take place. the main message we should send is that the european parliament is making the greatest contribution to fiscal rationalisation efforts at european level, trying to maintain a high level of legislative work and fulfilling its role as the only directly elected body of the eu, which defends and promotes the interests of eu citizens.
in writing. vat fraud is one of the most frequent economic crimes in the eu as a whole. for almost half a year now, the eu has been trying to coordinate member states' efforts even more quickly by establishing a rapid reaction mechanism (rrm) which will enable member states to react more quickly and effectively to vat fraud. the report, which i voted in favour of, provides that this mechanism allows member states to take emergency measures in cases of mass fraud and that they can, for example, apply for a period of one month a 'reverse charge mechanism' under which the recipient becomes liable for payment of vat instead of the supplier of goods or the provider of services. this would significantly improve the chances of effectively tackling complex fraud schemes, limiting financial losses for the public. greece can therefore also benefit from this mechanism, at a time when the fight against all forms of financial fraud is a prerequisite for improving the economic climate in greece and restoring positive growth rates.
in writing. although the removal of distortions in the functioning of the single market is a long-term endeavour in the eu, the economic crisis requires intensified action in this direction. in this context, the commission adopted a communication on single market governance entitled 'improving single market governance' to implement and enforce single market rules more quickly and effectively. the european parliament, in this report, which i voted in favour of, welcomes this initiative; the committee also welcomed the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european parliament and the council. the european parliament therefore sees the period immediately ahead as crucial and the commission should strengthen the single market legislative framework by giving preference to regulations over directives as legislative tools, as they prove to be more effective in this field.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint resolution, which is largely the result of confusion arising from the different legal systems in the member states and from the sometimes inadequate knowledge of european law on the part of national judges, who are, and should be, european judges who are co-shaping the eu legal framework. this resolution emphasises the importance of financial instruments to improve the training of judges and their access to the law, and the functioning of a network of interconnected judicial coordinators to facilitate judges to consult in their daily work with their counterparts in other member states on issues such as the interpretation of applicable european law (through a secure digital environment). for these reasons, the european parliament calls on the commission to promote and finance the national judicial coordinators of european law and the emerging interconnection of national networks of judicial coordinators.

member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has already made a number of proposals. i would like to ask you to give me a reply to a few of your remarks. firstly, energy efficiency: you can come to us with open doors. i think it was right that we have a 20% energy efficiency target in europe. it's just a matter of timing. when is the right time to propose a new target? i'm open to that, but i'm inclined to say this: we are currently monitoring the implementation of the adopted directive by the member states. we look forward to seeing the progress made on energy savings in 2012/2013. we will then, before the end of our commission mandate, make a forecast of whether the speed of implementation in the member states and our measures, which we have decided to make binding, will be sufficient to achieve the 20% target for 2020, or whether further measures will have to be discussed and decided upon with the new parliament in 2015. i think that the time has come for a new energy efficiency target, based on analyses of developments at european level, the effectiveness of your directive, our joint directive, the national action plans. that is, i would be more inclined - also in terms of when we will succeed in the council - to see 2015 as the right time to propose a binding, pragmatic and ambitious 2030 energy efficiency target. secondly, shale gas: we all agree that gas will play an increasingly important role in the global energy mix and also in the european energy mix. there are three reasons for this: gas is available in far greater quantities than oil. secondly, gas is much more environmentally friendly than coal, for example, both for industrial processing and for the heat market and the production of electricity. and the third reason is that gas is very flexible and therefore the ideal partner for renewable energy. this means that without wind, without sun, gas is available faster for heat and electricity production. we have a european gas market of about 540 billion cubic metres of annual consumption at present. our forecasts assume that it will tend to increase to 600 billion m3 of consumption per year. we still produce a third of our gas in-house, mainly from the netherlands and the uk. but all the projections for conventional gas production in europe say that these reserves will run out by 2030/2035. the question is: do we want to import 100% of our gas? russia, norway, algeria, nigeria, qatar, the southern corridor? so we want to be 100% dependent on imports? knowing that there are also significant environmental risks in siberia, in the arctic, in nigeria, in algeria? knowing full well that the working conditions for workers in siberia are not necessarily very friendly? the main thing is that the gas arrives clean in lubmin near greifswald, then we are not concerned about the environmental hazards in siberia and the arctic and the working conditions. i tend to look at it in its entirety.
