procedures, to conclude this negotiation at first reading and to start recording agricultural emissions by the member states as early as 2013. we have to get the global climate agreement by 2015, and we will get it.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but they also highlight very serious political issues, as they push for greater transparency and efficiency in our climate policy. we need more transparency and more efficiency in climate policy. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the biggest of these was the cdm scandal, which the commissioner herself fought to end, but parliament also fought to end it, and i helped, i can tell you, myself. and this joint response has led to savings of hundreds of millions or even billions of euros for the european electricity consumer, after previously paying corresponding amounts to factories outside europe and to intermediaries who profited by exploiting the complexity and opacity of the system, selling us rights up to 70 times more than they cost. we need to increase efficiency so that we do not repeat the phenomenon of photovoltaic panels, which we bought at great expense to create a strong european initiative, and we saw china finally take over our market by directly subsidising its own producers, to extract the subsidies we have been giving all these years. i hope that these mistakes will be avoided in the lulucf framework, where i believe satisfactory accounting standards are ensured from the outset. but we will see it in action and we will always be vigilant in defending transparency and effectiveness in our climate policy, because the issue is too big and too critical to be left to mere words and good intentions.
member of the commission. − mr president, i will keep it short as it has also been so positive to hear how broad a backing these two proposals have. there was one comment about our need to improve knowledge. i, of course, agree with this one hundred per cent, and that it is why it makes so much sense that we are now starting to account for lulucf. this is very important because when you account for it then you also need to – and you know that the next step will be that you have to – reduce the impact from lulucf and that is why this is a total thing. this is a first step and it is very important because the next step, of course, is that people will want to know more. so these things are interlinked and i think that what we just heard here speaks very much in favour of supporting what we actually have in front of us. my other concluding remark is just to say how sometimes, when we sit at these international conferences, you wonder what comes out of them. this is a very tangible and specific example. some of us worked on this back in durban fifteen months ago; then it came into the text of an international agreement, and now we in the eu are ready to make it eu law. it is being implemented. from here it can make a difference in real life and not only that – because we have an international agreement around these matters, others will have to implement it as well. this is just one example of why some of these really slow negotiations sometimes lead to things which actually make a difference in real life. i think this is a very important first step that we are taking on lulucf. once again, thank you to everyone who has been working on this, and i hope that the voice from parliament tomorrow will be as strong as the one we have heard here tonight.
rapporteur. − mr president, just some facts for colleagues, so that they know exactly what we are voting on tomorrow. lulucf activities produce savings for the eu member states. if we move to step two, which is to measure the lulucf emissions against our targets, it will have a financially positive impact on member states, so it is in our interest that we all measure in the same way, and this is what we are doing with this legislation. there is no burden on farmers. this has been clear in the text. this is a macro-statistical exercise. as the commission has said, after 2020 we will have monetary accounting for agricultural activities. so what happens between 2013 and 2020? we have both preparation and monetary accounting. for every year from 2013 until 2020, the member states will have to announce their estimates for their agricultural emissions. at the end of this period, they will have to announce the final estimates for each year of this period from 2013 to 2020, so by the end of 2020 we will know the complete accounting picture for the whole period from 2013 to 2020. this is a serious step ahead from what we decided all together in durban. we should all be proud that with both these reports the eu has shown the world its commitment to climate action.

the next item is the report by mr patrice tirolien, on behalf of the committee on development, on the financing of eu cooperation for the african, caribbean and pacific states and the overseas countries and territories for the period 2014-2020 (com (2011) 0837 - 2012/2222 (ini)) (a7-0049/2013)
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, first of all let me, on behalf of commissioner piebalgs, congratulate mr tirolien for his report on the preparation of the 11th european development fund. this report encompasses a broad range of areas regarding our cooperation with acp countries and gives interesting food for thought. the european council on 8 february confirmed the commission proposal to keep the 11th edf outside the eu budget. instead, the 2014-2020 period will be used to redefine the principles and architecture of the eu-acp partnership, ahead of potential post-cotonou budgetisation. in the meantime, member states’ contributions under the 11th edf will be further aligned with those used for the eu budget. in the long term, we in the commission believe that edf budgetisation would bring more political accountability to eu cooperation with acp countries, more efficiency to eu financial instruments and procedures, and more coherence to eu development cooperation as a whole. we also agree with the rapporteur that budgetisation should on no account be used to reduce expenditure on development aid. the european council also set the amount of the 11th edf at eur 30.5 billion, which remains stable compared to the 10th edf amount, extrapolated over seven years. the share between the broad financial envelopes of the 11th edf for acps, octs and support expenditure will still have to be decided by the member states. we also take note, with interest, of your request to maintain the same proportion for intra-acp and regional programmes as in the 10th edf, to keep flexibility and responsiveness with unallocated reserves and to maintain the figure proposed by the commission for the envelope dedicated to octs, and we are grateful for your backing for the commission proposal to use 5% of edf funding for support expenditure. this is fully justified in the light of the increasing complexity and number of implementing tasks attributed to the commission. regarding differentiation, we share the views expressed in the report that this is a vital tool for targeting eu resources where they are needed most to address poverty reduction. a differentiated approach depending on contexts and needs is also a fundamental principle of the cotonou agreement. therefore, we will further focus on the most vulnerable and the poorest countries, which may lead to a reduced allocation compared to the 10th edf for the most advanced countries. rest assured that all acp countries will continue to benefit from the edf, if they so wish, with national, regional and intra-acp funds. the ongoing programming exercise for the 11th edf will be steered by the agenda for change, but it also falls under the cotonou agreement. as such, it remains a joint undertaking based on a partnership with each acp government. regarding concerns expressed in the report on sectoral concentration, let us remember that the choice of programming will stem from dialogue in which we will seek to match partner countries’ wishes with our drive to get the highest impact from our cooperation. this is indeed why we cannot earmark funds for specific sectors. for the first time in the edf – as with the dci – we propose basing our programming directly on the partner country’s national development strategy and synchronising our programming cycles. this takes us a giant step closer towards genuine alignment and ownership. so, mr president, what next for the 11th edf? well, after an agreement has been reached between member states on the 11th edf internal agreement mid-2013, the text will have to be ratified by all of them. this delay until the entry into force of the 11th edf should not impede the programming process, as long as transitional measures are taken to ensure the availability of funds in the meantime. the commission will continue to apply the highest possible level of transparency towards the european parliament regarding the preparation of the 11th edf, with due respect for the inter-institutional process.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, tuesday 12 march, at 11.30 a.m.
the next item is the report by mr patrice tirolien, on behalf of the committee on development, on the association of the overseas countries and territories with the european union (com (2012) 0362 - c7-0285/2012 - 2012/0195 (cns)) (a7-0052/2013)
member of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank mr tirolien once again for this report and for welcoming the commission’s proposal. in preparing this, the commission has listened to the comments and suggestions from all sides and the final text demonstrates the commission’s unwavering commitment to assisting octs in their efforts to achieve sustainable economic and social development. the overseas association decision proposal takes into account not only the multitude of challenges with which the overseas territories and countries are confronted, but also their particular advantages and future opportunities. in that respect i would like to stress one substantial change that can be pointed out in comparison with the 2001 overseas association decision and which concerns the inclusion of a fully-fledged chapter on environment, climate change and disaster risk reduction. i will now go through some relevant points in mr tirolien’s report. with regard to the issue of competitiveness, i would like to emphasise that enhancing competitiveness is one of the main objectives of the relations between the eu and the octs and, as such, all activities undertaken in the context of the association decision shall contribute to this end. rather than limiting competitiveness to specific parts of the decision, we consider that to be a cross-cutting issue. the commission’s proposal contains provisions to allow for the eligibility of octs in all union programmes and instruments under certain conditions. we reaffirm our commitment to facilitate the active participation of octs in horizontal programmes. however the overseas association decision proposal cannot foreground the provisions of instrument-specific regulations as this would constitute a breach of the legal order. the commission shares the concern expressed by parliament regarding the need for simple and effective rules and procedures concerning programming and the implementation of financial assistance. these will be designed following consultation with the octs and the four member states concerned, in the commission regulation complementing the overseas association decision regarding financial assistance provided under the 11th european development fund and the programming guidelines. regarding trade and the rules of origin, the overseas association decision proposal constitutes a significant step towards improving the conditions for octs. while based on the general system of preferences model, the rules of origin proposed go further, by simplifying origin certification for small consignments, adapting cumulation of origin with other eu trade partners and – last but not least – by allowing octs to derogate. moreover, in terms of substance, the new rules simplify the product-specific rules, including for fisheries products. in terms of procedures, the new rules introduce, from 2017, origin certification by registered exporters, also known as self-certification. on regional cooperation, the commission concurs with the views expressed in the resolution. that is why this question is already accommodated in the text of the proposal. of course, regional cooperation can take different forms, exchange of information and best practices being one of them and support for regional or international projects, programmes and organisations another. to this end, the proposed overseas association decision includes a provision for the definition of rules and conditions to ensure better articulation between financial instruments and partners within the same geographic region. the european union wishes to support octs that seek to develop closer relations with their neighbours and to address joint challenges. in the past octs and the eu have cooperated on issues related to employment, education, health and social policy. the programmes implemented in greenland and new caledonia are cases in point. given the importance and success of this cooperation, these themes have been maintained in the legislative proposal. health issues, notably the spread of communicable diseases, are also of concern to octs, hence the inclusion of provisions relative to these issues in a general chapter covering social issues and which also includes employment and social policy. finally, concerning the upgraded role for parliament in the association envisaged in the report, i would like to reiterate that members of the european parliament will be closely associated with the annual oct-eu forum to improve communications and exchanges.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, tuesday 12 march, at 11.30 a.m.

madam president, today is a great day for europe, today is a great day for the single market. i believe that the two reports by mr louis grech and mrs róża gräfin von thun go beyond europe. they create conditions of confidence for the consumer whether he buys and consumes within the country where he resides or chooses to buy products from other member states of the european union. the important contribution of these two reports is that the consumer can, without fear of losing money if a product is defective, proceed to the market. it's a mechanism that ensures low-cost dispute resolution within reasonable limits. and it's more of a mechanism that one can turn to if one wants to. if this device produces results within 90 days, all is well. but if that mechanism fails, there is always the question of going to court. we'd hate to, but if necessary, the next step is collective precautions. let's keep that in mind.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, i have been very moved by many of the contributions to the debate. i have been shocked by some, but i prefer to forget them and to concentrate on the moving and correct analyses which have been put forward by the members of this parliament – and i can tell you that i am as concerned as you are about the rise of racism, xenophobia and hate crimes based on race, colour, religion, descent, ethnic origin or membership of lgbt groups. any of this intolerance is unacceptable: we cannot differentiate or rank some types of intolerance over others. they are all to be eliminated. we do have a european law, the framework decision on racism and xenophobia, which dates from 2008 – pre-lisbon times as you know – and which obliges member states to penalise intentional public incitement to violence against groups defined by their race, and we need to ensure that it is properly enforced. for that reason, we are reviewing it after five years, as is our normal practice, and at the end of this year i will come forward with a report on what has happened in the various member states on the basis of that legislation. only after 1 december 2014, when the pre-lisbon texts are ‘lisbonised’, can we institute infringement proceedings, so it is currently too soon to assess whether the instrument in question should be revised, because we first need to see how it is being applied. after 1 december 2014, if we see that there are holes in this text, we can propose a revision of it, which will, of course, depend on the legal analysis of whether we have the power to correlate criminal legislation against hate crime. we are aware of the european union agency for fundamental rights study on lgbt people: there were 90 000 respondents so it will be a comprehensive and solid study, and i imagine we will discuss it in this house in order to assess the extent of the problem generally and in the various member states. that brings me to the question of statistics, because before you can develop a policy you must know not only how a law has been applied, but also the number of people in danger, and the number of crimes being committed. the systematic collection of crime data is not as well developed as it should be. a recent report by the fundamental rights agency shows that only four member states collect comprehensive crime data, whereas 14 member states collect limited or no data. so you can see that we have a basic problem here because before you develop new legislation you need to have a basis for it. that is why, in the new laws which i have put on the table since 2009, data collection is explicitly provided for in the legislative text. let me give you the example of the victims’ rights directive: it places a strong emphasis on the systematic collection of statistical data because only that will allow us to see, after some time, whether the directive has been applied properly or whether we need to change certain elements of it. so all the new legislation which i have put on the table since lisbon has had the collection of data written in as a must; and we will have to resolve the ‘pre-lisbon’ questions when the statutory time comes to do so.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, firstly i would like to say that this has been a fascinating debate, with a broad range of views and perspectives from across the chamber. i would like to thank parliament for putting this very important item on today’s agenda. it is absolutely vital that we take every opportunity to continue to raise awareness of the scourge of racism and xenophobia. as i mentioned in my introductory remarks, we celebrate next week the europe-wide action against racism. we declare our solidarity with all minorities and our determination to protect their rights. that is extremely important. minimum standards enshrined in eu law are a way of fighting racism, anti-semitism and homophobia. once such laws are in place, we must ensure that they are respected, both in spirit as well as in practice. a number of members raised the issue of the anti-discrimination directive. i know that this is a very complex and challenging file for us in council. as you will be well aware, unanimity is required and negotiations have been ongoing since as far back as 2008. i can simply reiterate the irish presidency’s commitment to making progress on these negotiations. they are not easy, that is for sure. at the moment i am not in a position to anticipate the timing of an outcome, but we are very much committed and working very hard to make progress on that very important directive. it is important to say that, while the legal framework is extremely important, we must also have the courage to raise awareness and to guard against the attitudes that make discrimination possible. this is very much about getting into the minds and the hearts of citizens and changing attitudes and improving culture. a number of members have alluded to that. i think it is very tempting, at times such as these – times of great economic hardship – for some individuals and groups to look for scapegoats. therefore, at this difficult time, we have to be especially vigilant to defend our values and speak out against prejudice, against bigotry and against hatred. all crimes motivated by racism and xenophobia – all forms of discrimination – affect not just the victim, but all of society. that is something we have to continually remind ourselves. europe’s reputation, of course, depends on securing and defending the very basic values which our societies are based upon. i would just like to pick up on the point made by the vice-president of the commission, commissioner reding: that it is extremely important that the atrocities and hate crimes of the past are not forgotten; that we do not allow them to be forgotten; and that we do not lose those valuable lessons we have learned from those experiences in our quite recent history. i very much support the commissioner’s approach to trying to ensure that we remember and honour victims and that we continually try to remind people, particularly the younger generation, of those atrocities that have gone before them. a number of members, including mr kelly, also mentioned the value of reaching out to ngos, to civil societies and particularly to sporting organisations. i think that is a very valuable lesson that we have to learn; where the very important role models for young people show leadership and show conviction when defending our basic values and the things that we hold dear. a number of members, including mr claeys and mr vanhecke, made the argument that the subject of racism is often misused to constrain legitimate discussion. that certainly cannot be allowed to happen. but this is to confuse the subject. facts are not hidden. however, labelling, discriminating against, or indeed inciting hatred against people on grounds of race, colour, religious creed, sexual orientation, or other proscribed grounds, is not acceptable. that is the message that has to go very clearly from this chamber and from all of the european institutions as we work to promote and defend our basic values. i would like to conclude by thanking members for the very warm and positive reaction to the irish presidency’s initiative at the justice and home affairs council. you can rest assured that we will take that work forward over the months ahead. thank you very much once again for this very stimulating debate.

in writing. the debate on sustainable, secure and affordable energy has only recently been formally reflected at european level, in particular in the treaty of lisbon. however, despite the ambitious energy policy formulated by the commission, the eu's internal energy market is proving fragmented and performing rather poorly. barriers to cross-border trade and the development of energy infrastructure and a lack of an institutional framework are perhaps the most important barriers to the energy market. for greece, however, its successful and unhindered implementation is of particular importance, especially at the present time when the country is negotiating both the operation of the natural gas networks and following the evidence of the presence of energy deposits in the greek undersea area. this report on the regulation, which i voted in favour of, facilitates and speeds up the hitherto extremely long procedures for the authorization of energy infrastructure in the member states (on average 12 years), which discourage investors, but also lays the foundations for the mobilization of significant investment funds in the energy sector and consequently the creation of jobs, provisions from which greece can benefit considerably.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on transeuropean energy infrastructure, as i believe that the proposals it contains enhance the security and quality of energy supply, and that the considerable interdependence between member states makes a european approach to the problem necessary.
in writing. i voted against the report by ms auken, as i believe that the commission should review its approach to the infringement report concerning vienna airport and address the shortcomings identified by the ombudsman.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because accounting for land use, land-use change and forestry (lulucf) is a key element in climate policy with the potential, among other things, to contribute to mitigation, adaptation, conservation of biodiversity and sustainable use of natural resources.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr eickhout's report because i believe it makes a positive contribution to environmental protection through proposals to improve the measurement and identification of anthropogenic emissions contributing to the greenhouse effect which are not controlled by the montreal protocol.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the commission's draft proposal for a european platform for electronic dispute resolution. the increasing volume of sales made electronically today and the further increase expected in the coming years make it necessary to establish a framework for the out-of-court settlement of certain disputes arising from the cross-border sale of goods or the provision of services electronically. the platform, which the eu intends to launch, will be open and its services free of charge and available in all official eu languages. the commission will also be preparing a report on the implementation of the new rules on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment in the context of the implementation of the new rules. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the consumer is informed of the information it receives from the consumer.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because i believe that the proposals it contains contribute to a more effective resolution of consumer disputes. the extension of the scope to both cross-border and domestic transactions and the proposal to ensure that consumers are provided with substantial assistance through the electronic platform are considered particularly positive.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report. the draft report takes a three-pronged approach. firstly, the currently patchy coverage of alternative dispute resolution (adr) is remedied by providing a robust framework for the development of consumer adr entities which will ensure full coverage. secondly, the fact that many consumers and traders are presently unaware of adr procedures is addressed by requiring traders to provide adequate information on adr on their websites and in their documents. finally, the harmonisation of quality criteria will mean that, wherever consumers buy from across the union, they will have access to a high quality, impartial, independent, fair and transparent adr procedure.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report as it contributes to the development and protection of the overseas countries and territories (oct), which are considered to be of strategic importance for the european union. the octs offer many natural advantages and opportunities for action in relation to the new challenges facing europe, such as globalisation of trade, new environmental challenges, climate change, etc.

in writing. i voted against the two new regulations (two-pack) because they tighten even further the right of member states to draw up and implement their budgets according to their own needs. the two new regulations complemented each other to tighten the neoliberal package of economic governance, which in turn tightened the failed stability pact, and to ensure that european states and citizens were locked into the one-way street of continued austerity. the strengthening of supervision of member states receiving financial assistance, preventive supervision, balanced budgets and zero deficits will provide a further lever for the implementation of tougher neoliberal policies. the budgetary and investment programmes are fully aligned with the doctrine of austerity and exclude any alternative policy with a growth and redistribution role that would promote full employment. the wrong response to the crisis and the wrong neoliberal, anti-social and anti-democratic policies that are inextricably linked to it are once again being reinforced.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, as i believe that the proposed proposal for a regulation will help to strengthen small and medium-sized enterprises, as the aim is to create a european venture capital investment sector for small and medium-sized enterprises (smes).
in writing. the aim of this draft regulation, which i voted in favour of, is to lay down rules on the permitted investments and investment instruments to be carried out by the eltifs. their important social function is being strengthened by the eu, while an increasingly clear legal framework is being established for their operation and the community funding they can receive. for example, the regulation provides that each qualifying portfolio company must focus on achieving measurable and positive social impact and use its profits to achieve its primary objective in a responsible and transparent manner. in exceptional cases, where a qualifying holding company wishes to distribute profits to its shareholders and owners, it should be ensured that the distribution of profits does not undermine the primary social objective. however, as the aim is social work and not profit, the regulation makes it very difficult and under serious conditions for an sme to distribute profits, a point on which the european parliament has placed particular emphasis.

the committee on budgets has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - the madam president, more europe requires an adequate budget; we have instead a macroeconomic austerity budget. more europe means a thriving rural europe, a strong common agricultural policy; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. european agricultural income is shrinking, while production costs and the instability of agricultural markets are increasing. in the cap we are calling for adequate income support for farmers, effective market and risk management measures, strengthening the bargaining position of producers vis-à-vis the other links in the supply chain, and structural rural development measures centred on agriculture. at the same time, we urge the commission to subject imported products to standards equivalent to those of products produced within the union, as emphasized in my report on this subject, which was voted for by the parliament as a whole. the multifunctional european agricultural sector has a clear added value to the european economy and a major role in addressing the socioeconomic and environmental challenges facing the union. it provides public goods of major importance to our societies. a strong cap is therefore more necessary than ever.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the common organization of the market in cereals, which is to be adopted by the council on 1 january, is a step in the right direction. protecting the environment, biodiversity and natural resources from climate change is not a luxury; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. but it will only be achieved when its importance is understood by all. the role of counselling services is therefore crucial. it is also crucial that the farmer understands the importance of adapting to the demands of the consumer. how can the threat of opening up markets be turned into an opportunity? the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. let us take the first step towards a more targeted policy, closer to convergence for real rural development.

mr president, i would like to thank the rapporteur, nadja hirsch, for making a bold report, which goes beyond the usual. and in this parliament, at this difficult time, at this difficult period that the european union is going through, we need bold reports like that of nadja hirsch. i want us to reflect, especially those of us who come from small countries, like myself from greece, and to remember the time when we were immigrants too. greek immigrants went to various member countries of the european union, were able to get ahead and work and then returned to their homeland and added value there too. the memory in this case has its own value in a difficult time, a time when we must all, and the european parliament in particular, make sure that it is fair. when immigrants come from third countries and the host country is hospitable, is generous, is not miserable, that helps europe, it helps the residents of the host country, it helps to have human, social and economic capital within countries that need it very much; i would like to say that the commission has not been able to take the initiative in this matter, and that it is not the commission's intention to take the initiative. when they work legally, when they contribute to social security systems, they are literally blood donors to the economic condition of a country. that is why i, following the line of the nadja hirsch report, propose that we look at legal immigration not as a problem but as an opportunity for us all to become better. in writing. the present proposal for a regulation, which i voted in favour of, aims to establish clearer and more transparent rules for the collection and publication of macroeconomic data in the eu. the current economic crisis, the great public debate on the reliability of statistics and the need for accountability and control of the use of community resources require a comprehensive review of the european system of national and regional accounts. among other things, the main objective of the regulation is to provide eu citizens with statistics of the highest quality, based on the appropriate methodology, while being as complete as possible in order to ensure maximum transparency, particularly in the public sector.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the establishment of guidelines for the 2014 eu budget, an investment and solidarity budget, whose priorities should be economic recovery and growth, competitiveness, strengthening employment and combating youth unemployment. however, 2014 is a year in which two very important challenges will have to be addressed. the commission notes that the commission did not provide any information on the number of sampled union producers in the sample. as no agreement has yet been reached, the ceilings for 2014 have not been set. this makes it more difficult to define the general guidelines for the budget for the year in question. the second challenge concerns the level of payments. it is crucial to have an adequate level of payments in 2014, given that since 2010 the payment shortfalls have been steadily increasing and at the end of 2013 exceeded eur 16 billion, effectively putting the community budget in deficit. to address this, parliament calls on the commission to present a corrective budget for 2013 as soon as possible and expresses its determination not to accept an inadequate level of payments, both for 2014 and for the mff 2014-2020.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution because it proposes measures applicable to the spirit drinks sector that safeguard the reputation which spirit drinks have achieved in the european union and on the world market, by continuing to take into account the traditional practices used in the production of spirit drinks.
in writing. − i voted in favour. one of the major issues young farmers face when deciding to start out is the expense and administrative hurdles of taking over farming activities from their elders. these different elements, and the difficult generational transition, are among the reasons why the average age of farmers in the eu is above 50 years old. facilitating generational transition through public-private partnerships should be integrated into the indicative list of measures and operations of particular relevance to thematic sub-programmes, in order to take this element into consideration when developing rural development tools at national level.
in writing. energy must be one of the most basic public goods for the citizens of the member states. the javella report does not follow this logic, as it is entirely in line with the neoliberal orientations of eu energy planning. it gives limited emphasis to the social dimension of energy policy, does not protect the rights of the citizen-consumer and does not promote practices to reduce inequality in access to energy. further privatisation and full liberalisation of the energy market are its political priorities. the report argues that competition rules are the key tool for achieving the completion of the single energy market, despite the fact that they give the strongest advantage to the union's economies. the commission's proposals also underestimate the importance of renewable energy sources, energy efficiency and the reduction of the greenhouse effect. the main negative of the report is the reinstatement of the increased importance of nuclear energy in energy planning. the report, in its reference to exclusive economic zones, essentially bypasses international law, is vague and does not consistently emphasize the right of member states to declare eezs and conduct surveys. i have voted against the javella report for all these reasons.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs javella's report, which i consider to be balanced and complete. i fully support the proposal to delimit the exclusive economic zones of the eu member states and the third countries concerned, in accordance with the united nations convention on the law of the sea. i also welcome the reference to the importance of the deposits in the south-eastern mediterranean, which can play a role in regional stability and peace in the region.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report, as did my colleagues in the new democracy group, on the energy roadmap 2050, an own-initiative report without legislative force, but that does not mean that there are no points of concern, which were reflected in the group's individual negative votes. paragraphs 9 and 103: the replacement of national energy objectives by global european or regional objectives constitutes a limitation of the possibilities or the multiplicity of national energy policy. paragraph 76: the omission of reference, other than to unclos, to international contractual law in general and to national law, makes the regulation problematic and dangerous. paragraph 77: the eu's prevention of international frictions must be based on the international convention on the law of the sea alone. the reference to article 49 of the nabucco project, in which greece does not participate, even indicatively, limits the national energy network policy represented by other networks. the text downplays, if not ignores (paragraphs 4, 18, 63, 64, 83), the extremely cautious attitude of the great majority of european citizens and member states towards nuclear energy and calls on the commission, in paragraph 63, to campaign to change public opinion in favour of nuclear energy.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution because it stresses that the commission and the member states should encourage non-eu countries with nuclear power plants, in particular neighbouring countries, to apply the stress test procedure and to share their results. equally, it notes the importance of strengthening international nuclear safety and security standards and implementing them properly, and it encourages the eu to continue cooperating in this regard at international level, in particular in the context of the iaea.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this joint motion for a resolution because i strongly believe that it is important that the eu and its member states take action to fight racism and xenophobia by preventing them through education, promoting a culture of respect and tolerance, and ensuring that hate crimes are reported by victims, investigated by law enforcement authorities and punished by the judicial system.
in writing. i voted in favour of the westlund report because it recognises the health effects of endocrine disruptors, the pathologies they cause and the need for immediate action in the field of chemicals policy. according to a eurobarometer survey in the 27 eu countries, 83% of consumers are concerned that the food and drink they consume contains chemicals, while between 74% and 90% are concerned about chemicals in cosmetics, clothing, furniture and, especially, toys and childcare products. the existing eu legislation is based on a chemical-by-chemical approach. but today we know that endocrine disruptors act even at very low doses and that we should take into account their combined effects. this is why further action is needed in the area of chemicals policy, research and evaluation methods to address the combined effects of chemicals. the european commission should review its strategy on endocrine disruptors based on the precautionary principle. with the primary objective of ensuring the safety of consumers, particularly vulnerable groups, it should follow the example of denmark, which banned diphenol a in paediatric food and cosmetics packaging.
in writing. − the increased incidence of hormone-related disorders and illnesses in humans needs to be taken very seriously indeed. the endocrine system regulates much of what happens in the body, including reproduction, metabolism, growth, salt and water balance and cardiac function. there is a disturbing trend particularly with regard to human reproductive capacity, where endocrine disruptors are thought to be a contributory factor. i voted in favour of this report because endocrine disruptors influence the human environment, and if not addressed quickly can create irreversible damage to humans and the environment.
in writing. the commission's own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, deals with an issue which has also been of great concern to our country. although not binding, this report makes some valuable suggestions, particularly with regard to the development of asbestos removal and safe deposit schemes. it also stresses that directive 1999/77/ec states that 'no exposure threshold has yet been established for gold-silver asbestos below which it does not present a carcinogenic hazard' and therefore calls for the relevant european framework in this area to be strengthened. the same applies to the dumping of asbestos waste in landfills, which does not seem to be the safest way of eliminating the release of asbestos fibres into the environment, a problem for which the ep proposes the use of asbestos inactivation systems.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report. it has been generally accepted by courts that there is no known threshold exposure to asbestos below which there is no risk. the aim should be job creation and the provision of workplace conditions which promote the health and well-being of individuals.
mr president, backloading means higher electricity bills throughout europe, even in the countries most affected by the crisis and suffering from appalling unemployment: higher bills at the expense of the living standards of people in desperation, and at the expense of the competitiveness of economies in crisis. why is backloading happening when our environmental goals are being met? the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries, but that the commission has not provided any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries. the backloading is done to collect extra revenue from the back door for the states, and it is done mainly because its promoters refuse to admit that the economic objectives of our climate policy have failed miserably because of the american 'revolution' in shale gas and the 'defeat' of photovoltaics by china. it's because they refuse to accept reality.

mr president, the economic crisis, unemployment, insecurity and the violent loss of income have a significant impact on the health of citizens. people with severe survival problems are twice as likely to die prematurely or to become more sick because of problems that act cumulatively. unemployment leads to increased mortality, mental disorders, heart disease, increased addiction and substance dependence, and increased smoking. there is also a worsening of health problems associated with poor diet. large segments of society are driven to social exclusion, poverty and high morbidity, particularly those belonging to vulnerable groups. we cannot ignore the increase in suicides either. it is, however, doubly unhealthy to impose, in the context of horizontal restrictive policies, measures which also affect public health systems, increase inequalities in social protection conditions for health and worsen health and safety conditions. greece has had serious problems which are now being further aggravated by the policy of horizontal cuts.

in writing. enisa (european agency for network and information security) was established in march 2004. for greece, the continuation of its operations is important as the organisation is based in heraklion, crete. however, this report has raised the question of splitting the seat (mainly for reasons of reducing operational costs) between heraklion and brussels, a proposal which we are opposed to. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the safety of the networks is not compromised by the fact that the network is not protected by a computer. the over-concentration of bodies and institutions in brussels undermines the importance of decentralisation, which the eu itself is advocating, at a time when europe and european solidarity are particularly sensitive. the european union also has a number of decentralised agencies in various european cities. in any event, the argument of the proper use of community resources is refuted by the fact that recently eur 7 million was spent on the agency's building needs in heraklion and is now proposed to remain largely empty. i have voted against the whole report on this basis.
in writing. i would like to express my satisfaction with the renewal of enisa's mandate, but at the same time my regret that its mandate was renewed for only seven years, with a strict assessment of the seat after five years. such a short period is not sufficient to provide the agency with the predictability it needs to carry out the very important operational and institutional responsibilities it has now assumed, as it takes a long time for the investments and measures to enhance its functionality to bear fruit. this limited time also creates insecurity among staff, which will undoubtedly have a negative impact on their performance and the effectiveness of the agency's own actions. i could not ignore the fact that the establishment of an office in athens would involve a considerable increase in the agency's operating costs, since its operational staff, and particularly the technical staff, who constitute the vast majority of its employees, would be transferred there. the creation of an additional financial and administrative burden at a time when all member states are struggling to save raises serious doubts about the advisability of such a decision. i have therefore decided to vote against the report.
in writing. i welcome the recognition of the importance of enisa in ensuring cybersecurity in the eu, the renewal of the agency's mandate, and the confirmation of its seat in greece. the report, however, attempts to solve a virtual problem - the distance of the agency from the eu centres - by adopting a real problem: the split of the agency between athens and heraklion. it is a bad compromise, an impractical and very costly solution which does not meet the needs of the eu. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would point out that the new building of enisa in heraklion, which cost 7 million euros, is now virtually unused and will remain largely empty. i would point out that enisa remains the only eu agency that does not have a permanent mandate. an agency with a temporary mandate cannot operate in a strategic way. for these reasons, i voted against the proposal for the renewal of the mandate of the agency as it was put to the vote.
in writing. the european globalisation adjustment fund is a recent european instrument designed to support citizens who lose their jobs as a result of the economic crisis and to re-train them so that they can re-enter the labour market in a short time. i supported this report and others which help to achieve the fund's objective. it is true that italy and spain in particular have made extensive use of the fund's resources, while greece has made use of its provisions only once (for aldi staff), and therefore, given the crisis, there is scope for even greater mobilisation.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because the european globalisation adjustment fund has been created in order to provide additional assistance to workers suffering from the consequences of major structural changes in world trade patterns. it includes, amongst others, personalised services to be co-funded such as occupational guidance, job-search assistance, entrepreneurship promotion, vocational training and skills upgrade, guidance for the over-50s, job-search allowance, hiring benefit, contribution to commuting expenses and contribution to the expenses for a change of residence.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for italy to support the 1517 workers made redundant by the company antonio merloni spa, which is active in the manufacture of household appliances. the ongoing economic and financial crisis in italy further highlights the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as a tool of the european union's social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. the support provided is also a practical expression of the european union's solidarity. the italian government is making efforts to rationalize the budget and to cut expenditure drastically. the strengthening of the european globalisation adjustment fund will be able to make a decisive contribution to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the country's fiscal consolidation efforts.

in writing. i voted against the report recommending that the discharge be deferred to the european council and the council for the financial year 2011. for another year, the european parliament faced the council's intransigent attitude and its refusal to provide any information or answers to the written questions of the members of the committee on budgetary control. it goes without saying that it is an inalienable right of the european parliament, as the granting authority, to have access to information and data which will help it to take an informed decision on whether or not to grant the exemption. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the control of the budgetary execution of all the institutions of the european union is a necessary step in the process of implementing the budget, and the same should of course apply to the european council and the council.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report granting discharge to the european court of auditors for 2011. in general, there were no substantial problems in the implementation of the court's budget, which is required to speed up the recruitment procedures for filling vacant posts in the court of auditors, and it is useful to assess the level of corruption in the use of community funds on the basis of olaf investigations. the court is also called upon to improve the content and presentation of its special reports, which provide valuable information on the financial management, efficiency and effectiveness of european policies and are an important tool in our efforts as meps to improve the work and policy produced by the union. parliament supports and appreciates the work of the european court of auditors and encourages it to focus more on performance audits than on compliance audits.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report granting the european ombudsman discharge for the financial year 2011. there were generally no substantial problems with the implementation of the ombudsman's budget. as an indication, the ombudsman's annual management plan for 2011 includes the key performance indicators to measure the performance of the service in achieving the internal auditor's objectives. the recommendations for improving internal management and control procedures have also been implemented and the comments made in the ombudsman's annual report on its activities for 2011 are considered to be highly satisfactory.
in writing. i voted in favour of the discharge report on the implementation of the budget of the eu decentralised agencies for the financial year 2011, as i believe these agencies play an important role in achieving the objectives of european policies, generating significant benefits for citizens. moreover, although they represent less than 1% of the eu budget, their presence in the member states contributes to strengthening the european identity by demonstrating their european added value. it should also be underlined that 2011 marks a positive step in general in the horizontal approach to governance issues and is expected to contribute to their strengthening. in particular, progress has been made in implementing their budget, improving their audit systems and accounting management, and their performance is improving year by year. of course there is room for further improvement, particularly in the area of budgetary planning and financial management, and we should encourage the agencies in their efforts. it is our duty as meps to ensure that taxpayers' money is used correctly, transparently and efficiently, particularly in the current economic crisis, where the need for fiscal rationalisation is paramount at both national and european level.
in writing. the role of the body of european regulators for electronic communications (berec) is to promote the smooth application of eu law and thereby contribute to the better functioning of the single market. taking into account the positive report of the court of auditors, the body concerned may be granted discharge in respect of the implementation of the budget.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the granting of a discharge to the european centre for the development of vocational training for the year 2011, as i believe that this agency plays an important role in achieving the objectives and results of european policies, particularly those relating to lifelong learning and education. it therefore also provides significant benefits for citizens and contributes to strengthening european identity by demonstrating its european added value. it should also be noted that the agency has made progress in implementing its budget, has improved its internal control systems and has perfected an online tool which will enhance the speed and efficiency of its recruitment, increasing its performance. of course there is room for further improvement, particularly in the area of financial management, and we must help the agency to take the necessary steps in this direction, in particular by supporting the development of synergies and closer cooperation with bodies such as the european training foundation. it is our duty as meps to ensure that taxpayers' money is used correctly, transparently and efficiently, particularly in the period of economic crisis in which we are living, when the need for fiscal rationalisation is acute, both at national and european level.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on discharge in respect of the implementation of the budget of the european police college for the financial year 2011. the opinion of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, on which i was rapporteur, states that the annual accounts of the european police college (cepol) show that the budget of the institution is being managed and used in a satisfactory manner. in addition, there has been considerable progress compared with previous years both in the academy's financial planning and in its performance, particularly in streamlining its governance. in the same context, the opinion calls on the court of auditors to pay greater attention to the audit of the sound financial management of seppol, in particular the economy, efficiency and effectiveness with which seppol uses its appropriations to meet its obligations.
in writing. the european centre for disease prevention and control (ecdc) aims to strengthen europe's defence against infectious diseases. i voted against this report for discharge, since the committee responsible and the court of auditors have given a favourable opinion on its financial situation.
in writing. echa helps companies comply with legislation, promotes the safe use of chemicals by providing information on chemicals and chemical addresses of concern. i voted for the discharge as it was accepted by the committee responsible.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because the importance of control is essential if we want to have an effective common fisheries policy, especially in view of the new reform of the common fisheries policy (cfp) and the implementation of the objectives. therefore, and taking into account the court of auditors' report showing that its cash flows for the year ended are in line with the provisions of the financial regulation, the discharge is considered justified and in any event valid.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the discharge for the implementation of the budget of the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction. as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, i have found, on the basis of the court of auditors' findings, that the annual accounts of the centre present fairly, in all material respects, the financial position of the centre and the results of its operations and financial flows for the year 2011, and that the underlying transactions of the centre were legal and regular. however, the court of auditors found in its report that the emcdda still had to bear costs of eur 275 000 for unused office space. we therefore call on the centre to find adequate solutions in cooperation with the commission and the national authorities without delay and to keep the discharge authority informed of any developments in this matter. in the same context, we call on the centre to take all the necessary corrective measures announced in response to the individual critical comments of the court of auditors.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the service supports member states in developing and implementing eu legislation on maritime safety, pollution from ships and general safety at sea. it also has operational tasks in the field of oil pollution response, vessel monitoring and long-range systems for the identification and tracking of ships. its mission is considered important, especially in the light of the new common fisheries policy, which strengthens its role in the control and sustainable exploitation of fish stocks.
in writing. i cannot understand the deletion of a paragraph from the report, as approved in the committee on budgetary control, on the preparation of a cost-benefit study of the parallel operation of enisa in two cities by the competent institution, the european court of auditors, given that the fragmentation of the agency, creating a branch in athens and relocating the vast majority of the staff there under the pretext of improving its operational capacity, entails a significant increase in its operational costs and a burden on community resources. the impact of such action on the european budget can only be a reasonable request, given the efforts being made at national and european level to streamline the budget. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. for these reasons, i have decided to vote against the resolution accompanying the discharge decision.
in writing. i voted in favour of this interim report because it calls for greater clarity on some of the key concepts in the proposal and seeks to establish financial support procedures which are clear, fair and do not impose unnecessary consultations where they are not required. moreover, the role of the eu budget as the ultimate guarantor for the financial support provided under that regulation should be reflected in appropriate accountability procedures towards the european parliament.
in writing. the kke voted against the report because it supports the commission's reactionary proposal for the operation of a balanced budget (bbm) mechanism, i.e. a fund to provide liquidity to non-euro area member states when they are in a state of fiscal instability. the report calls for this "financial assistance" to be accompanied by the same anti-people measures and capitalist restructuring imposed on eurozone member states that have recourse to the corresponding eurozone supervised insolvency mechanisms: cuts in wages and pensions, abolition of collective agreements, labour rights, insurance, cuts and commercialization of the public health-welfare and education sectors, divestment of public enterprises and mass layoffs of workers. the report's proposals for the provision of hot money through the emi or the european support mechanism for the recapitalisation of banks based in non-euro area member states are aimed at strengthening large banking monopoly groups, greater concentration and centralisation of financial capital, within the framework of the european banking union and the eu's pursuit of controlled capital relief. the working class and the popular strata will face the increasing aggression of ever more powerful monopoly corporations, whose oppressive domination of all sectors of the economy will steadily and more intensely worsen the position of the working people.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the facilitation, improvement and development of international rail transport services is important for strengthening the single market.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on discharge in respect of the implementation of the budget of eurojust for the financial year 2011. as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, and on the basis of the information provided by the court of auditors, i have found that the agency's budget is being managed and used in a satisfactory manner. the commission nevertheless expressed its disappointment at the high volume of eurojust transfers, which was again described in the court of auditors' report this year as 'excessive', as it was last year. however, the acceptance of the court of auditors' comment and the commitment of the agency to take measures to improve the financial forecasts and the implementation of the budget is considered positive. in the same context, it was requested to resolve the shortcomings in the absence of certain implementing rules in the staff regulations and in the recruitment procedures, which were included in the report on the subject, which was voted on today by the european parliament's plenary.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report in order to complete the update of eu vat rules and ensure the uniform tax treatment of all types of vouchers in all member states. this will have a positive impact on businesses as it will eliminate double taxation and uncertainty about tax compliance. in addition, the gaps in legislation which facilitate tax avoidance by certain undertakings exploiting the disparities between member states will be addressed. the new rules are also in line with the broader strategy of the 'digital agenda for europe', in particular with regard to its objective of creating a digital single market.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on discharge in respect of the implementation of the budget of the european police office for the financial year 2011. the opinion of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, which i have drawn up, states that the annual accounts of the european police office (europol) give a very accurate picture. however, there are high levels of carry-overs of administrative expenditure appropriations, which europol attributes to the delayed move to its new premises and to efforts made to better comply with the principle of annuality, as well as to actions to improve its planning and budgeting process. in the same context, the opinion calls on the court of auditors to pay greater attention to the audit of europol's sound financial management, in particular the economy, efficiency and effectiveness with which europol uses its appropriations to meet its obligations.
in writing. the budgetary control of the european agency for the management of operational cooperation at the external borders of the member states of the european union (frontex) is of particular importance for greece given the important contribution of frontex to the effort to tackle illegal migration flows. as rapporteur for the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, and on the basis of the information provided by the court of auditors, i have noted the satisfactory implementation of the agency's budget and the successful implementation of the first operation of the rapid response team at the borders of our country. however, since frontex is an operational agency and the execution of its budgetary planning is heavily dependent on external factors, for example because it has to react to exceptional developments, it is necessary to allocate additional funds to the agency in a timely manner, which was included in the report on this subject, which i supported.
in writing. − the hague convention on the international recovery of child support and other forms of family maintenance of 23 november 2007 is an important instrument for citizens, as it provides for a mechanism under which child support obligations can be applied for and enforced against a debtor resident in a third country. i voted in favour of this report so that certain changes to the annexes can be approved in a quick manner, so that they can enter into force in regard to the european union as soon as possible.
