in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, jens geier, on his excellent report, which focuses on the main challenges of the structural transformation processes in the eu's former industrial regions and the role that cohesion policy can play in this context. i agree that there should be a comprehensive strategy for the revitalisation of former industrial regions, combining the resources of the structural funds with the new industrial policy of the eu, and that european public funding can make a decisive contribution to any future efforts to this end. integrated territorial investments and new mechanisms for the implementation of cohesion policy in the new 2014-2020 programming period are also expected to play a key role. the industrial sector has always been the eu's driving force for job creation, growth and innovation in its regions. in the context of the current crisis, it has become clear that the ambitions of the eu industrial policy and the eu 2020 strategy cannot be achieved by the various sectoral policies alone. the support from cohesion policy is therefore necessary and vital.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. this is undoubtedly a positive development, provided that we do not once again stop at words and declarations of good intentions but proceed to concrete and binding decisions, ensuring the greatest possible synergy and efficiency at national, european and international level. the outflow of large deposits from dubious sources of income, money laundering, tax havens, aggressive taxation - these are all huge problems that cannot be tackled at national level alone - coordination and synergy are needed at european and international level. in my country, ladies and gentlemen, which has been implementing a very strict budgetary adjustment programme for three years now - as in other countries affected by the crisis, with huge unemployment rates and a drastic reduction in incomes, especially of the taxpayers - tackling tax evasion is the key to successfully overcoming the crisis. in fact, commissioner, i would add that the adjustment programmes would have a much greater social acceptance if the troika had given the fight against tax evasion in the various countries the highest priority, with specific and binding targets, rather than horizontal cuts in wages and pensions. ladies and gentlemen, the spread of euroscepticism, anti-parliamentarism, anti-establishment figures and parties is also linked to the widening sense of frustration and injustice among large sections of citizens, including conscientious taxpayers. the sustainability of the european project depends on regaining the confidence of citizens; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i hope that you will take this message to the heads of state or government tomorrow.
question of the day: blue card. in your speech to commissioner šemeta on taxation, you mentioned the financial transaction tax and urged him not to implement it because it would encourage tax evasion and tax avoidance, as you said. the question i would like to ask you is this: as we are discussing today the enormous losses of one trillion dollars suffered by tax evasion and avoidance in the budgets of the member states of the european union, do you not think it is time to introduce measures of fiscal justice which will shift the burden from the productive economy and from employment to those sectors which are undertaxed and which are called upon to contribute equitably to the response to the crisis?
the european council will be dealing with two very important issues tomorrow, mr president. the first concerns energy and, in particular, the internal energy market, the existence of proposals for new energy sources and the very high energy prices which affect production in europe and therefore its competitiveness. the second issue concerns tax evasion and fraud. it is a fact, mr president, that the citizens of europe cannot bear to see people and companies who, by moving their capital from one country to another, a practice which is freely permitted, are committing tax evasion and tax avoidance at the same time, and this at a time of crisis in which, unfortunately, the multiannual financial framework has not yet been agreed. mr president, there must be some kind of coordination in europe in tax matters, but this does not mean that all countries can have exactly the same taxation, since they do not have exactly the same development. at the same time, this issue must be raised at the g20. there are many in europe who fear that this will lead to capital flight. we're going through a very difficult time. the message that the european commission must certainly send to all countries is that there is no tax evasion, no tax fraud, no tax relief. everyone has to pay their taxes. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. we must take into account the political climate; the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: we must not allow parliament to become a eurosceptic body, which would undermine the course of europe.
mr president, we are tabled, together with mrs podimatou of the socialist and democratic group, an amendment to recital i. the amendment concerns the deletion of the last paragraph concerning greece, according to which the commission estimated that, since the beginning of 2012, some eur 60 billion had been transferred from the country to swiss banks. it is not a statement by the commission, the content is not correct and the rapporteur has agreed to delete this paragraph, for which we thank her very much, since such inaccuracy cannot be contained in the report we are voting on today. i am very grateful to you and i ask you to accept this amendment.
in writing. the european university institute (eui) in florence is entering its 30th year of operation. the commission's contribution as a source of information on the history and heritage of the european union has undoubtedly been important throughout this period. the need to change the way it operates is due to the fact that it has been given even more responsibilities in the field of cultural content, which, however, do not affect its basic function as defined by regulation (eec, euratom) no 354/83. the present report, which i voted in favour of, focuses on the institute's better cooperation with the eu institutions in order to enable a way of exchanging files which respects personal data but is also based on new technologies and their digitisation. at the same time, increased funding is being provided in the next budget precisely to achieve the digitisation of the archives and thus to make them more easily accessible to eu citizens.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report given that, in most member states, the dates on which the elections would ordinarily have been held would have coincided with the whitsun bank holiday weekend. taking into account the council’s view that it would be preferable to bring them forward, i agree that the proposed electoral period should run between thursday 22 and sunday 25 may 2014.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it identifies that the european union’s industrial sector has always been a driving force in creating jobs, growth and promoting innovation in all regions of the european union, and it is crucial that this be promoted at a regional level.
in writing. i voted in favour of the excellent report by jens geier on regional strategies for industrial zones in the eu. it is an indisputable fact that the industrial sector has always been the driving force of the eu, helping to create jobs, growth and promote innovation in its regions. the financial crisis that the eu has been experiencing in recent years has demonstrated in the most striking way that the ambitions of the eu industrial policy and the eu 2020 strategy cannot be achieved by sectoral policies alone. the support from cohesion policy is therefore necessary and vital. in this context, there should be a comprehensive strategy for the revitalisation of former industrial regions, combining resources from the structural funds with the new eu industrial policy, as european public funding can make a decisive contribution to any future efforts to this end. integrated territorial investments and new mechanisms for the implementation of cohesion policy in the new 2014-2020 programming period are also expected to play a key role.
mr president, both i and the new democracy members of the european parliament have consistently and systematically supported the protection of women's rights, the fight against discrimination and the implementation of strategies, through various mechanisms and resources, to promote women in the labour market and in public life in our neighbouring countries and in the countries in the process of accession. we have voted against the cornelissen report because we believe that a european parliament resolution must respect the official terminology and, in particular, the official names of countries and must not cause confusion. the cornelissen report mentions the terms 'macedonian parliament', 'macedonian government', 'macedonian authorities' instead of the terms 'parliament, government, authorities of the former yugoslav republic of macedonia'. mr president, respect for the law and respect for the principles on which the agreements governing relations between two countries are based must be respected by these countries and, even more so, by the european parliament, in order to avoid problems being created by countries which ignore reality and history, such as the former yugoslav republic of macedonia.
in writing. gender equality is a community acquis and mainstreaming gender in the eu's external relations is a thematic priority of the european commission's strategy for equality between women and men 2010-2015. gender equality is a fundamental human right and a key objective of any modern democratic country. we support the accession process of the countries of south-east europe and consider that safeguarding the rights of the female population of these countries is a necessary condition for them to gain membership of the european union. i voted against the report on women's rights in the balkan countries in the process of accession because, in the part of the report referring to macedonia, the adjective macedonian is repeated in order to identify the government, the authorities and the parliament of that country. this use is contrary to the un resolution and the official texts of the european commission and seriously interferes with the transnational dialogue that has been taking place for years between a member state and a candidate country for eu membership on the name of fyrom, under the auspices of the united nations.
in writing. in recent years, the crisis has forced dozens of media outlets, mainly print and regional, to suspend their operations. the social cost is, of course, enormous because of the jobs lost. the commission has also been asked to provide information on the situation in the member states and to provide information on the situation in the community. the eu has the power to adopt legislative measures to guarantee, protect and promote freedom of expression and media pluralism. in order to ensure that regional and local media, both printed and non-printed, remain viable and able to cope with the current economic difficulties, their funding channels should be supported. this is foreseen and to some extent already met by programmes such as media but the support effort needs to be further strengthened. i voted in favour of this report because it takes account of this need and because it proposes that more resources be mobilised in this direction in the new multiannual financial framework.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because it can be said that freedom of expression in the public sphere has been shown to be formative of democracy and the rule of law itself, and coaxial to its existence and survival. free and independent media and free exchange of information have a decisive role in the democratic transformations taking place in non-democratic regimes, and the commission should be requested to undertake the close monitoring of media freedom and pluralism in accession countries and to pay sufficient attention to the role of free media in the promotion of democracy throughout the world.
in writing. − i voted in favour. the european agenda for adequate, safe and sustainable pensions for both young and old which is presented in the white paper is an excellent starting point for discussion, after which reforms can be implemented by means of ‘soft law’, and also by means of legislation where necessary. overall, while recognising and preserving the responsibilities of member states and social partners, we now need to build up systems which are adequate, secure and durable. not only that, but they must also contribute to greater labour-market mobility and freedom of movement, systems which provide solutions for today and tomorrow.
commissioner, when you announced a year ago, in june 2012, the plan for a banking union with its three pillars, the single supervisory mechanism, the european clearing house and a european deposit guarantee scheme, many of us in the european parliament welcomed your initiative with great enthusiasm, because it finally laid the foundations for the implementation of a key structural element that is missing from today's economic and monetary union, which is clearly lacking. we have also welcomed with equal enthusiasm the decisions of the summit last june, because it was one of the few occasions when the council sent a message of determination and coherence, halting the worsening crisis in spain and italy. but while we took a big step forward, defending the interests of europe, we then took steps backwards, because national priorities and national logic once again prevailed. the agreement on the single supervisory mechanism is very important, commissioner, but we must not forget that our aim was to have this mechanism already in place. we cannot operate as if we had a decade to finish what we started. i wonder if what happened in cyprus is not enough to make us realise that the emu cannot continue to be so weak in managing banking crises. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have not understood that even the correct principle of bailing out the banks, the principle of bail-in, which we voted for yesterday in the committee on economic and monetary affairs in the context of the hökmark report, must, if it is to be effective, be applied everywhere in the context of a functioning banking union and not piecemeal and flattening out, as was done in the case of cyprus, increasing the risks of destabilisation rather than reducing them?
mr president, it is a mockery of the peoples that the banking union will bring about regulation and control of the financial market, parasitism and speculation of the banks. the european banking union which is being established is a mechanism for greater concentration and concentration of banking capital with the aim of financing and strengthening monopoly groups and the controlled destruction of capital. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council and the european parliament, and the council will be consulted on the matter. the commission will be consulted on the matter, and the council will be consulted on the matter. we have experience of the dramatic consequences for the peoples of ireland, spain, greece and cyprus. the present compromise on banking union will also prove fatal before the ink of its signature has even dried, because capitalism is not regulated, because it has its own iron economic laws of profit - uneven growth. no banking union, no measure of so-called 'market regulation' can prevent capitalist crises of over-accumulation of capital. capitalism is rotting in its deepest contradictions and suffers, first and foremost, from its basic contradiction between labour and capital.
the banking union is the new european objective, mr president, it is a fundamental plan for stability, consolidation, confidence and security for investors and depositors. a project that we could not have imagined a few years ago is now at our doorstep. it requires the will and determination of the institutions and of all the member states and imposes equal obligations and rights. this will make our banking union safe and attractive. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the banking union testifies to that. i would like to congratulate the rapporteurs on their work and their commitment to the european parliament's values of efficiency, integration, transparency and democracy in the mechanisms that will operate within the framework of the banking union. the chaos and insecurity we faced in the cyprus crisis would not have been there, and it is enough to help us realise the importance of the union. the operation of the first pillar supervisory mechanism already makes clear the difficulty of the project, both politically and technically, in relation to the procedural aspects of the role of the european central bank and the supervisory authorities. the functioning of this system is of fundamental importance. it is a difficult task which we must succeed in in order to then move on to the next stages, such as the single resolution mechanism for failing banks and the common deposit guarantee scheme. i would like to congratulate commissioner barnier on the speed with which he has taken action in tabled ing the proposal and its implementation from 2015. the longer we delay, the more vulnerable we make the weak banks, the more likely it is that the burden on public finances we want to avoid will be greater, and the more insecure the financial system will become - a delay that is unprecedented today.
the commission is aware that the banking system in most member states of the union is suffering from a number of problems. many banks need to be recapitalised to avoid bankruptcy and the loss of european citizens' deposits. to a large extent, the banks' losses and their precarious claims are not due to the classic, pure banking products, but to the fact that the banks were exposed to toxic products that are creations of casino banking. but banks are not casinos. they are almost exclusively managing the people's savings and they must manage them prudently. the european depositor must above all feel secure. but i am afraid that many european banks need support now and will need it in the future, according to the new theory that the risks created by the well-paid 'golden boys' of the banks must be paid for by the depositors, who are not to blame for anything. i therefore call on you, commissioner, to work with the european central bank and the european banking authority to solve the problem and to introduce new rules.

mr president, in the previous debate on the implementation of the stockholm programme, the question of revising the regulation on access to documents was raised. we all agreed that we were behind on this and that we should move on. it is indeed a democratic obligation under the lisbon treaty, as has already been said, to facilitate public access to documents of the european institutions. it is also a matter of democratic accountability, and no one is denying that. it is equally clear that it cannot be done at the expense of the public interest, much less at the expense of public life and personal data. i agree with commissioner borg's position today. human rights cannot be put on the scales. we cannot accept the sacrifice of some of these rights for the benefit of others. in short, is the public interest in the matter fully compensated by the need to make all documents public? is the need for transparency offset by the exposure to the public of citizens' personal data, particularly if they are minors? obviously, it is not always easy to maintain this balance. the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs are well aware of this. the path to date has not been easy at all - there have been many disputes, there have been many frictions. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. however, following the progress of the negotiations, i note a relative lack of provision for entry in the register of sensitive documents and i would like, on the occasion of today's debate and the presence of the commissioner, to ask and receive an answer, if that is possible. why, commissioner, are some documents not even registered in the register, whether access is granted or not?
madam president, economic and monetary union, a policy which is profoundly anti-people and hostile to the interests of the working class and the popular strata, as indeed is the whole structure of the european union, is the spearhead for strengthening the european union's monopoly groups and their profitability. the so-called 'social pillar' of emu is nonexistent because it is incompatible with the nature and character of the european union. the right of workers to work has been transformed into the right to underemployment. collective agreements are being struck down, social security is being levelled, wages and pensions are wages and hunger pensions, the unemployed are 27 million, those living below the poverty line are 120 million, the poverty of the peoples in the member states of the european union is absolute. the plans and proposals for deepening emu have nothing whatsoever to do with the popular interests; the european union is not a political union, but a political union, and it is not a political union, but a political union, and it is not a political union, but a political union. the ideas that the european union would ensure the welfare of the people, that there could be an emu with a different, supposedly 'more social' architecture, that the european union could be transformed from an alliance of capital into a 'european union of the peoples' have been shown to be blatant lies - a deception of the peoples.

in writing. in a recent reply, the commission informed me that europe loses an amount of one trillion euros a year due to tax fraud and tax evasion. the problem of tax evasion concerns all member states but, because of the economic crisis, it is particularly urgent to tackle it in the member states with the greatest fiscal problems, such as greece. but in addition to the obvious reforms needed at national level, the dizzying and largely contradictory tax system applied by each of the 27 eu member states is hampering the effort to crack down on tax fraud. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a good example of the need to ensure that the safety of workers is not compromised by the use of asbestos. the proposals for increased coordination of tax policies are certainly moving in the right direction. but their implementation also requires greater determination and consensus in the member states, on which we hope to see significant progress at the next summit when the issue will be discussed by the heads of state. the recent proposal by the president of the european commission to exchange data and information between member states to combat fraud is also aimed at this.
in writing. − i voted in favour. an estimated eur 1 trillion in public money is lost due to tax fraud and tax avoidance every year in the eu. the size of this alarming tax gap represents a rough yearly cost of eur 2 000 for every european citizen. the average amount of tax lost in europe today exceeds the total amount that member states spend on healthcare, and it amounts to more than four times the amount spent on education in the eu. tax fraud and tax avoidance, therefore, represent a serious, multifaceted problem, requiring a coordinated approach at national, eu and international level. with member states holding competency over their fiscal systems, action at national level is important. therefore, all focus should be directed towards establishing a cohesive, concrete and common european tax strategy, embraced and implemented by all member states.
in writing. this initiative is part of a package of legislative measures to strengthen the rights of victims in the eu and in particular to support victims of crime. the report, which i voted in favour of, highlights a number of important new measures in this direction, such as simplifying the procedure for recognising protection orders, addressing the situation of victims of gender-based violence and the right of access to information and translation. the report is part of the overall effort to create an area of security, peace and justice in europe, which was intensified with the adoption of the stockholm programme for 2010-14, but which requires even greater commitment from the member states to fully implement it.
in writing. the recast directive on the harmonisation of the laws of the member states relating to the placing on the market of fireworks aims to bring nine older directives into line with the new legislative framework. this alignment will not only clarify the obligations of undertakings and provide the supervisory authorities of the member states with more effective means of controlling the market, but will also lead to a reduction in the supply of unsafe products and to a reduction in red tape and a general increase in consumer protection. for these reasons, i voted in favour of this report, which is also urgent in that it contains, among other things, important provisions on the manufacture of airbags and safety belts, provisions which, in order to continue the smooth functioning of the market, must be implemented by the member states before 4 july 2013.

mr president, we are discussing today the investor-state dispute mechanism, which is the first time the commission has used its right, under ceta, to decide on and agree investment protection agreements. it is the eu’s competence now – and why has this competence been transferred from the member states to the eu? because the member states believe, and citizens believe – in accordance with the lisbon treaty – that the commission and the european union can better guarantee labour rights and other sensitive issues that have proven to be better managed at eu level than in the member states. nevertheless, we now see the commission introducing the form of investor protection which is, according to the commission’s impact assessment, most likely to endanger democracy, labour rights and much of the legislation that this parliament will decide from now on. i will be very brief and ask the commissioner to explain the commitment the commission made in discussions with the environment committee to bring specific solutions to address ceta, the new trade agreement with canada. i would like to raise the issues of treaty-shopping, third-party finance and the protection of the principles of impartiality and transparency which exist in the european courts but not in the isds. how, specifically, are you going to protect and address these three issues?
the tragedy of the collapse of the eight-storey garment factory building in the ranna plaza quarters in bangladesh, which led to the death of over 1,000 workers, is a link in the chain of countless crimes of capitalism against the working class. similar crimes occur daily by the thousands in the capitalist countries, the european union, the usa, japan and elsewhere and are called provocative "work accidents". employer terrorism, work intensification, flexible forms of employment with 12 to 16 hours of work a day during peak production, in hazardous workplace infrastructure, without any controls, in order to maximize the profitability of the monopolies, are the main causes of tragic accidents at work. according to official figures from the international labour organization, one worker dies every three and a half minutes from an occupational accident or disease. the proposals in the joint resolution of the political groups in the european parliament, which calls on capitalists to exercise 'corporate social responsibility', i.e. to 'control' themselves, are aimed at perpetuating the capitalist barbarity of exploiting the working class and are a challenge to the relatives of the victims and the working class as a whole. only organized struggle within the workplaces can impose measures to protect the workers and can overthrow this rotten capitalist system to lead us to the abolition of the exploitation of man by man and the conquest of the workers' popular power.

in writing. the european union has been taking initiatives to improve the quality of life of people who move pets since 2003, when it established the basic framework for the possession, movement and hygiene of pets in regulation 998/2003. this report upgrades the existing framework. the main objective is to facilitate the movement of pet animal owners together with their animals within the union or to third countries, while preventing risks to public or animal health. the context of the concept of 'non-commercial pet animals' is also clarified so that the term cannot be exploited by professional traders in their attempt to circumvent the normal tax framework.

in writing. i voted in favour of the joint resolution on a macro-regional strategy for the alps because i believe that it will undoubtedly be an important tool for strengthening european territorial cooperation in the region and will make a tangible contribution to the development of the concept of territorial cohesion of the eu, which, following the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, has now been set as a priority on an equal footing with economic and social cohesion. i also believe that it will allow the countries of the region to coordinate existing eu funds, in particular those of cohesion policy, to implement projects aimed at addressing common challenges. in general, as the existing baltic and danube strategies have shown, macro-regional strategies help to promote cooperation, strengthen synergies with major community policies and create significant european added value as they must be based on the principle of multi-level governance in order to ensure cooperation between local, regional and national actors. in this context, it is legitimate and welcome to develop similar strategies throughout the eu, such as in the mediterranean region.

mr president, by imposing infrastructure charges on motorways, member states are applying the principle that the user should pay for the use of the motorway, rather than the taxpayer. this practice allows fair competition, for example in the transport of goods, between road transport on the one hand and maritime and rail transport on the other. the imposition of these charges is therefore justified in principle, provided that it is based on correct pricing which reflects the cost of construction and deterioration of the infrastructure. the commission's proposal for a directive on the harmonization of the laws of the member states relating to road transport is a very good one. firstly, the inertia of the member states and the relevant sectors in the organization of the european telecommunications service and, secondly, the asymmetry in the way that vignetting systems are used by several member states, which indirectly give their citizens a premium over other european users.

in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because council directive 2003/8/ec of 27 january 2003 will improve access to justice in cross-border disputes by establishing minimum common rules relating to legal aid for such disputes and is a very important piece of legislation for european citizens, as it aims to ensure that less well-off members of society also have access to justice in other member states, for example when exercising their right to free movement or benefiting from the internal market.
in writing. the negotiations between the european parliament, on the one hand, and the commission and council, on the other, have been successfully concluded, covering almost all food intended for infants and young children and for special medical purposes, thus enriching the relevant community legislative framework and increasing the protection of european citizens. by regulating a number of elements in food, such as gluten, lactose, food additives and food substitutes, this report seeks to protect consumers while at the same time regulating the avoidance of abusive rules and misleading advertising practices for the food sector and for the diet as a whole. the compromise solution recognizes the need to adopt specific requirements to meet the nutritional needs of infants, particularly for products intended for infants, which have at times been a matter of concern in our country. the amendments are considered satisfactory and i therefore voted in favour of the report.

mr president, i think that anyone who has not been persecuted and imprisoned can easily say a lot of things and say let us deal first with people who are in our region, in our country. we hear this rhetoric very often, but i remind you that citizens from countries like greece, which today receives a large number of refugees or asylum seekers, were in the past forced to seek asylum when persecuted by dictatorship and the junta. we therefore have an obligation to uphold this core human right, which is the right to provide asylum to those who are in real need. but the council must send the right message, because if it sends the wrong message then extremists take advantage of it and rely on it and create a great reaction in societies, as is being seen especially in societies in the south at the moment. we need a common european policy which is not a minimum standard but a correct standard, and we really need to share responsibility and not be led into situations where people who have been tortured are put back in detention, because, as you know very well, they are reliving their torture if they are forced to go through similar experiences.
mr president, although the level of protection in the asylum sector is being significantly increased by this new package, there are still obstacles to a truly integrated system. unfortunately, the revised regulation still provides for the return of asylum seekers to the country of first entry, even though it is known that ports of entry are countries such as greece, italy and malta, which receive the largest flows of migrants and refugees from asia and africa. we therefore continue to ask the most vulnerable members of our family, simply because they have the 'bad luck' of being on the border of a crisis area, to take full responsibility, knowing that they are not in a position to do so, despite the admittedly substantial financial and technical assistance provided by the commission. the libyans, commissioner, did not come to italy because they preferred that country, but simply because it is the easiest european country to reach. the european union has a duty to take care of them, and the same applies to the syrian refugees who have arrived in greece. so where is the fair distribution of the burden that is supposed to be the primary objective of european union policies? what is involved in the good faith sharing of responsibilities between member states or the humanitarian duty? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union is able to meet the challenges of the 21st century, and i am pleased to say that the commission has taken the necessary steps to ensure that the european union is able to meet the challenges of the 21st century. for these reasons, mr president, i have no choice but to vote against the dublin regulation.
