blue-card answer. – thank you very much for your intervention and question. i think the sharing of responsibilities is not to be measured only in economic terms, and i said in my intervention that generous economic support is also provided by the union and by the commission and, once again, i would like to thank you for this. sharing of responsibilities also applies in political and ethical terms: you must acknowledge the fact that we have external borders in the european union and that those countries that make up these external borders have to take on a much bigger burden because of crisis regions, so this is why i think that the sharing of burdens is also an ethical issue.
mr president, there is no doubt that the final agreement on the schengen package is a very important development. i would like to congratulate mr coelho and mrs weber on their efforts and tell you, ladies and gentlemen, that i myself have been following closely as rapporteur the whole process of preparing a third report on the amendment and revision of the schengen code and the schengen treaty in other areas, which we have completed with our colleagues without any problems at first reading and which we are voting on tomorrow. the schengen area and free movement within it remains one of the most promoted forms of cooperation in the european union, governed by very specific operating rules, and much more demanding respect by all member states. in two words: the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one. and it is clear that unilateral actions undermine the overall effort, they wound the sense of solidarity between the states that make up the schengen area as a whole. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption by the end of the year. the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the council on the basis of a proposal from the commission. and i'm referring specifically to: those who think, for example, that by giving travel documents to illegal immigrants, they solve the problem of the large number of illegal immigrants in a country, i do not know if they have any idea of reality, they certainly have no knowledge and no idea at all of the spirit and the way the schengen area works. they obviously have no concept of the effects of this speech. i would like to ask you, commissioner, if it is possible to give me an answer to that point.
the schengen system is certainly a great achievement for the free movement of citizens in europe, but it also has its side effects. i am one of those who believe in the need for security at the borders of the member states, which is of course guaranteed exclusively by the observance and application of internal rules without the intervention of the european union. we are supposed to be trying to ensure that the burden of the refugee flows on national societies is shared fairly. but how is this to be ensured, mr president, ladies and gentlemen? of the 26 european schengen countries, only 6 are experiencing all the migratory pressure, as countries on the external borders of the european union. the northern countries, the countries which have no maritime borders on the mediterranean, do not understand the problem facing greece, much more than any other country, in relation to its population. the economy of our country and its social structures have been devastated and unemployment is rampant. unfortunately, european migration policy does not involve a bona fide allocation of responsibilities between member states. suffice it to say: for the period 2007 to 2011, almost €1 billion was allocated to these six countries alone to address migration flows. the cost to greece is more than 30 billion.
mr president, i would like to stress one of the main messages of the excellent report by mr sánchez presedo, namely that competition policy must have as its ultimate aim the entry of new players and the emergence of new ideas and initiatives in the economies of the member states. this message has not been heard equally loudly in all the countries in crisis, although it is a prerequisite for the organization of a new, sustainable production model in the economy. at the same time, we are still far from convincing everyone that the rules are common to all. the factors which impede the completion of the single market have a macroeconomic dimension, such as differences in the accessibility or cost of money, labour law and others in the member states, which are of course only radically addressed by a single economic governance. however, the landscape will change dramatically if we move immediately to harmonise and simplify regulatory frameworks across all economic activities.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. mr president, commissioner, the issue we are discussing is very important, it concerns the operation of criminal organisations with an impact on local communities and, in fact, the commission must stop giving the impression that it covers countries and companies and draw up a proper list that includes all the countries and ships involved in such illegal activities. but i would also like to mention a matter which is topical in my country. a few hours ago the greek government, out of the blue, announced that at 12 o'clock tonight, it is shutting down the greek public television, all public television channels and radio stations. at the same time, the water supply system of my city, thessaloniki, and that of athens, is being privatized and sold, and schools and public buildings are being sold at the same time. i would ask the commission very clearly, commissioner: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. is the commission - through the troika - ordering the sale of the water company, schools and public buildings in greece?
mr president, commissioner, illegal fishing makes it economically, socially and environmentally impossible to manage the exploitation of the world's marine resources in a sustainable way. it is one of the most serious threats to the biodiversity of our planet's seas. regulation 1005/08, adapted to the lisbon treaty, is therefore a necessary instrument to combat this scourge, since it defines the responsibilities of the flag state, the coastal state, the port state and the market state, both in relation to the member states of the union and to third countries. but it is equally crucial that we fully implement our legislative framework, insisting strongly on the effective fight against illegal fishing by - and above all by - third countries. the need to combat this phenomenon is also a key argument for the establishment of a european coastguard.
mr president, today's debate on fisheries is of particular interest to an island country like greece. but that's not going to happen, because the three-party government has decided to shut down the greek public television, starting at midnight today, laying off 2,650 employees. the communist party of greece expresses its solidarity and supports the struggle of the greek television workers, demanding the government to immediately withdraw the legislative act.
mr president, commissioner, i too call on my colleagues to vote in favour of the amendments supporting the exemption of small farms from the linear reduction that may have to be imposed after the finalisation of the budget for the period 2014-2020. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new forms of production, which are not yet available. on the contrary, small farms are responsible for providing a wealth of social goods that are not rewarded by markets. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not affected by the effects of the crisis. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, i am very grateful for this opportunity to discuss the preparations for the next meeting of the european council. the irish presidency will be working, as always, very closely with president van rompuy to ensure the best possible preparation for this important meeting. the june european council will continue our focus on the goal of improving competitiveness, returning to strong and sustainable growth, and creating jobs. president van rompuy has therefore decided to focus the debate on the european semester, the fight against youth unemployment, improving access to finance and further deepening emu. on the european semester, the june meeting will bring this year’s exercise to a close through the endorsement of the country-specific recommendations. the european council is expected not only to underline the need to continue with fiscal consolidation, but also to acknowledge the importance of extending certain edp deadlines, given the current economic situation. this is part of a renewed effort to accelerate and consolidate structural reforms. the discussion should help create ownership of the recommendations at the highest level. this will in turn contribute to ensuring their swift and effective implementation at national level. in our preparations for the presidency, we identified effective management of this third european semester cycle as a key focus, and an instant contribution which we could make towards enhancing european stability. we felt it important to work very closely with others, ensuring that the lessons learned from last year’s exercise were fully taken on board, and building on the stronger bilateral dialogue now in place between the commission and the member states. what is important is that relevant council formations work in a coordinated and consistent manner towards a thorough preparation for the june european council. our work will continue over the coming weeks, particularly in terms of streamlining committee work under the aegis of epsco and ecofin, recognising their overlapping responsibilities on issues such as pension reforms, wage indexing, and the tax wedge on labour. the june european council will then set clear country-specific orientations for the national budget cycles across the union in the second half of the year. it is crucial that we keep up the momentum behind national-level reforms, underpinned by the goals of the europe 2020 strategy. this means restoring competitiveness, and unlocking the necessary investments across the union in growth that is smart, sustainable and inclusive. it is also appropriate that i acknowledge again on behalf of the presidency the crucial work parliament has been doing towards strengthening the democratic legitimacy of the european semester. parliament has been in the vanguard in highlighting that these must be open and inclusive processes, supporting meaningful engagement with all relevant stakeholders at both the national and european levels. stepping up our efforts to fight youth unemployment remains, of course, a top priority. there is a very strong focus at the top political level on the importance of confronting this enormous challenge, and its significance to all of our societies. i know it is of particular concern here in this parliament. it is very welcome therefore that president van rompuy has given such prominence to this issue for the june european council meeting and it is why getting agreement on the youth guarantee at epsco in february – that every young person should be in education, training or work within four months of leaving school – will rank amongst the most important presidency achievements for us. i expect that early proposals for national-level implementation must now be the key next step. this is of course an issue which is primarily the responsibility of individual member states, but one where there is clear added value at eu level, both in terms of support and learning. we should also acknowledge that different member states face very different implementation issues. this is where the eur 6 billion youth employment initiative, as part of the new mff, can make a crucial contribution. we look forward to parliament’s cooperation in making sure it is ready to go from the beginning of next year. and we also need to explore the other options, such as better mobilising structural funds to tackle youth unemployment. eu support will also be key to helping increase the mobility of young workers, and among other measures, the commission has made considerable efforts to develop eures into an effective tool for recruitment at european level, and plans to expand this further. it has also put forward a directive to tackle the remaining barriers to the freedom of movement of workers. we hope that both the council and parliament will be able to reach a rapid agreement on this. let me now turn to the issue of access to finance. financial fragmentation and the lack of access to credit are seriously undermining growth and must be tackled urgently. all the economic indicators are telling us that we need to redouble our efforts here: further contraction this year for both the eu and the euro area, and unemployment up nearly two million on a year ago. the key to bringing down unemployment across europe will, of course, be recovery in the real economy: companies growing their businesses and creating new jobs. the compact for growth and jobs, agreed last june, represents an important reinforcement of political commitment to doing what is necessary to support recovery. there will be a full review of the compact in june, one year on from its adoption. a top priority is to facilitate the take-up of financing instruments by companies, in particular smes, which constitute the backbone of the economy in most member states and which are absolutely key to creating jobs. rapid agreement on the cosme programme is a particular priority in this context, given its importance to smes. i would like to thank parliament for its cooperation on this extremely important file. but more needs to be done to encourage private investment and the european council is expected to decide on further action. the eur 10 billion increase in the eib’s paid-in capital that was agreed as part of the compact is now in place and will support a 40 % increase in lending capacity over the 2013-2015 period. it is also welcome that president draghi has made clear the ecb’s commitment to working closely with the other institutions, including the european commission and the eib, to improve business lending conditions. europe will have recovered from the current crisis only when its economies are growing again and creating jobs. this means, above all, unlocking new business investments in the real economy: successful firms expanding their activities at home and abroad, including new firms and new growth areas. there are a number of further issues that arise in june in the context of the compact, including industrial competitiveness and smart regulation. in the current difficult economic climate, a strong eu industrial base is absolutely crucial. the european council is expected to discuss action needed to assist recovery of european industry in the short and medium term and to ensure its long-term competitiveness and sustainability. focus will be on those strategic sectors which have the potential to underpin growth and create jobs. this will help to prepare for a more substantive discussion which is planned for the february 2014 european council. finally, on smart regulation, the heads of state and government will take stock of progress, in particular the commission’s ongoing work on the most burdensome regulations for smes and on regulatory fitness. the focus of the discussion will again be on competitiveness and job creation, in particular through avoiding unnecessary burdens on smes and micro-enterprises. all institutions have a part to play in this work. the other major issue on the agenda, of course, is economic and monetary union. although the situation on financial markets is less volatile than a year ago, we must continue to make progress on completing emu. the june european council will take stock of work so far, and will place particular emphasis on the top priority of completing the banking union, which has been advanced through the agreements on crr/crd iv and the ssm. banking union is intended to break the vicious circle between banks and sovereigns, and minimise the risk to taxpayers of having to deal with failed financial institutions. our earlier debate showed broad agreement and i think that more has yet to be done. we must agree quickly on the directives on bank recovery and resolution and deposit guarantee schemes. we must also concentrate on the single resolution mechanism. the discussions in the european council are certainly not the end of the story. i am very pleased to note that the european council is also expected to hold a discussion on latvia’s application to adopt the euro as its currency. the recent positive commission convergence report on latvian accession to the euro was hard-earned, and is testament to a country which has shown huge determination in confronting enormous economic challenges in recent years. the european council may also take decisions on the opening of accession negotiations with candidate countries, but this will of course depend on progress in the council in the run-up to the meeting. and the european council will also formally adopt the decision establishing the future composition of this parliament, based on a proposal from parliament, provided that you have given your consent. the june european council will be a further step in our efforts to return to growth, embed stability, create jobs and improve competitiveness in europe. this is part of a process which is absolutely essential for the future well-being of our societies and for the good of europe as a whole. i want to thank parliament for its support in working towards this shared goal.
president of the commission. − mr president, the next european council takes place in a very challenging context. several years of very weak or negative growth have led to very high levels of unemployment. youth unemployment and poverty are rising in many parts of europe. we need to go further with our efforts for growth at european level and in the member states. and i see this european council mainly focused on two issues: employment, particularly youth employment, and financing the economy, particularly smes. the june european council is expected to endorse the country-specific recommendations which the commission presented two weeks ago under the third european semester of economic policy coordination at european level. our analysis has shown that, in spite of all the difficulties, the european union and its member states are making progress in meeting many of the challenges. a rebalancing of the european union economy is taking place on the back of wide-ranging reforms but is not yet finished. significant adjustments are under way simultaneously across europe and need to be completed, also because of the interdependence of our economies. fiscal consolidation is ongoing and releasing pressure from the markets. this is precisely why it must continue in order to ensure a return to sustainable growth. we have seen, we have learned the hard way, that growth fuelled by high levels of debt, public debt or private debt, is simply not sustainable. reforms for competitiveness have been undertaken, particularly to improve the resilience and flexibility of the labour markets. this must be complemented by further urgent action to address the unacceptably high levels of unemployment and increasing poverty rates in a number of our member states. we must support the young so they succeed in the transition from education to work. restoring lending to smes, indeed to the economy in general, remains an absolute priority. lending conditions remain tight and the supply of credit limited in spite of massive support given to the banking sector. we need solutions for businesses with solid business plans that cannot get financing. above all, we need to accelerate these structural reforms. we need to step up their pace across the european union to secure recovery and ensure the rebalancing of the economy. ‘deficit’ countries need to boost their competitiveness. ‘surplus’ countries need to remove the structural obstacles to the growth of their domestic demand. the commission’s country-specific recommendations clearly say that member states can, and should, do more to help themselves get back to growth and move europe beyond the crisis. employment creation and measures that will restore lending to smes were already highlighted as priorities in our annual growth survey and are now at the core of our european semester. let me focus on the first point. 26 million europeans are unemployed, of whom over six million are young people. we must deliver effective measures to tackle this social crisis here and now. to this end, the commission will leave no stone unturned in its efforts. the commission and parliament have long been pushing for concrete action in terms of employment. indeed, as some of you remember, it was already in 2009 that i first proposed that the european council should meet specifically to address the issue of employment. frankly, at the time, there was little interest in this idea. instead of a special european council, there was a meeting of the trio of presidencies and in prague we met with the prime minister of the czech republic, the prime minister of sweden and the minister of labour of spain. i welcome the fact that now we have a consensus among member states to move this forward as a priority and that different member states are now taking initiatives nationally and bilaterally. it is important that all these initiatives are integrated in the european framework and momentum. the forthcoming european council should give a new impulse, building on what has already been set in motion. almost a year and a half ago the commission established youth employment action teams for those member states where youth unemployment was at its highest. over eur 16 billion from the structural funds have been reallocated. this has allowed eight member states to establish national youth action plans backed by real resources and we are seeing the first results on the ground. for example, in ireland an education and training fund, ‘momentum’, will provide skills training to 6 500 long-term unemployed. the esf will contribute 50 %, around eur 10 million, to this fund. in slovakia projects worth eur 70 million from the esf are being implemented with micro-enterprises and smes. to date 6 200 new jobs have been created. in latvia the focus is on upgrading vocational education schools in order to double the supply of vocational training to cover 40 % of young unemployed people. and in portugal a major initiative called ‘impulso jovem’ aims to promote youth employment and job creation, as well as targeted support for the private sector. some 90 000 young people and 4 500 smes are expected to benefit by 2015. in greece a major reprogramming has allowed the creation of a national youth action plan with european union funding of eur 571 million. and while these programmes took time to start in some countries, there will be hundreds of thousands of young people across europe who will benefit. but more work is needed and more work is already under way, because unemployment affects not hundreds of thousands but millions across europe. so we need more to make the youth guarantee a reality. the youth employment initiative should make a real difference in parts of europe where the youth unemployment rate is above 25 %. from our side we are working to frontload this initiative to hit the ground running at the very start of 2014. that means concentrating the financing in the very first years of the next programming period. by this i mean 2014, 2015 and 2016. these efforts need to be complemented with an appropriate allocation from the esf. it is therefore essential to assure a significant minimum share of the european social fund within the overall structural funds. programmes for education and mobility of young people, such as the proposed ‘erasmus for all’, are crucial. the ‘your first eures job’ programme will be strengthened by using the european social fund to support mobility of young jobseekers across europe. well-established apprenticeship systems are a feature of the member states with a better performance in terms of youth employment: for instance, austria and germany. therefore the commission is launching on 2 july 2013 the ‘european alliance for apprenticeships’ to improve the quality and supply of apprenticeships all over the european union, by bringing together national authorities, social partners, companies and relevant stakeholders. addressing skills mismatch in sectors with recognised job creation potential is also a priority. the commission has launched a multi-stakeholder partnership, the ‘grand coalition for digital jobs’ to tackle the challenge of the anticipated 900 000 vacancies in the ict sector. action for employment is a focus of the measures set out for the four programme countries. our proposed country-specific recommendations include youth employment-related recommendations for a further 17 member states: recommendations ranging from improving vocational training and effective employment services to reducing skills mismatches and expanding apprenticeships. we will support the member states wherever we can with the instruments that we have at european level and we will intensify the work with the social partners, which have a vital role to play in this matter. to create jobs we need growth, and for growth we need investment, smart investment – at european level, in the member states. the first point i want to make here is that europe can have a growth fund, a fund with almost eur 1 trillion, if the multiannual financial framework is adopted. that is why i urge all the member states to cooperate with the irish presidency and to make a move in accepting parliament’s justified requests, for example on flexibility. as the commission has always said, the difference between commitments and payments in the european council conclusions makes flexibility indispensable to a final agreement for the mff. if the mff is to have the desired impact on investment and growth it should be completed as soon as possible. some of our member states have a dramatic need for the investment it will bring. and all member states – i stress, all member states – have to realise there is no time to lose. ahead of the european council i will report, together with the european investment bank, on possible short-term measures to restore lending to the economy. our focus will be first of all on the financing of smes. smes in particular are feeling the pinch as bank loans dry up. smes, in fact all our companies, need a return to normal lending conditions. the need is most acute in those member states which have been hardest hit by the crisis. excessively tight access to credit remains a key obstacle to the revival of economic activity – especially in the most vulnerable member states. the differential in lending spreads across the union implies a wide variation in financing conditions for companies in the single market and, particularly, in the euro area. these spreads seem to relate less to the intrinsic credit quality of the borrower and more to their geographical location! this is absurd in an economic and monetary union. smes are particularly affected by these constraints in the credit supply. here is the vicious circle which we need to break. those countries most in need of investment and of a return to a normal financial sector are those where national actions alone are unlikely to achieve this result in the short term. if there is no confidence, banks will not lend and firms will be reluctant to borrow. a european consensus will help restore confidence, break the vicious circle and boost economic activity. let me give you some examples. the net savings rate in portugal, in spite of all the extreme difficulties that country is facing, is higher than at any time in the last decade. in france, in ireland, in slovakia, in sweden and in the uk, savings rates post-crisis are above – sometimes well above – pre-2008 levels. yet savers are not prepared to invest their funds, nor is there a willingness to consume. that shows that confidence is key to growth. we are also exploring other ways to improve access to finance. we need to provide companies with alternatives to bank loans – for example, private equity, risk-sharing instruments with the european investment bank. we will soon come forward with a proposal for a regulatory framework for long-term investment funds. the commission was at the forefront of the successful efforts to increase the capital of the eib. we now hope that the eib will be able to contribute to our efforts to improve access to finance. we are working actively with the eib to that end. three types of ‘joint instrument’ are currently under consideration. all three involve pooling of resources from the structural funds, horizon 2020, cosme and the eib/eif and other public banks to increase lending to smes by providing guarantees to banks. in every case the provision of public support will be conditional on the benefits being passed through to smes in the form of increased lending. restoring this confidence by setting out a vision of where the european economy is headed is the reason for working towards deeper economic and monetary union. the progress of latvia towards joining the euro area is a sign of confidence in the euro. it is a concrete message. contrary to what some have predicted, what is happening is not that countries are leaving the euro, but new countries are joining the euro. creating a banking union is essential. it is the most important short-term action in the process of deepening economic and monetary union and our efforts towards the banking union cannot be relaxed. next week the council should reach a general approach on the bank recovery and resolution directive. the single supervisory mechanism will be formally adopted before the summer. this paves the way for the commission’s proposal for a single resolution mechanism which will be ready in the coming weeks. at the same time, the commission is working on the other dimensions of emu; on the ex-ante coordination of major economic reforms; on the competitiveness and convergence instrument and most importantly on the social dimension of emu. in all of these areas the commission will come forward with specific proposals. we must also do more on implementation. for this, we need to complete and fully enact the compact for growth and jobs which also includes the important measures to strengthen the internal market, presented as single market acts i and ii. let me take just one example, an example that we discussed yesterday in the college of commissioners: the digital market, and telecommunications specifically. one of the main problems we have is that we do not have a digital market in europe at this time of digital transformation. indeed, instead of one single market for the digital field, we have 27 mini-markets, or even micro-markets. i would even say nano-markets, when you compare them to the other big markets in the world, like the united states or china. this is why we need to address this issue, and the commission will put forward new proposals in early september. we have also worked on proposals to develop an industrial policy which delivers industrial competitiveness. the steel action plan which we adopted yesterday shows that we are responsive to the difficulties which european industries face. the commission is also working to reduce the overall burden of regulation, especially on smes. our forthcoming communication on the top ten burdensome regulations is an example of how we are working to make life easier for european firms. at the same time, the work on fair burden-sharing between all parts of society is continuing. just yesterday, the commission adopted a proposal for the automatic exchange of information on savings taxation. we will continue to deliver on the tax agenda, and in the g8 in northern ireland next week we will push forward this priority at global level. indeed, we will work on the other priorities set out by the british g8 presidency: transparency and trade. as a conclusion, let me tell you that this is the time to develop what i could call a european consensus: if we do not pull together, we will be pulled apart. we need a consensus built on practical steps to resolve the crisis. a consensus not just on the need for growth and jobs, but on the means to achieve this. a consensus for an economically more competitive europe and a socially more inclusive europe. such a consensus is not just a political formula. it is of critical importance for economic confidence for investors and for consumers. because divisions and contradictory messages coming from different capitals are counterproductive and undermine confidence – confidence we need from financial markets, from businesses, and particularly from our citizens! in a nutshell the consensus has three elements: first, on-going fiscal consolidation should continue at a pace that reflects the situation in each country and is focused on structural rather than nominal deficits. second, member states should intensify in parallel their efforts on the implementation of structural reforms for competitiveness. third, because many of these structural measures take time before delivering concrete results in terms of employment creation, specific focused action should be taken to deliver short-term results for the unemployed, and especially for the young. we need action to build on the foundations we have put in place. in the european council i will urge the member states to assume their responsibilities, implement what we have already agreed and commit to effective action for the future. i know the commission can count on the support of this parliament for our agenda for growth and jobs.
mr president, today's debate in the european parliament on the preparatory work for the european council on 27 and 28 june is very important for the peoples of the member states of the european union, for all peoples. unfortunately, however, this is not the case for the greeks. last night the three-party government decided to shut down the greek public television and radio stations, simultaneously firing 2,650 workers. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
mr president, mr vice-president of the commission, mr commissioner, a short while ago my group's leader, hannes swoboda, said that he was shocked by the government's surprise decision in greece to close down public broadcasting overnight. the european parliament has also called for the commission to be given the task of drawing up a report on the situation in the baltic sea. i think that the commission should be able to give its opinion on the matter, and i hope that the council will be able to give its opinion on the matter. do you know who is responsible for this decision, mr vice-president of the commission? it is giving in to the troika's pressure to have an immediate effect on the demand for 2,000 redundancies in the public sector by the end of the month. unfortunately, the government was unable to implement a credible plan for substantial restructuring and, to avoid funding and installment problems, it closed down the public broadcaster overnight. i would therefore like to ask you directly: does the commission, as a member of the troika, agree to the closure of public broadcasting in greece? the commission believes that democracy can be part of a programme of fiscal consolidation. are there cuts in democracy? i ask you to take a stand, because in greece the sudden death of public broadcasting is a confirmation of the slow death of democracy and this is not a greek problem, it is a european problem.
mr president, as always, we shall be discussing today, or are discussing, how we see the council developing in the coming days. but what about the council, mr president? the commission has lost its real, deeper, more substantial role as rapporteur and the european council is rushing to make decisions, while the european parliament is being ignored. we all talk about growth and competitiveness. does anyone think that there will be competition with the brics, with the working conditions and the situation that exist there? we need new data, we need research and innovation, we need to avoid duplication of work in the member states on the same specific issues. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the lisbon guidelines have failed miserably and now it is very likely that the 20-20 strategy will fail, with a multiannual budgetary framework which is suffering and for which we have not yet found a solution among ourselves. mr president, in addition to money for growth and job creation, we also need to have the will, we need new ideas and, above all, we need to put into practice the decisions that have been taken, because it is unprecedented that the council has decided last year on banking matters, on specific matters, and then, a few months later, the council refuses to do so.
in writing. the kke opposes the directives and regulations which make up the eu's so-called 'asylum system', because they make its anti-migration policy even tougher. the rights of refugees and migrants are being stifled and the conditions for granting asylum are being tightened up. the dublin ii regulation remains in force, keeping a very large number of migrants who want to move to other eu countries trapped in the countries of entry. the upgrading of the eurodack electronic fingerprint database will record all migrants and refugees in all eu member states. their personal data are made available to national police authorities and europol, creating a giant eu mechanism for monitoring and repression of migrants. in a period when imperialism is becoming more aggressive, eu-us-nato imperialist interventions are uprooting millions of people from their countries who are desperately trying to survive, and the eu is upgrading repressive mechanisms so that - and in conjunction with the governments in its member states - it can open the floodgates of immigration in line with the needs of the monopolies for industrious foreign labor and the intensification of the exploitation of the working class as a whole.
in writing. the report, which deals with issues of critical importance to greece in the area of asylum (the dublin regulation), was adopted by the ep's plenary without a vote and by simple announcement from the bureau, because the procedure for an early second reading is followed, as required by the rules of procedure. in my speech yesterday in the plenary i said that since there will be no roll-call vote, my vote on the revision of the dublin regulation is negative - as it was in the libe committee in all the votes - and the same applies to the vote of my greek colleagues from the new democracy in the european parliament. at the same time, i stressed once again the need to establish a mechanism for internal (intra-eu) resettlement of migrants, based on a redistribution clause according to qualitative and quantitative indicators applicable to each member state.
in writing. the dublin regulation makes greece a country that has to face alone a huge wave of illegal immigration, without any mechanisms of real european community solidarity. the implementation of the eu's dublin iii treaty provides for the return of illegal migrants to their country of entry, which unfortunately, in an overwhelming proportion, is greece. i therefore voted against mrs willstrom's report, which i disagree with on the substance for the reasons i have just mentioned.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report which refers to the effort to combat poverty and social exclusion in the eu. the economic crisis requires even greater action at european and national level in order to achieve the europe 2020 target of reducing by at least 20 million the number of people at risk of poverty or social exclusion by 2020. it is noted that at present this figure is close to 120 million. to date, the main union instrument to support employment, combat poverty and promote integration is the european social fund, which will of course remain the main pillar of social actions in the next financial period (2014-2020). however, the proposed new fund, which will have a budget of eur 2,5 billion for the period 2014-2020, will be a broader instrument for providing material assistance in the form of goods other than food already provided to homeless people and/or children, as well as accompanying measures aimed at the social reintegration of those assisted. it is therefore not a substitute for existing eu policies but an auxiliary and targeted tool in addressing contemporary challenges.
in writing. the economic and financial crisis has exacerbated poverty and social exclusion across the union. children in particular need to be protected as they are particularly vulnerable to malnutrition and poor eating habits can adversely affect their development. at the same time, the economic and financial crisis has reduced the capacity of many member states to provide support to people at risk of poverty and social exclusion. the proposed fund, with a budget of eur 2,5 billion for the period 2014-2020, will be able to help the economically weakest. i therefore voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, first of all, as regards the scope of the proposal, the increased and eu-wide harmonised thresholds for small companies (article 3) will help them to benefit from a level playing field across the eu. following the impact assessment carried out in 2009-2011, the commission aims at reducing the administrative burden by 25% by 2012 and foresees potential savings of eur 1.5 billion per year for all companies falling under the scope of the directive.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because, with growing volatility in global financial markets, investors are increasingly demanding more information about companies’ practices. it is clear that investors want improved transparency to gain a comprehensive economic picture of investment and risk. in addition, transparency in terms of disclosure is an important indicator of strong corporate governance. given all of these initiatives, there is currently a risk of a fragmented response to transparency. it is therefore important to establish a framework for strong global standards in this area. the proposal to amend the 2004 transparency directive puts such rules in place for eu companies, reflecting standards put forward in the dodd-frank act in the us. this provides a strong starting point for achieving the ultimate goal of global rules in these areas
in writing. − i voted in favour of this report because i support the proposal of the rapporteur for an amendment where the cut to direct payments for claims made in 2013 would amount to 0.748005%, rather than the 4.981759% put forward by the commission.
in writing. the kke voted against the regulations to reformulate the schengen treaty in an even more reactionary direction, because they deepen its anti-people character as a mechanism for encapsulating, strengthening and coordinating eu and national repressive mechanisms. the european commission is given increased powers to impose additional controls, including the closure of the external borders of the member states, and even the intervention of eu or police and military forces of other member states at the borders of another member state. these provisions serve the need of the eu and the bourgeois governments to strengthen their repressive mechanisms and action against the workers' and popular movement, the prohibition of demonstrations, the movement of protesters, and popular mobilizations in the run-up to the meetings of the european council of the eu, the c20, the g8, etc. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force. the lies of the bourgeois governments, the parties of capital and opportunism are exposed, that the schengen treaty is supposedly a 'guarantee of the free movement of citizens', of a 'europe without borders', etc. the reality is that the intensification of anti-people barbarism by the eu and its governments in the member states goes hand in hand with the intensification of repression against the labour movement, authoritarianism and violence against the people.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the situation of the outermost and least populated regions and island regions is difficult and needs to be strengthened. the rapporteur's proposal that the commission redefine the new guidelines on regional aid for outermost regions in order to improve operating aid in these regions is in full agreement with me.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on regional policy as part of wider state aid schemes, which is the european parliament's contribution to the final regional aid guidelines so that this type of aid can play an important and effective role and be focused where it is most needed. it is important that the existing system of regional state aid be adapted to the new economic reality and to the future cohesion policy, taking into account its impact on the real economy, job creation and regional development. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the quality of the aid, including the introduction of a new system of aid for the production of new products. also, with regard to the issue of investment aid to large enterprises, the overall exemption of large enterprises from the state aid rules should be considered in the light of their contribution to employment, the opportunities created for the benefit of small and medium-sized enterprises and their participation in research and development.
in writing. the forthcoming european council meeting, which will focus on tackling unemployment and the challenges facing young people today, is certainly encouraging. this initiative coincides with the evaluation of the european youth strategy (2010-2018) which i am submitting to the european parliament and which contains important proposals for the member states to take advantage of. it is clear that member states and the european commission need to step up their efforts on a number of issues, such as education, training and support for innovation. but what is urgent today is to tackle youth unemployment. the european social fund until recently had 30 billion euros for this purpose and unfortunately the member states had not adopted the appropriate policies to make full use of them. at the same time, new tools such as the 'youth guarantee' are increasing opportunities for young people to enter the labour market, participate in training or start their own business. of course, much needs to be done. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of young people from the risks of exposure to asbestos is a good one, but it is not enough to make the directive more effective.

mr. president, instead of a clear apology for the unjustified police violence, prime minister erdogan organized counter-protests and ordered police yesterday to push back the protesters from taksim square. so it seems that he does not understand the seriousness of the situation and does not understand that, even if he wins the battle in taksim, the "war" for the trust of a significant part of the citizens will be lost. the crisis in turkey has political-religious characteristics and is related to the way of governing, the personal style of the prime minister, but also a misunderstanding of the concept of democracy. democracy is not just holding elections. when a leader, a party, an ideology, thinks that they can impose their worldview on everyone because they won an election, then democracy is in great danger. in a democracy, every opinion must be respected and the majority must respect the view of the minority. the country is being driven into a polarisation with uncontrollable consequences for the stability of the wider region. the myth of turkey as a model of democracy for the arab world is now being shattered. the socialist group has said this many times. a regime with the largest number of imprisoned journalists in the world is not a model of democracy. the restriction of free expression on the internet and the persecution of social network users in recent days have increased our concerns. we have no reason to celebrate the developments. turkey is an important country for stability in the middle east and it is important that it plays this role. but, lady anston, unfortunately your statements last sunday were below the circumstances, equating the perpetrator with the victim. we must stand firmly, council, parliament and commission, on the side of those who are fighting for democracy and human rights.
mr president, over the last 10 years the european union has shown itself very satisfied with the changes that have taken place in turkey, and indeed the attack on the military establishment and the attempt to bring about democratic changes have given us a great deal of satisfaction. but today we see islam being introduced through the back door as a principle and value of governing the country. the people who took to the streets and the protests, mr. president, are not with the kemalists, the corrupt establishment, or the current situation. it is with us, with us europeans and with european democracy. we must support them too, mr president. we will not be the ones to decide who will be the prime minister of turkey, but we must tell turkey that the caresses of europe will stop unless there is real freedom and democracy in that country.
mr president, a strong smell of authoritarianism and arrogant leadership is being emitted from turkey all these days. the functioning of democracy is becoming extremely problematic. it is therefore confirmed that turkey cannot be an example of democracy for the arab world. it is thus confirmed that it has a very long way to go on its way to europe. but this must not lead europe to turn its back on turkey. and that is because, as it has been shown these days, the most serious motive and the most serious guarantee of democracy for turkey is europe after all. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force. finally, mrs. ashton, say something bold and do something meaningful.

mr president, it is true that the creation of the foreign service has brought a lot of good things so far and i want to congratulate lady aston on her efforts, because this has not been an easy case. but, following the way in which the office has operated, i think it must be accepted that certain things must change, as parliament is proposing. the first is that the hierarchical structure must be better structured. it seems that there is an overload of senior officials and that there is often a concentration of efforts, not fully coordinated, between the european commission in general and the external action service. i have the impression, therefore, that together with an effort to have more women, who are unfortunately under-represented in this service, and with the establishment of a headquarters in brussels for security and defence, whether for military or civilian operations, the union's external action service will have to be much more effective and will be able to react much more quickly. of course, mr president, i repeat, we must recognize the efforts that have been made and the struggle that is being waged, and on the basis of these efforts we are calling for more substantial changes, such as those which will make the european external action service better coordinated and above all show results, because the union must acquire a brand name abroad which it does not yet have. these results are disproportionate to the substantial efforts made. so there is a need for greater promotion.
madam president, the arrangements proposed in the report are designed to upgrade the agency as a political military machine with greater firepower for the european union's imperialist interventions on an international scale. they are aimed at making the european union's foreign policy even more aggressive, a policy of permanent intervention by strengthening its military characteristics to carry out extensive military operations. the upgrading of the service is being promoted by measures such as: the establishment of a permanent operational headquarters for common security and defence policy operations, the participation of battle groups in eu interventions, the acceleration of financing and decision-making procedures, measures for a permanent structure for conducting military operations 'crisis management'. the establishment of a political council and measures to influence the policies of the member states are intended to ensure that the office can operate without control. the only purpose of the unrealized and hypocritical request for the european parliament to be informed and to be able to monitor the agency's activities is to give a democratic legitimacy to the eu and nato's military interventions.

i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, safety at sea is a fundamental and non-negotiable priority and one of the most important elements of maritime safety is effective port state control of ships. the very important maritime labour convention of 2006 which will come into force in august is thus strengthened. the agreement on the proposal for a directive on port state control of ships which we are called upon to adopt tomorrow in plenary ensures all the objectives and priorities we have set, without unnecessarily burdening the member states with a large increase in the number of inspections. it is a perfectly satisfactory compromise which we have reached after intensive negotiations with the council. it fully meets the european parliament's key priorities, while taking into account the sensitivities of all parties, without departing from the provisions of the international maritime labour convention. in particular, the text we are voting on tomorrow guarantees the protection of seafarers' rights and a level playing field for countries and shipowners who have undertaken to offer seafarers decent working and living conditions, thus protecting them from unfair competition from ships with a low level of safety. i would like to conclude by urging those member states which have not yet done so to ratify the maritime labour convention as soon as possible.

mr president, commissioner, the maritime labour convention of 2006 is a milestone in the effort to curb some of the most damaging effects of globalisation. in recent decades, the shipping industry has been the driving force of the world economy, transporting 90% of world trade, but the transport project was achieved without ensuring basic working conditions for the 1.2 million seafarers and the result, among other things, is that the position of serious shipowners and flag states is undermined by widespread irresponsibility and community dumping. the integration of the convention into the port state control system of the european union will ensure better conditions for seafarers on ships calling at ports in the european union, irrespective of their nationality. in this way, against the backdrop of the tragic events of april in savar, bangladesh, it is once again clear that globalised labour can and must be subject to fair rules.
in writing. to achieve the zero road accident target, initiatives and investments are required in all aspects of road transport. the safety of the vehicles themselves is of course an important prerequisite, but it must be accompanied by other measures such as better driver training, safer infrastructure and roads, and of course the implementation of intelligent systems. the periodic vehicle inspection is regulated by directive 2009/40/ec, which came into force just four years ago. it would be legitimate to include in the scope of the legislation bicycles, which should be subject to compulsory control. on the other hand, the change in the frequency of checks for category m1, i.e. i.d.s, from biannual to annual, must be considered more thoroughly and clearly take into account the financial burden that would be imposed on eu citizens in the prolonged period of crisis that we are experiencing.
mr president, the figures speak for themselves and are a cause for great concern and concern. although they are well known, they are worth hearing once again: more than 30,000 people have been killed and nearly 1.5 million have been injured. in fact, of these, 250,000 were serious road accidents in the european union in 2011 alone. for every fatal accident, there are 4 accidents resulting in permanent disability and 10 resulting in serious injury. more than half of these injuries occur in urban areas. the social and economic cost of road traffic injuries amounts to 2% of the european union's gross national product, or eur 250 billion for 2012. from 2001 to 2010, the targeted actions proposed by the european commission have had positive results and we must acknowledge this. the number of road accident victims has decreased significantly in several countries. it is important and encouraging that in 2010 the european commission renewed its commitment to further reduce accidents by 50% by 2020. it is committed to developing a comprehensive strategy on issues such as the impact of conflict, strategic accident management, first aid and emergency services, and long-term rehabilitation processes. but more decisive european and national policies on road safety are needed. the loss of even one human life on the streets is not an acceptable possibility and we must use all appropriate means and all other technical means to succeed in meeting this great challenge to the european citizens.
mr president, commissioner, as the commission points out, we have the potential to drastically reduce the serious injuries that occur with alarming frequency. we have just heard some chilling figures on the urban areas of the union. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the road network is not affected by the accident, and that the road network is not affected by the accident. road safety, which threatens millions of cyclists, cannot be disconnected from the decisions needed on infrastructure and road rules in europe's cities, whether it is cycling infrastructure, speed limits or the implementation of smart systems. it is time for the european union to intervene more effectively in urban mobility.

the next item is the report by mr richard seeber, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on priority substances in the field of water policy (com (2011) 0876 c7-0026/2012 2011/0429 (cod)) (a7-0397/2012)
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. madam president, commissioner, we have asked for and achieved in this directive that medicines should be included in the watch list so that the public is aware of the concentrations of specific medicines and so that the commission is prepared to bring the matter back for discussion in the next review. we have decided to change and we are calling for a change in the way medicines are authorized, in order to take account of their impact on the environment. we have succeeded in having stricter limits for substances already classified as harmful. but it is a real shame for the council that it has really squandered an opportunity for europe to invest in getting out of the crisis, namely investing in water management infrastructure, by postponing the date of application of the new obligations on substances which have been shown to be dangerous and harmful to 2027. but in europe in general, water is the target. i have emailed you, colleagues, the letter from the european federation of public service unions, asking for your support by signing the call against the privatization of the water in thessaloniki. and, commissioner, a matter of direct concern to you: in the north pindus national park, in a natura area, a river is being promoted for diversion. the water framework directive management plans are promoting its legalization to be used to produce energy equivalent to five wind turbines. it's really a shame! destruction of a national forest through management plans! please look at it carefully.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. besides, what we see with austerity policy is that in areas that are small, public services are being abandoned and the population is being burdened, because they don't have access to health services, there are no good connections - there are islands that don't have regular connections - schools are being closed and public services are being withdrawn from nearby areas. so instead of reducing the disparity, of bringing europe closer together, the distance between regions is increasing, both because of the crisis and because of austerity policies. the commission should therefore consider whether there is a need for a specific policy for those areas which are currently suffering from a profound economic, social and ecological crisis, and it is important that the population which is being left behind by the basic and fundamental rights has access to health, education and a proper transport network.
mr president, commissioner, the external aviation policy, introduced in 2005, has benefited the union in recent years, with the agreement with the united states of america alone creating 80,000 new jobs. but this institutional framework has entered a period of stagnation awaiting ambitious political action and agreements, given, firstly, that union airlines are going through a prolonged period of crisis and therefore unable to exploit opportunities on the world market, especially in asia, and secondly, that agreements with neighbouring states such as ukraine, tunisia and lebanon, which can stimulate the european market, remain to be concluded, while the council has not yet given the commission a mandate for negotiations with turkey, armenia and libya. finally, i would add that the gulf companies, and not only them, continue to receive direct and indirect state funding and thus to be in unfair competition with european companies.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, the main cause of the greatest debt crisis the union has ever known and the consequent social scourge of unemployment, which is constantly expanding at a nightmarish pace, is that europe has lost its competitiveness. the emergence of new global players has displaced it from the vanguard of the global economy. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. the shift towards a marine economy and rich marine resources is an ambitious, visionary but realistic proposal. it opens up prospects for broad cooperation between economic sectors not only in the coastal regions of the union of 28 member states, but throughout its territory. coastal and maritime tourism in all its thematic expressions, aquaculture, blue energy, marine biotechnology, marine mineral extraction and, through them, the strengthening of shipping and the european shipbuilding industry are the areas of blue growth that can increase the size of the maritime economy to eur 590 billion by 2020 and with appropriate training policies ensuring a mobile workforce with adequate skills and experience, total employment in blue growth could exceed an estimated 7 million jobs by 2020. local, regional, national and european authorities should create the necessary conditions for sustainable development, such as systems of maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management, upgrading infrastructure, access to professional skills, dissemination of information and best practices and, of course, ensuring adequate funding. in order for the blue growth sectors to develop and unleash their full potential contribution to the european economy, strategic planning at european union level is necessary in terms of coordination and cooperation between existing policies as well as in terms of the development of new initiatives and tools. of course, to achieve the maximum potential of blue growth, public and private actors must join forces and work within a single policy framework that takes account of the new priorities of the european union. at a time of cutbacks in public investment in the member states, it is imperative that development policies, and in particular high-cost projects such as transport, communication and energy infrastructure, ensure adequate funding during the 2014-2020 programming period and beyond. the available resources of european funds and financial mechanisms need to be channelled into blue economy projects which are sure to bring european added value. the primary prerequisite and key challenge is to ensure the full sustainability of blue development, i.e. that it does not harm the highly vulnerable marine environment and at the same time contributes to combating climate change. the peoples of europe have for the last nine thousand years been turning in an organized way to the sea in search of and securing a better future. we welcome the commission's communication on blue growth and ask you to speed up the procedures and take whatever action is necessary to support it. finally, i would like to thank all the shadow rapporteurs from the bottom of my heart for the excellent cooperation they have given us and for all those who have worked on the final drafting of our report.
mr president, we do want to develop the blue economy, but it must also be greener. because certainly many professions depend on the sea, but we also often have pollution of the environment and degradation of marine biodiversity. we want a blue economy, which will strengthen and increase activities around the marine wealth, but which will also help to preserve this wealth in the long term, and which will help to promote social cohesion and new professions which very young people have studied but which are not being used in current policies. we also want proper maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management plans so that the blue economy really benefits the environment, employment and the economy.
the new cooperatives, mr president, we would say cooperatives which meet the seven basic principles, play a very important role in the economy, in social cohesion and in social and environmental responsibility. the countries in crisis are the ones that need the support of cooperatives most today, so that they can create new and sustainable economic activities and absorb the millions of unemployed in countries like greece. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in my country, for example, in greece, an institutional framework was created, which changed very quickly before it was even implemented. the department responsible for promoting facilities for the development of social cooperatives was dissolved. we see that the cooperative banks are not being recapitalised and that they are facing very great difficulties in their survival and, whereas the troika should have met with them from the outset, it was only recently that it agreed to meet with representatives of the greek cooperative banks.
in writing. the agreement on the eu's long-term budget for 2014-2020 is not the most ideal. however, the substantial improvements made by parliament to the february summit proposal make the agreement acceptable. parliament's main demands to focus on growth and job creation, combat youth unemployment, strengthen research and innovation programmes and support small and mediumsized enterprises were largely met. we have also received guarantees that the total budget will be used to the maximum, that there will be more flexibility in payments and commitments and that the budget can be reviewed and revised in the meantime. we also received the commitment from governments that there will be an immediate agreement to disburse eur 7.3 billion to cover part of the outstanding accounts for 2013. the priority now is to prepare the new programmes in good time so that they can run as smoothly as possible from 1 january and contribute to efforts to overcome the crisis. let us not forget that the eu budget is the main community investment instrument, acting as a catalyst for economic growth, job creation and the achievement of social, economic and territorial cohesion.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which aims to strengthen and enforce roadworthiness tests on motor vehicles to improve road safety and environmental protection. the proposal improves the existing legislative framework by providing for more precise conditions for the revocation and cancellation of vehicle registration certificates. for example, vehicles which pose a direct risk to road safety are not allowed to circulate on the road network until they have successfully passed a new roadworthiness test. at the same time, red tape is being tackled more effectively, as the original registration of vehicles reclassified in another member state is now automatically cancelled, and new practices are being introduced in relation to electronic registration databases.
in writing. the intensification of checks on commercial vehicles is a priority in the effort to reduce road accidents and improve road safety. for greece, a country with an extensive rural and dangerous road network, further strengthening of these provisions is important and welcome. the present proposal for a directive, which i voted in favour of, introduces a number of important new provisions, such as technical roadside checks on light commercial vehicles, an increase in technical roadside checks by the member states each year and even more intensive checks on goods, an area in which our country has paid a heavy price in the past in terms of human and unfortunately children's lives, with the most tragic incident being the accident in tempe a few years ago. however, proper controls certainly require qualified and properly trained controllers, which is a point for which member states are responsible in view of the increasing complexity of control and the high requirements imposed by the new directive.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr danelli's report. marine spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management contribute to the coherent and efficient use of marine space. the importance of the commission developing programmes to promote coastal and maritime tourism, the fisheries and aquaculture sectors and to promote education and job creation in coastal, island and maritime areas are considered very important measures and should be strengthened.
in writing. blue growth is the maritime arm of the eu's europe 2020 anti-people strategy, so that capital can take advantage of the latest technological developments to exploit traditional and new marine resources, with major investments in shipping, ports, coastal shipping and transport networks, sea corridors, and the extraction of hydrocarbons and minerals from the ocean floor; profitability opportunities in shipbuilding and ship repair, renewable energy, offshore, wind and marine energy infrastructure; the intensification of fish farming; the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement. it promotes the further completion of the single market, especially in maritime transport, and the creation of free trade areas on the islands. it demands the complete abolition of "campotas", even more flexible labour relations without rights, flexibility and mobility, direct subordination of public structures, research and education, to the choices of the large tourist enterprises and shipowners. the report is in line with the aspirations of the greek bourgeoisie and the nd-pasok government for a so-called "european eez", by assigning the country's wealth-producing resources to monopolies and integrating the eez issue into the confrontations and rivalries of the major imperialist powers and emerging capitalist centres. the recommendation to create a 'european coastguard' is also moving in the same unacceptable direction.
in writing. the bioeconomy is a relatively new field of action for europe and initiatives in this direction are certainly encouraging. given the increasing demand for food and energy and the scarcity of global natural resources, the search for new forms of production and consumption, and early planning towards this end - despite the obstacles posed by the crisis - is imperative for europe. this own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, calls for europe to be vigilant and for the commission to take new and more thorough initiatives on how to strengthen the bioeconomy in the eu. it is noted that this sector is expected to be significantly strengthened in terms of community resources and investment in the near future and that the country should closely monitor the upcoming developments. proposals such as research and innovation programmes, support for industrial investment, technological infrastructure, scientific excellence, bio-based facilities and industries aimed at innovation and competitiveness are expected to be intensified, offering significant economic opportunities that our country should exploit in its effort to build a new production model and overcome the economic crisis.

has been taking a consistent and coherent position on the financial transaction tax because we believe that it ensures that the financial sector is fairly shared in the costs of the crisis, because it secures significant resources for both the national and the community budget, it opens the way for the same resources and, finally, because it discourages speculation and encourages the sector's shift towards the productive economy. i hope and we hope that the same consistent and coherent stance will be taken by the 11 member states which have committed themselves to moving forward with the individual interest, of any sector, however powerful, is the common european interest, the interest of european citizens, who are bearing the enormous economic and social costs of the crisis.

in writing. the programme of the lithuanian presidency of the council of the eu the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a sign of the intensification of the anti-people policy of the eu and the bourgeois governments in all aspects of the life of the working class and the popular strata, in order to maximize the profits of the monopolies. new cuts in wages and pensions, the collapse of collective agreements, the tightening of public social security, health, welfare and education systems, an even greater increase in unemployment, the spread of poverty and misery. on the other hand, the european banking union will be promoted, with a view to controlled destruction and greater concentration and centralisation of financial capital, the deepening of all the reactionary features of emu, the implementation of 'memoranda' in all the member states with 'enhanced economic governance' and the 'european semester'. with plenty of hot money from the looting of the people's incomes, the monopoly groups will be financed for investments in fast and highly profitable sectors such as "green technologies", energy, transport, activities across the spectrum of economic sectors related to shipping and the sea. investments on the ruins of workers' rights, with the price of labour demolished. the european union's imperialist policy will continue with even greater aggressiveness, with its open fronts in the southern mediterranean, the middle east and north africa at its core.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) to provide technical assistance at the initiative of the commission. the cor aims to support workers made redundant, particularly in regions and sectors which are disadvantaged by the opening up of the global economy. the regulation provides that up to 0.35% of its annual amount may be allocated each year to technical assistance. the commission requested eur 750 000 for 2013 to cover activities related to the follow-up of applications submitted, the extension of the fund's website, the production of publications and audiovisual media, administrative and technical support in the member states and the preparation for the evaluation of the fund for the period 2007-2013. i would like to take this opportunity to highlight the added value of the egf as an instrument of eu social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. in addition, the support provided is a practical expression of eu solidarity.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the communicable diseases early warning and response system was launched in 1998 to prevent serious crossborder health threats, equipping the european commission with the logistical infrastructure needed to carry out its work. fifteen years later, in addition to the evaluation, the early warning system must also be modernised. this report proposes to extend risk assessment measures to all threats to health, whether of biological, chemical or environmental origin, while providing for the possibility of recognising a european 'public health emergency'. the report is of particular importance for greece, as it concerns diseases introduced into the eu through illegal immigration and poses a risk to public health.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report as it aims to improve the legal basis, particularly at the level of cooperation between states and effectiveness in relation to crossborder threats in the event of serious health threats.
