in writing. i voted in favour of this joint resolution. the heavy rains and flood damage hit central europe hard. the resolution also stresses the need to release financial aid to provide aid and support through the structural funds for the reconstruction of the devastated areas.
in writing. i voted in favour of the resolution on the recent floods in the countries of central europe, the worst floods to have ever hit the region, causing loss of life and material damage with very serious economic impact. i would also like to express my solidarity with the citizens who have been affected by this immense disaster. this demonstrates the need to increase the capacity for prevention, for example by better monitoring of wastewater infrastructure. the rapid and efficient disbursement of financial resources following the submission of reconstruction plans by the national and regional authorities is also required. in this context, the solidarity fund, the eu's main instrument for solidarity, and whose success is beyond doubt, will play a key role. i strongly support any measure aimed at improving the mobilisation and implementation of the fund, such as the minimisation of red tape. the aim must be a more flexible and simplified fund, which will be easily and quickly mobilised and will enable the union to respond better and more effectively to major disasters, providing timely relief to citizens in need. this must be the essence of the solidarity which our union offers its citizens.
in writing. this own-initiative report examines the results of the high level expert group (hleg), set up by the commission in early 2012. although no new action is proposed - and this is not the purpose of the report - the hleg has identified some important issues with regard to the structure of the eu banking sector. for example, concrete examples and empirical evidence suggest that the financial crisis was caused by a combination of excessive risk-taking, excessive leverage, insufficient capital and liquidity requirements and excessive complexity of the banking system. the report, which i voted for, identifies security gaps and goes one step further, proposing to limit their connection with high-risk trading and to limit the bailout of banks with taxpayers' money. the hleg report therefore renews and strengthens our argument for banking reform at european level.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, which demonstrates the importance of protecting the eu's financial interests. i agree with the need to combat fraud in all areas of european budgetary expenditure. i would like to stress in particular the reduction in irregularities reported as fraud compared with the previous year in the fields of agriculture and cohesion policy. however, further simplification of the rules on public procurement and the management of the structural funds is deemed necessary in order to further reduce the risk of error. i also support tackling tax evasion and tax avoidance, particularly in view of the efforts to streamline the budget both at national and european level. in this context, i consider it necessary to improve the collection of vat, to address shortcomings in customs supervision and to combat cigarette smuggling. in addition, i believe that in order to improve the efficiency of olaf, which plays an important role in safeguarding the eu's financial interests, the relations between olaf and the supervisory committee need to be further improved. finally, i welcome the european commission's actions in the field of combating fraud and in particular the higher levels of cooperation with third countries in this field as well as in the field of recovery of pre-accession funds.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report as i agree that the european commission should make further efforts to secure a positive statement of assurance from the court of auditors as well as support measures to improve the quality of financial management, which is becoming particularly important at this time, given the efforts to streamline the budget both at national and european level. we must not forget that, despite the commission's responsibility for implementing the european budget, the member states spend the bulk of it, so they are also responsible. in this context, the availability of reliable statistical data and in particular the simplification, improvement of the control and surveillance systems of the member states and the redesign of their programmes or systems of implementation, in particular in the context of the legislative proposals and programmes for the period after 2013, are considered necessary in order to ensure a more efficient management of community resources, to enhance their added value and to achieve growth. finally, i support the performance approach through the establishment of a performance-based budget, which, combined with the implementation of compliance checks, should help to reduce the costs of eu policies and maximise their benefits.
president-in-office of the council. − madam president, the presidency is fully aware of the concerns which have been publicly expressed by various members of this house regarding the recent decision by the greek government to close the greek national broadcasting company. you have requested that the council participate in this debate, and the lithuanian presidency, representing the council, is ready to do so. however, the council has not discussed the issue which is the subject of this afternoon’s debate nor has it agreed any position on it. since i speak on behalf of the council, it follows that i am not in a position to make a substantive contribution to your discussions. indeed i would go further and say that it would not be appropriate for the representative of the council to take a view on an issue which is entirely an internal matter for the greek government and the citizens whom it represents. furthermore, this is a complex issue touching at the heart of national politics in greece. events have been developing rapidly over the last few days. there is very little clarity as to the current situation and even less as to the reasons for some of the decisions which have been taken. i am sure that you will understand why i cannot say more on this issue now. i speak as a presidency of the council and therefore in the name of all 28 member states. i will, however, follow this debate very closely and attentively. i can assure you that i will pay particular attention to the range of views and opinions which will doubtless be expressed here this afternoon.
vice-president of the commission. − madam president, the commission is well aware of the debate around the closure of the hellenic broadcasting corporation (the ert) which was announced by the greek authorities three weeks ago. let me clarify upfront that this is a decision which was taken in full autonomy by the greek authorities. the commission has already stated that, of course, it supports the role of public broadcasting as an integral part of european democracy. the commission seeks, within its areas of competence, to ensure respect for media pluralism, as it is a fundamental value enshrined in the charter of fundamental rights and a pillar of democratic society. the commission will continue its assessment of the situation with regard to media pluralism in all member states. hence, while it cannot prescribe to member states how to organise their public service broadcasters, it attaches special importance to the role of the dual system of public service and commercial service broadcasters in preserving media pluralism and promoting european values in all economic circumstances. greece, like all european countries, needs media pluralism, media freedom and media quality. we therefore welcome the commitment of the authorities to relaunch a public broadcaster that fulfils its role in terms of media pluralism, media freedom and the expression of cultural diversity. it is obvious to all observers, however, that the new broadcaster must be more efficient and financially sustainable than its predecessor. this case must be seen in the broader context of the rationalisation of greek public administration – which is crucial. greece needs a more efficient and effective public administration that serves the general interest and contributes to the recovery of the economy. in conclusion, i would like to emphasise that we fully support the significant efforts by the greek authorities to modernise the public sector, even though we are all aware that this implies very difficult decisions.

mr president, since yesterday we have all been following the news on the situation in egypt. this is not the point of my intervention: i know that we all wish violence to be avoided and the rights of the egyptian people to be fully respected. i would like to draw your attention to a dimension that, i believe, requires our full and direct involvement. according to the ngo human rights watch, during the last four days alone almost 100 women – protesters or journalists, locals or foreigners – have been raped or sexually assaulted in cairo. this is totally and completely intolerable. unfortunately, we do not have time for a proper debate or for a proper resolution, but i believe that as an institution and as a european union we have to be adamant. we have to make it absolutely clear that, no matter who is in charge in egypt, the eu will not establish proper and normal relations until we are sure that any kind of aggression against women is duly prosecuted and punished. (applause)
in writing. i voted in favour of the report, which is of interest to greece. the impact of the so-called austerity measures is particularly acute on vulnerable groups in particular. it is very important and we strongly support at european level the allocation of 20% of the european social fund funding both to the fight against poverty and to the creation of a new fund for the poor (with resources of eur 2 billion). the burden of the new financial instruments is proposed to be placed on the elderly and people with physical or learning disabilities, children and young people or other people with special needs, who are increasingly under pressure from the economic crisis. particular reference is also made to the issue of social responsibility in relation to universal access to healthcare, as the cost of participating in medical costs prevents early access to care, with the result that delay leads to more expensive and urgent care services in the future. similar weight is given to the child's development, health and educational opportunities. the european parliament hopes and contributes to the new multiannual financial framework of the eu (2014-2020) being characterised by a social dimension, responding as much as possible to the challenges posed by the economic crisis.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. the report recommends positive measures to improve health services in view of the ageing population, which requires the implementation of appropriate management. i also welcome the proposal to implement drastic measures, in the context of the fight against unemployment and the economic and financial crisis, to protect the young and the most vulnerable groups of the population.
in writing. the debate on hybrid tv is not just technical. it concerns pluralism and free expression, and therefore falls within the competence of the eu and competition law, since information is a very sensitive issue in the free market. hybrid television is a major technological step forward in the audiovisual media sector, as it receives and displays both traditional linear programming and material from the internet. the transition from conventional television to internet programming certainly requires technical adjustments. however, the increased cost of transition should not be at the expense of pluralism. the report, which i voted in favour of, aims to strike the right balance between market participants, in particular equipment manufacturers and content providers, in order to avoid giving individual content providers an unfair competitive advantage in the distribution of their content. it is also necessary to ensure that the equipment, platforms and gateways are created on the basis of an open and interoperable model, which is not based on ownership, which is also important for our country.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget no 1 for 2013, a purely technical amending budget concerning expenditure relating to the accession of the republic of croatia to the european union. croatia has already joined the european union on 1 july and is the 28th member state of our union. we must therefore ensure that the money associated with its accession is given to croatia so that it can assume the obligations that go with it and enjoy the rights that come with membership of the union directly. i would therefore like to express my regret that it was possible to reach agreement on draft amending budget no 1 for 2013 in the council at the very last moment, as several member states were not prepared to honour their legal obligations and provide fresh money, as required by the treaty for the accession of any new member.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the amendment of the interinstitutional agreement of 17 may 2006 on budgetary discipline and sound financial management relating to the mff to take account of the expenditure needs arising from the accession of croatia to the eu. although, whenever a new member state accedes, the appropriations for new expenditure are covered by increasing the mff ceilings, the council refused to do so and proposed that the commitment appropriations be reallocated to the various categories of the financial framework. parliament has repeatedly argued that the financing of croatian accession should be done by adjusting the mff, i.e. with fresh money, in order to send a strong message of welcome to croatia. the council's decision not only runs counter to the spirit of the unanimous decision taken at the signing of the accession treaty and the interinstitutional agreement, but it sets a dangerous precedent and sends the wrong political message, not only to croatia but also to the other candidate countries. however, the political importance and the legal urgency of securing funding for croatia force us to accept this position.
in writing. i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution. it is important that the european parliament expresses its position on the important events taking place in egypt. egypt is a key country for developments in the wider region. the aim is to stabilize the situation and de-escalate the violence.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, as we speak, big companies are buying people’s land throughout africa, buying the land where people cultivate the food that they need to eat. they are buying all this land thanks to corruption and weak governments. these companies pay back 3%, or even less, in taxes – that is nothing – and the result is famine and poverty. at the same time, the hike in prices caused by the use of food crops for the production of biofuels is leading to poverty, famine, unrest and emigration throughout the poorest parts of the world. at the same time, beautiful pristine forests – so necessary to the earth – are being chopped down so that the land may be used, illegally or legally, for the cultivation of these energy crops, leading to more emissions. what is the link between poverty, farming and increased emissions? the link is that the process is largely fuelled by our european policy, by more than eur 6 billion in indirect and direct subsidies from our european policy on biofuels. we made a mistake and we have to correct it. that is why the socialist group is supporting the 5.5% cap, supporting a 2.5% target for second generation biofuels and supporting the indirect land-use change (iluc) factor being taken into account from 2018 onwards in relation to both directives.
mr president, september is a month of starting and going back to school. however, fourteen-year-old hannah from england, fifteen-year-old bart from poland and a fourteen-year-old student from rome will not be returning to their school this year. all three committed suicide last august. three more tragic victims of cyberbullying. more and more children in europe are being trapped in the networks of the sick phenomenon of bullying. its effects are poisoning the lives of children, not only today, but also in their adult lives. offline or online, bullying and violence kill not only dreams and aspirations but children themselves. we must therefore do much more than we are doing today. parents, students, teachers, civil society are calling for greater vigilance, more information, more effective action. the european parliament has therefore supported and supports the establishment of a european day against bullying, calling on the commission to take more decisive and effective action.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it strengthens the existing legal framework for the conservation of fishery resources through technical measures to protect emerging marine organisms.
in writing. the protection of the rights of citizens governed by registered partnership agreements is a particular concern of community legislation. today, the number of registered partnerships in the eu is well over 200,000, which is why the regulatory framework, which concerns property relations, the applicable law and international jurisdiction, is of concern to an ever-increasing number of our fellow citizens. there are, in fact, enormous differences between national laws, so that the better informed partner (if the contract is concluded between nationals of different member states) may be interested in having recourse as soon as possible to a court which applies a law favourable to him. the present report, which i voted in favour of, is intended to alleviate these problems, given that the mobility of citizens in europe is increasing and, therefore, the number of stakeholders is widening. adopting a common approach to the problems of marriage and civil partnerships, the new framework proposes a system whereby registered partners will be able to choose the applicable law governing their property relations.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. there are enormous differences between national laws, which are much wider in the case of registered partners than in the case of marriages. the rapporteur rightly recognises that there is a need to take measures to improve the legal position of the couples concerned. the proposals in its report make a positive contribution to this.
in writing. one in seven marriages in the eu involves citizens of more than one member state. therefore, issues arise regarding the property relations of the spouses and in the context of the management of property during a marriage. i voted in favour of this report because the rapporteur recognises that the legal situation needs and can be significantly improved for the spouses concerned and the introduction of 'simpler, clearer and more accessible procedures' in the area of civil law is a positive measure.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it suggests reducing energy consumption, traffic noise, air pollutants, greenhouse gas emissions, accidents and land use, thereby reducing the impact of transport on climate change and helping to improve the health and quality of life of eu citizens.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report which is the result of the effort to ensure stronger protection of the rights of victims of crime and of suspects and accused persons. these rights concern access to translation and interpretation, legal advice and legal aid, communication with relatives, employers, consular authorities and others. this proposal establishes the basic principle that every suspect or accused person should have access to a lawyer as soon as possible and in a way that allows him or her to exercise effectively his or her right of defence. the report aims to ensure that appeals are effective. the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs has repeatedly called for the strengthening of the procedural rights of suspects and accused persons, but this requires the mutual recognition of judicial decisions in criminal proceedings between member states, so that citizens can be sure that, during their movements, their rights are fully respected regardless of which member state they are in. however, this point, although it should be constantly emphasized, is beyond the aims and purposes of this report.
in writing. i voted against this proposal because it establishes the basic principle that every suspect or accused person should have access to a lawyer as soon as possible and in a way that allows him to exercise his right of defence effectively. furthermore, it is clarified that, in any event, those rights should be granted before the opening of the investigation procedure, during the performance of any procedural act or act of taking evidence, as well as in the case of deprivation of liberty. the proposal further defines the content of the right, stresses that meetings between the suspect or accused and the lawyer should be confidential, lists the derogations allowed from the general principles and provides for rules in case of waiver of this right.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it aims to strengthen and update the measures to combat market abuse and insider dealing.
in writing. the adoption of the new common fisheries policy requires adjustments to other relevant fisheries regulations in the various sea basins. i voted in favour of mr gróbarczyk's report because it calls on the commission to propose a new, substantially amended draft regulation on the use of technical measures in the baltic sea, adapted to the new eu common fisheries policy.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it stresses that the development of indigenous energy sources will lead to the emergence of new trading hubs in the eu and new markets for the direct distribution of gas and electricity, which presents a real opportunity for the eu and the member states to set their energy prices, inter alia, at regional and local level. it also recognises the european added value of better coordination and cooperation between member states' energy policies, in a spirit of solidarity, and the creation of efficient and secure cross-border energy systems, so as to create synergies through improved management of energy supply and demand, facilitated by smart technologies at the distribution system level.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the eu faces serious challenges in achieving the target of reducing overall energy consumption by 20% by 2020, and in this context, policies to promote energy efficiency could and should play a more important role. improvements in energy efficiency are crucial for european competitiveness and are a win-win option in the fight against climate change. investments in energy efficiency measures can bring immediate and tangible benefits to households and businesses, such as reducing costs, creating jobs and growth opportunities, but can also encourage the transition to a low-carbon economy.
in writing. i voted in favour of this excellent report which aims to contribute to the debate on the importance of energy efficiency by assessing the results of cohesion policy measures targeting this area and presenting a series of recommendations to enhance the impact of the measures in the next programming period. the report also attempts to clarify whether the objectives of cohesion policy at european, national and regional level are compatible with energy efficiency objectives. it is a fact that cohesion policy is one of the most important sources of support for meeting the eu's energy efficiency targets. the commission has proposed, for the next programming period, to allocate almost twice as much of the budget for this policy to climate-related expenditure, including energy efficiency. it is also important that in view of the new programming period ending in 2020, all necessary measures should be taken to ensure that the eu 2020 targets are met and that cohesion policy measures on energy efficiency have a significant potential to meet those targets.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report as it aims to promote the development of a new community strategy in the field of fisheries and aquaculture in the adriatic and ionian sea basin, in order to make a substantial contribution to the development of sustainable activities with a positive impact on coastal communities and the respective inland areas.
in writing. the european union is the only organisation in the world with an official multilingualism policy with 23 official languages, soon to be 24. i voted in favour of this report because the measures it proposes are considered positive in terms of improving the right to multilingualism and making more efficient use of available resources based on the real needs of users.
in writing. i voted in favour of the s&d alternative resolution which addresses issues such as the general framework for interpretation applicable in the ep, the recent efficiency gains partly derived from the agreement on the 2012 ep budget, which required cuts of €10 million in the budget for interpretation, and the measures to be taken in the future, notably through the implementation of 'interpretation on demand', the reduction of late cancellations and the updating of the 2005 court of auditors report on interpretation expenditure. in the general context of budgetary constraints at national and european level, improvements in efficiency in the field of interpretation have succeeded in generating considerable savings in parliament's budget. however, these cuts should in no way compromise the quality of interpretation and affect the right of members to use the language of their choice. finally, it should be stressed that live webcasting of parliament's meetings is an important part of transparency and citizens' contact with our institution. this is why a high level of interpretation in all the official languages of the eu is essential.
in writing. the committee on culture and education, in its opinion on this report, in which we tabled proposals to this effect, stresses the need to protect minors by applying, in all member states, strict and reliable standards for verifying age and identity, and believes that effective prevention and protection measures should be imposed for vulnerable consumers, such as the application of strict deposit limits and limits on loss of profits that can be set by the player himself. in addition, an adequate framework for the gambling market requires the existence of national regulatory bodies responsible for licensing gambling and helping to combat both illegal gambling activities and corruption in sport. the final text of the report largely satisfies our proposals and i therefore voted in favour of the own-initiative report.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because it represents a step towards ensuring that consumers have access to safe and properly regulated online services. i also believe that effective regulation of the online gambling sector should, in particular, exclude risks of gambling addiction and ensure that gambling is appropriate, fair, reliable and transparent.
in writing. draft amending budget (dab) no 2 of 2013 concerns the increase of payment appropriations to allow the commission to pay the outstanding 2012 accounts in all policy areas of the eu budget, in particular under the structural funds and the cohesion fund, which could not be paid on time due to the lack of sufficient payment appropriations in the 2012 budget. in the context of the negotiations on the mff 2014-2020, the presidents of the european parliament, the commission and the council agreed that the council should release the first instalment of the mff 2/2013 of eur 7,3 billion and that the commission should submit an additional mff for 2013 in early autumn to avoid any shortfall in payments by the end of the year. the european parliament has repeatedly made it clear that it will not allow a structural deficit in the eu budget at the end of 2013 to be carried over and burden the new financial period. it therefore awaits the commission's proposal for a new mff and recalls the council's political commitment to decide on this new amending budget without delay.
the rapporteur. - the mr president, i would first of all like to thank my shadow rapporteurs, my colleagues from the committee on employment and social affairs and the committee on regional development for their opinions, and the council and the commission for all the cooperation we have had, which ultimately led to the unanimous adoption of this report by the committee on culture and education last june. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is the europeans who have reaped the greatest share of prosperity and development, who have seen europe transformed after the wars from a geographical space into a political entity, who made may '68, who demanded that power be clothed in imagination, who have known the most effective welfare state in world history. they are the ones who are now at the helm of political, economic, social and productive developments in europe. so how do all these people imagine the future for their children and grandchildren? how do they pass on to future generations the unprecedented prosperity they have experienced? how do they feel when they see the ghosts of extremism, which they themselves had only known as bad memories of their parents, haunting europe again? the answer, of course, for the elderly is that we need a comprehensive european strategy for our young people today. and indeed, for the first time, immediately after the treaty of lisbon, we adopted a strategy for young people at european level, and now, some three years later, we are called upon to evaluate the first cycle of this strategy. it is true that the european union's powers in youth matters remain limited. but that doesn't mean they're not critical. it's not! the european union today has a number of important instruments to support young people. it is not just the targeted programmes we all know about for mobility and training, but also the initiatives that are being taken. i refer to the debate which took place at the extraordinary council last summer on the problems of our young people. i refer to the statements made by the president of the commission last time on the 'opportunities for young people' initiative. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. we all realise that the results are still not satisfactory. the european union must intervene decisively, for example, when its structural funds for education, employment and training are only partially used. the council and the commission must monitor more effectively the facilitation of the transfer of resources towards the empowerment of young people. we must stand up and resist when, while calling for greater spending and emphasis on education, innovation and youth entrepreneurship, we have often seen in recent years serious budget cuts in this area by a significant number of member states. we must reverse the current reality that excessive unemployment in some member states is forcing young people to emigrate, leading to the loss of a country's most productive potential in what has become known as 'brain drain'. the fact that several billion of the community funds for the period up to 2013 have remained unused to date cannot satisfy us. the commission's proposal for a directive on the coordination of certain provisions laid down by law, regulation or administrative action in member states concerning the taking-up and pursuit of the business of credit institutions and investment firms is a good example of this. it is time to discuss even bolder policies, such as tax breaks and incentives for employers to encourage young people to enter the labour market. we must consider the possibility of using european resources to ensure that the unemployed can travel free of charge on public transport and fight against red tape so that it does not even exist as a concept when a young person is trying to realize his business idea. it is good that the member states have started and are implementing our new tool: the 'youth guarantees'. but that's not enough. in our reports we have tried to record parliament's views on what is lacking, what should be done with specific proposals, and i invite you to study them all and try to implement them. we keep track of where we're lagging, what we're doing right and what we're not. in our view, the key principles for the next period, the titles of this strategy, can be summed up in the words: education, employment, innovation, entrepreneurship, finance, exploitation of new technologies, participation. we believe that the role of our parliament should be enhanced in this debate. the implementation of the youth strategy cannot be discussed every three years or every two years when the time comes for the evaluation. the situation is changing so fast that we simply cannot afford it. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. and we must certainly include, even more actively, all those who, along with the fight for work, for education, for progress, are also fighting for equal treatment, for independent living. i would like to point out that the 'freedom drive' conference taking place in strasbourg these days is attended by many children, including a greek delegation of 24 members. youth who give us courage and strength, who remind us that opportunity is for everyone, without exception. the problems of young people in one country are problems for the whole of europe. it is not just a matter of deepening and consolidating, it is primarily a matter of perspective and vision. no corner of europe can feel comfortable about our common future when the spanish youth has lost faith in tomorrow or when six out of ten greek young people lack the courage to dream. it is true that today imagination has been lost, europe is following the developments without having succeeded so far, despite the efforts, in reversing the situation. and a dynamic youth strategy is the weapon to turn this around. the dynamic application and implementation of this is the imagination that is lacking today.
madam president, i think that the work done by mr papanicolaou and mrs skrzydlewska is excellent. but i'm afraid we're having a discussion of good intentions and nice words for the young. at a time when the number of young unemployed in europe exceeds 14 million and the rate in greece and spain is over 55%, we are going to offer a grant of 8 million euros. it's about charity, and we don't solve the problem with charity. the problem will be solved by work, and to open up work, madam president, we must change policy. we must have a policy of investment in europe, not a policy of contraction. "give money to the men and women of europe!" we must realize that and we cannot come here and make fun of ourselves with nice speeches for the young. the young people of europe do not need the 8 million, they give it back to you. the young people of europe are asking for jobs, and we are not giving them jobs when we do not help businesses to grow and when we do nothing to be able to re-establish this ageing population of europe which will be its big problem in 2030, madam president, and ladies and gentlemen. we're 8% of the population today, 30% of global gdp, and we'll be 4% in 2030, and we don't know how much less of global gdp. we're getting poorer, and instead of facing our poverty, we're making wishes. i'm really sorry about that.
madam president, we are having another debate on youth unemployment. many words, many positive plans, many thoughts, but in practice nothing. the only tool we have at the moment is the famous youth guarantee. but as my colleagues have rightly said, the youth guarantee is a key that opens a door. the point is that youth should not be left alone at the door or risk going out again. we want specific legislative proposals, we want specific funds and we want commitments but, in fact, to be honest with each other, all of us in this room - and especially to be honest with young people looking them in the eye - we know that the answer to unemployment is one word: is the word "growth". if there is no growth, no investment, no jobs, unemployment will be presented as a monster that will frighten the young generation and europe.
the rapporteur. - the mr president, i thank all my colleagues for their excellent speeches and for their participation in today's debate. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the future of europe is the future of the young, there is no doubt about that. i would like to thank the commission in particular for putting commissioner andor at the beginning and end of the debate, and i would ask that in the next debate on such matters we have commissioners for other areas than employment. it is clear that youth issues affect all political horizons. youth policy is not a vertical policy in isolation. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we have not had the representatives of the member states to explain to us what has gone wrong and we have not had better results so far and why we have not been able to make the best use of all these tools: all these communications that are being made, the funding that is not enough - but there is funding - and the european programmes. i am particularly pleased to see many young people in our theatres today, mr president. i would like to highlight the presence of 11 young people with severe disabilities who have come from greece, facing many difficulties, but who have managed to get here and inspire us, give us the courage to continue. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's citizens are not excluded from the european union's citizenship, and that the european union is not forced to take action against the citizens of the union. europe and the opportunities we want to create are not just a right for young people, they are a duty and an obligation for all of us.
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. madam president, i would like to thank my colleague, mrs corazza bildt, for her work. however, there are some points to be noted. the first point is that we have worked well and made very hard efforts to be able to find a common ground. we've succeeded to a point. the result was that the socialists made their contribution in four areas. in particular, it became clear that the reference to growth of up to 2,6% over a period of five to ten years is a very ambitious formulation with considerable difficulties in implementation, because it does not take account of the economic conditions prevailing at the moment. secondly, we have made it clear that we cannot speak of an interpretation of the directive that goes beyond the letter. we have stressed that, as regards social services, account should be taken of the specific circumstances of each member state. fourthly, we have balanced the excessive criticism of the report on national regulations. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work is a very good one. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations, and i would like to ask the commissioner to give us a brief overview of the progress made in the negotiations. it will be for the good not of parliament, not of europe, but of the citizens and workers of europe. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on wednesday 11 september 2013 at 12 noon. written declarations (rule 149) the president of the court of justice shall be informed of the decision of the court of first instance.
the next item is the debate on the report by constance le grip, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on the establishment of the union customs code (recast) (com (2012) 0064 - c7-0045/2012 - 2012/0027 (cod)) (a7-0006/2013).
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. madam president, this debate on the modernisation of the customs code is a very good moment for the european parliament. we have all worked very hard, but especially hard has been worked by mrs le grip, who has given us an excellent basic text, and especially by the chairman of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, mr harbour, who has conducted with great skill the tripartite dialogue which has resulted in this extremely balanced legislation which benefits the citizens of europe. it is very important that the citizens of europe understand that this very technical legislation is very beneficial to them. this is in the interests of business, and in a time of crisis, when we must promote and protect it in the best possible way. in this time of crisis, this legislation is benefiting the public, especially now that the trafficking of dangerous materials is extremely difficult to detect. we have also ensured that there are no inequalities in this legislation. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the customs authorities of the member states are able to take account of the needs of the community and its citizens. but the countries which have already made progress can also continue their way and create good practices, good data, which other countries can imitate. i think it is a really great moment for parliament and it will help to achieve what we all say and believe is needed, the establishment of the single market.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on wednesday 11 september 2013 at 12 noon.
in writing. the proposal on the conditions determining the preferential origin of products, on the basis of which the preferential tariff measures are established, is extremely positive. it states that in order to be eligible for preferential importation into the eu, a product must either have been produced entirely in the country of origin or have undergone significant processing or working resulting in the manufacture of a new product. it also expressly incorporates in the basic legislative act the criteria for determining a product as having preferential origin. the proposal is an important step towards eliminating unfair competition, inter alia, between fresh agricultural products from third countries and their eu counterparts. i would remind you that fresh agricultural products from third countries, with low production costs, were imported into the eu and after a minimal treatment that did not alter the characteristics or the specific texture of the product, were placed on the european market as eu products.
in writing. today's speech by the president of the european commission, expresses the determination of the monopolies and their political staff to continue with the same vigour their anti-people attack against workers, in all eu member states. it is true, as the president of the commission has said, that in recent years the eu, together with the european parliament and the civil governments of the member states, has achieved a great deal. i did. they have supported and secured the profitability of capital by drastically reducing the price of labour in all eu member states. the commission president referred to these "successes" for the capital when he congratulated the nd-pasok capital co-government (as well as the previous ones) on the "impressive reforms" they had carried out in greece "over the last three years". behind the propaganda injections of optimism ahead of the european elections, there is the concern of the political staff of the monopolies about the bankruptcy of the anti-people eu policy in the popular consciousness. the peoples must reject the tragic present and the nightmarish future that the union of monopolies holds for them. their hope lies in the dismantling of the eu, the untying of the bonds of the wolf alliance, the building of the social alliance, to transfer power from the monopolies to the working class. "
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, my group in the conference of presidents had proposed that this matter be put to the vote after discussion. the matter, as you know, is very serious. as the author of the committee's opinion, i would point out that, according to our data, 85% of the reports relate to the issue under discussion, namely the adverse environmental impact of public and private projects. the committee on petitions has worked very seriously and would like to hear its views. but unfortunately the subject was raised without the debate. i therefore propose, madam president, ladies and gentlemen, that if there is to be a debate to hear the commission's opinion and to enable parliament to take a responsible position on very critical matters, on which i know full well that pressure is being exerted in one direction or another, i propose that the matter be discussed in the october partsession by debate and vote, provided that there is a substantive debate. i've given you the reasons.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because i believe that every language, including endangered languages, reflects a historical, social and cultural knowledge, abilities and a way of thinking and creating that are part of the richness and diversity of the european union and the basis of european identity. therefore, linguistic diversity and the presence of endangered languages in a country should be considered an asset rather than a burden and as such should be supported and promoted rather than as a tool for promoting political, ethnic or territorial aspirations.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report as it strengthens the role of the european parliament in the recruitment process for managers in the european environment agency. it is rightly proposed that the procedure for appointing the eea executive director should be aligned with the procedures applicable to the executive directors of other bodies falling within the remit of the envi committee.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget (dab) no 2 of 2013 to increase payment appropriations to enable the commission to pay the outstanding 2012 accounts in all policy areas of the eu budget, in particular under the structural funds and the cohesion fund, which could not be paid on time due to the lack of sufficient payment appropriations in the 2012 budget. after several rounds of intensive negotiations, following the meeting of the presidents of the three institutions, the council agreed to release the first tranche of the 2013 mff 2 of eur 7,3 billion and to submit an additional mff for 2013 by the commission in early autumn to avoid any shortfall in payments by the end of the year. we, as the european parliament, have made it clear that the eu should not end up with a structural deficit in its budget at the end of 2013, which will be carried over to the new financial period. we therefore await the commission's proposal for a new cfsp and recall that the council has given a political commitment to decide on this new corrective budget without delay.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget (dab) no 3 of 2013 concerning the entry in the 2013 eu budget of the surplus resulting from the implementation of the budget for the financial year 2012. budget execution for 2012 showed a historically low surplus of eur 1,023 billion, which is therefore entered as revenue in the 2013 budget and will correspondingly reduce the overall contribution of the member states to the financing of the eu budget. i can only emphasize the paradox of the matter, namely that on the one hand there is a payment deficit in the eu budget and the commission is having difficulty paying its bills and, on the other hand, any surplus which has arisen from the implementation of last year's budget, instead of going to the budget financing to cover the deficit, is being returned to the member states. however, the european parliament accepts this approach as the adoption of fp3/2013 will reduce the share of the member states' gni contribution to the eu budget and partially offset their contribution to the financing of fp2/2013.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report and welcome the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund for floods due to the heavy rains which in autumn 2012 caused damage to buildings, water and sewerage infrastructure, businesses, agricultural land and forests. the authorities of slovenia, croatia and austria estimate that the total direct losses amount to eur 359,535 million for slovenia, eur 11,463 million for croatia and eur 9,6 million for austria. the priority now is to restore as soon as possible normal living conditions for the people and to restore economic activity in the affected areas. the solidarity fund has once again demonstrated its importance in responding to major natural disasters by providing financial assistance to the affected states. however, i cannot help but regret that it took almost a year to complete the process of mobilising the fund. the three institutions should therefore work to make the fund's interventions faster and more flexible. this will ensure that the real added value of the instrument is maintained.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget (dab) no 5/2013 to mobilise the eu solidarity fund in response to the heavy rainfall and the ensuing disasters caused by the floods that hit slovenia, croatia and austria in autumn 2012. it is positive that the council has confirmed the commission's proposal without amendments, thus ensuring that fp5 will be financed with fresh money. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the budget and the implementation of the budgetary discipline and the budgetary discipline of the union. finally, it is important to stress the need for the timely release of financial assistance through the solidarity fund to the countries affected by this natural disaster. it is therefore regrettable that the council has once again decided not to limit, for reasons of urgency, the eight weeks for informing the national parliaments before adopting its position on this amending budget, as provided for in article 4 of protocol 1 to the treaty.
in writing. the report and the directive, which are designed to be environmentally friendly, are once again masking the competition between the eu monopolies for their share of the eu's energy pie and the related subsidies from the community funds. the eu sees the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions as another opportunity for 'green' business to tap capital overhangs in areas where it sees a comparative advantage over its competitors. the report and the amendments concern the parts of the energy sector that will get a bigger piece of the pie, and those that will limit the loss of profits from the policy change. the compromise of the report, which maintains the production of biofuels by reducing their share, does not solve the problem but, on the contrary, perpetuates it, it is a temporary balance between monopoly shares with no connection to the people's interests and the protection of the environment. the question of the two paths of development and power is also raised here, so that proposals which are generally sound from a scientific and technical point of view, for example, for the production of zero-emission biofuels or the improvement of energy efficiency or the use of renewable sources in the whole energy mix, acquire a people-friendly content and do not constitute a means of increasing monopoly profitability at the expense of the people and the environment.
in writing. the present report, which i voted in favour of, focuses on achieving the pre-defined european environmental protection targets and, of course, on increasing the share of renewable energy sources in total fuel demand and consumption in the eu. this debate is of interest to greece given the country's investments, especially in solar energy. it is noted that the renewable energy directive sets the target of increasing the share of renewable energy in total eu energy consumption to 20% by 2020, and the corresponding share of energy in transport to 10%, targets which greece appears to be meeting. however, this report also focuses on second generation renewable energy sources, and in particular biofuels, proposing solutions to avoid repeating the mistakes made with first generation renewable sources, such as issues related to the use of these forms of energy. for example, the use of forest biomass requires particular attention as scientific studies currently question the greenhouse gas emission reductions achieved by the use of wood for energy.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as i agree with the recasting of the eu customs code (ccu). the customs union is an essential element for the functioning of the single market and the smooth functioning of the latter can only be ensured if the common modernised customs rules and systems are uniformly and consistently applied.
in writing. i voted against the proposal as it is an important tool for framing and evaluating rural policies, since data on income, inputs and outputs of a representative sample of eu agricultural enterprises are collected each year.
in writing. the report is a response to the commission's proposal to amend the wording of technical characteristics relating to product descriptions. the european parliament finds that this explicit delegation of power to amend characteristics affecting product descriptions, definitions or names goes beyond the scope of delegated acts under article 290 of the treaty, which limits the delegation of power only to supplementing or amending certain non-essential elements of a legislative act. the report therefore proposes that the commission's powers should be limited to certain directives (in particular to technical elements affecting product descriptions, definitions, names or sales descriptions), a position with which i agree and for which reason i voted in favour of the report.
in writing. the kke voted against the report 'implementation of the eu youth strategy 2010-2012' (a7-0238/2013) and the report 'combating youth unemployment: possible solutions' (a7-0275/2013) because they aim to manipulate young consciences to prevent social explosions from the accumulated anger generated by anti-people policy and to form a generation of modern slaves, without rights, in the grip of the most cruel exploitation. the reports propose measures to recycle unemployment along four lines: (a) false training and linking education to the market to serve the needs of the capital (b) hot money to companies for hiring young workers, with subsidies - exemptions from insurance contributions, wage subsidies, etc. (c) strengthening monopolies with a full-time workforce of young people, on hunger wages and without rights, with the so-called apprenticeship, training and volunteering programmes (d) roaming young people across the length and breadth of the eu, as cheap and flexible employables. it is proposed that a 'european volunteer corps' be set up of young people under 30 years of age, who will travel from one eu country to another to offer free work to businesses in the name of gaining experience!!
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because it seeks to highlight, propose and help find appropriate solutions to the issues which are the biggest challenges facing the young generation today. the main objective remains to strengthen the policies implemented by the member states in order to achieve, together with their objectives, the best possible results. in addition, the report highlights issues not covered by the current eu youth strategy. for example, the role of new technologies and social media can neither be ignored nor marginalized, because their dominance in every aspect of young people's lives (from employment and participation in society to entertainment and expression) is increasing at an alarming rate today. in addition, the impact of the crisis on young people should be tackled in the most effective way, as youth unemployment has risen to unacceptable levels, particularly in the south of the eu: the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the number of cases of non-compliance with the requirements of the public procurement rules.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as actions to combat youth unemployment should be aligned with those to boost the general economic recovery and reforms in employment policy. initiatives are needed to support entrepreneurship, to maintain as many of the jobs already in existence as possible, and actions should be taken to encourage entrepreneurs to create new jobs. the real improvement of the situation of young people depends to a large extent on state support, on strengthening cooperation between public employment services, providers of vocational guidance services, educational institutions and youth support services, together with the active involvement of the social partners and youth representatives or youth organisations.
mr president, on 21 august a crime against humanity was committed before our eyes: the use of chemical weapons against civilians, an act which - wherever it originated - constitutes a flagrant violation of international law. the crisis in syria goes far beyond the level of a local conflict, with the greatest instability at stake in an already troubled region like the middle east. is there a responsibility of the international community towards what is happening in syria? i'm sure you do. but first, there's no clear evidence as to who carried out the attack. secondly, there is no clear military target for a possible intervention. thirdly, there is no clear ally for the forces wishing to intervene. the opposition is divided and infiltrated by al qaeda and other extremist islamic organizations. fourthly, an intervention would create a new wave of refugees in addition to the two million who have already fled the country. and fifthly, there is no legal basis because we have neither a security council resolution nor a regional organization. it is no coincidence that european public opinion is against a military attack on syria. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. the russian initiative is precisely the basis for all the parties concerned and the permanent members of the security council to work towards the convening of the geneva ii conference. the european union must also take the necessary steps to ensure that the situation of refugees and internally displaced persons is not exacerbated by the current situation, which is uncontrollable and which must be addressed as a matter of priority. humanitarian corridors must be established immediately. finally, at european level, there must be a plan for the distribution of syrian refugees coming to europe so that the countries bordering the union are not alone in managing the migration flows.

the rapporteur. - mr president, i am very pleased to have you back and i hope you will not have another accident. i feel obliged to commend our ombudsman, mr diamantoure, who has represented and served the institution well for the last ten years; the european union is a political union, an institution that unites european citizens and gives them a safe feeling of belonging. i would like to the institution, and i note that it is even greater than last year. i think it is important to mention some points in the report which highlight the progress that has been made. in particular, 2012 was a particularly productive year as the number of investigations carried out and completed exceeded any previous year. the total number of investigations initiated was 18% higher than in the corresponding period of 2011. the number of investigations carried out increased by 23% compared with the previous year. as in 2011, the ombudsman directly served more than 22 000 citizens, examining their reports, responding to their requests and providing advice through the interactive guide on the website. thanks to the useful advice provided by the guide, the number of cases outside the ombudsman's remit was the lowest in the last decade. the commission has also received a number of complaints from the member states concerning the use of the 'consumer protection' clause in the regulation. most of the investigations launched in 2012 concerned alleged forms of maladministration in matters of legality, access to data and information, fair treatment and adherence to reasonable time limits in decision-making. among other things, the ombudsman's competence on the rights of persons with disabilities was recognised in the council decision which ratified the relevant framework of the european union under the united nations convention on the rights of persons with disabilities. the ombudsman works with four other bodies, including the european parliament's committee on petitions, to protect, promote and monitor the implementation of the convention. ladies and gentlemen, i warmly congratulate the new ombudsman, mrs o'reilly, on her election and wish her all the best vice-president of the commission. − mr president, it is now 24 years that i have been sitting in this house. ten years as an mep and 14 years as a commissioner, and let me tell you that i have never seen this before: that on all the benches there is an absolute agreement to applaud the work done by the ombudsman and his team. that is extraordinary, just as your work was extraordinary. the rapporteur. - the the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european parliament wishes to give the outgoing ombudsman its full support and full recognition of his work. it should be understood that the institution of the ombudsman is a judicial institution, not a judicial one. and because it is a judicial institution it is even more difficult, because the ombudsman's decisions are not binding. court decisions are binding, they are enforced. the ombudsman's decisions are rendered with a justice that is respected because of the person's status and the correctness of the decisions. and we've had success with that over the last decade. the european citizens, who are the users of this institution, seek a good social and economic standard of living. and i believe that there is no right living if there is no justice. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. i fear that when pretexts are born or pretexts are created for governmental arbitrariness, something is wrong. and the economic crisis has created a lot of pretexts for governmental arbitrariness at all levels of government, i believe, not just in europe but around the world. we have to be careful about that. the demand for justice from the citizens of europe is more intense because today, unfortunately, basic human rights are being violated. the commission's role is also important here. in conclusion - and i do not want to go on - i would also like to thank all the political groups for their support for my report.
in writing. the conditions to be fulfilled in order for third-country nationals to be exempted from the visa requirement for short stays in the territory of the european union are defined in accordance with the current regulation 539/2001. one of these is to facilitate the acquisition of visas for third-country nationals who are facing emergency situations in their countries. however, in the course of the application of the regulation, it was observed that many third-country nationals, taking advantage of the visa waiver, travel to the eu and seek international protection with insufficient justification. the report, which i voted in favour of, provides for the introduction of the so-called safeguard clause and the reciprocity mechanism, measures whose activation may affect the european union's international relations. however, the debate remains open as to how strict or not the criteria for their activation will be, with greece clearly interested in a coherent approach to more effectively discourage illegal migration flows to our country.
in writing. the kke votes against the two reports on the establishment of a single supervisory mechanism for banks within the european banking union, because their sole objective is to strengthen financial capital at the expense of the people's interests. the banking union aims to be a centralised eu mechanism for controlled capital destruction, controlling the processes of capital accumulation and concentration in the sector, with the aim of further strengthening and strengthening large financial groups. the eu seeks to have direct control and intervention in banks and member states and to coordinate centrally the decisions and procedures for their bankruptcy, bank takeovers and mergers, even looting the savings of the working-class family. the extent and depth of the disaster are the subject of intense confrontations between the bourgeois classes of the member states - which monopoly groups will lose and which will be strengthened - which will come back to the fore even after any temporary compromises. what is certain is that the eib will burden the working class and the popular strata who will pay, either by looting their life savings, or by tax-avoiding and anti-people measures, bank bailouts and controlled bankruptcies.

the rapporteur. - the madam president, commissioner, i should first like to thank the shadow rapporteurs and all the colleagues who worked on this report and, thanks to the good cooperation we had, we were able to agree on a coherent text which was voted unanimously by the committee on fisheries. a large part of the mediterranean's sea surface is now outside the jurisdiction or sovereignty of coastal states. it therefore remains largely unprotected in terms of living aquatic resources and their marine environment. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in the negotiations, but i am saying that it should be involved in the negotiations, and i am not saying that it should be involved in the negotiations. it is clear, of course, that the basis is existing legislation in the member states and the european union, international law and, in particular, the un convention on the law of the sea, unclos. of the 21 mediterranean states, only three have not signed and ratified the un convention on the law of the sea. the european union has a duty to help the palestinians. as this convention is part of international law on the management of waters and fisheries resources, the european union should use its diplomatic means to get these countries to sign the convention, especially those countries which are in the process of acceding to it. marine management, the protection of the marine environment and fisheries resources, and the control of illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing have been shown to be better protected when there is an institutional framework. in this way, my report encourages the creation of marine zones and, in particular, the declaration of exclusive economic zones, and calls on the commission to help in this area by providing guidance to the member states. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. the creation and definition of exclusive economic zones (eezs) is a matter for the member states alone, which will weigh their own interests in this area and take the relevant decisions. i thank the commissioner for her intervention and welcome the commission's efforts towards the creation of an integrated maritime policy within the framework of the blue growth strategy and the recent commission study which examines the costs and benefits of establishing maritime zones in the mediterranean and concludes that the establishment of maritime zones, including exclusive economic zones, would have positive effects on european blue growth strategies by attracting investment and further economic activity.

the committee on petitions' opinion was drawn up by mr glinne, rapporteur. - the the directive we are discussing is a key instrument for developing environmental policy. the committee on petitions has for years received a number of reports concerning the circumvention and infringement of this directive. the opinion i submitted to the committee on petitions on updating this directive was unanimously adopted, which means that the committee on petitions is sending the message that the directive should be strengthened to meet citizens' expectations. the rapporteur is moving in the direction of improving the commission's proposal. i do, however, consider the following points of amendment to be non-negotiable: public participation at all stages of projects, the submission of the whole of a project to an environmental impact assessment rather than parts of it, the strengthening of the assessment, the mandatory monitoring of the project from start to finish, the consideration of the negative effects on the environment in general and the development of environmental impacts in the case of natural gas and oil shale extraction. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in the member states. there have been many violations, and that was one reason for the fact that the committee on petitions visited her - as it did recently; however, i would point out that recently a new bill has been introduced which makes it even easier to carry out such interventions as aquaculture plants, sewage treatment plants, waste disposal plants, etc. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, this is indeed a very important directive for the european union. for many people, it represents the presence of the european union, the positive changes that it has brought to countries, at least as far as the environment and participation in development projects is concerned. it is the directive that really increases democratic participation in consultations for development projects, which is the only way people have, in many countries, to defend their rights, to protect the environment and public health as established in the constitutions and in legislation. it is the only way people have to express their views about upcoming projects. the commission proposal is reinforcing this directive. we in the socialist group are supportive of all elements of the commission proposal. furthermore, we ask for more social participation. we ask for more participation by citizens at all stages. i would remind you that two weeks ago we had a great debate here in parliament, on shale gas operations. we are asking for mandatory impact assessments on all shale gas operations, independently of the quantities of gas to be extracted, not only in the extraction phase but also in the research phase. why? because we all know that when all these liquids, all these chemicals fall into the subterranean aquifer, the damage is done, whether in the research phase or in the exploration, so we will be strongly supporting this idea in the vote. also, on air corridors, we believe that there should be an impact assessment when we construct an airport, at the construction phase, where the airplanes pass into the air streams, otherwise there are issues of noise and pollution which affect citizens.
in writing. in its 28 years of application, the directive has proved successful. we consider it necessary, however, to revise it, particularly as regards the procedure for checking the selection criteria on which decisions are based, the quality of the information provided, administrative simplification in order to establish a clear timetable for all the phases of the report and, finally, the extension of its application to include shale gas projects. our aim is to adopt a truly sustainable development model in all member states, harmonized by common rules, taking into account biodiversity, the rational use of natural resources, climate change and natural and man-made hazards. in order to be consistent and effective, we support the revision in this direction, believing that it will be an important step towards addressing the weaknesses identified in recent years and which have given rise to many legal disputes both at national level and at the level of the european court of justice.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for italy in respect of redundancies in the information and communication technology companies anovo italia s.p.a. and jabil cm s.r.l. the aid amounts to a total of eur 1 164 930. the ongoing economic and financial crisis in italy further highlights the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as one of the most important social policy tools available to the european union. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. the support provided is also a practical expression of the european union's solidarity.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for italy to support redundancies at the car manufacturer de tomaso automobili s.p.a., for aid amounting to a total of eur 2 594 672. in recent years, the italian government has made considerable efforts to streamline the budget and to drastically cut expenditure. the strengthening of the european globalisation adjustment fund, in addition to being a visible and practical expression of the european union's solidarity, makes a decisive contribution to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of the member states to consolidate their national budgets.
in writing. to be credible and to lead the fight against corruption internationally, the eu must tackle organised crime, corruption and money laundering both within and outside its borders. to contribute to improving transparency in countries where corruption permeates vertically and horizontally those institutions, such as political and judicial authorities, which should promote transparency and ensure respect for democracy. this report, which i voted in favour of, calls on the eu member states to amend their criminal laws to give jurisdiction to persons on their territory, of whatever nationality, who have committed bribery or embezzlement of public money, while at the same time calling on european companies operating in third countries to respect human rights and to establish codes of conduct that will clearly promote efforts to combat corruption effectively.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on long-term policy planning and long-term trends and the budgetary implications in terms of capacity development. it is of course important, both at national and european level, to develop long-term strategic planning in decision-making. with this in mind, the european parliament first proposed as a pilot project and is now continuing as a preparatory action the european strategy and policy analysis system (espas) with the aim of creating an interinstitutional system to identify long-term trends on the major policy issues facing the eu. the results achieved to date are of particular importance and the final results of the preparatory action to be completed in 2014 are awaited with great interest. it is important that closer cooperation between the research departments of the various eu institutions and agencies has been promoted. however, it is essential to involve the wider research community more actively in the collaboration, so that there is also a contribution from civil society to the whole project.
in writing. the declarations by the commission and the majority of the european parliament that the aim of the directive is to protect, especially young people, from the proven harmful health effects and risks of smoking are hypocritical and pretentious. the directive does not provide for any measures to prevent - especially for young people - or to help people to quit smoking. behind the various measures and photographic provisions to ban various types of tobacco and cigarettes, such as flavoured or slim, the extent of warnings on cigarette packages, etc., lie fierce competitions between the monopoly groups operating in the tobacco industry and related or even new tobacco-related products, for market conquest, dominance and dominance over their competitors, especially those with a small market share. the result of the vote is a compromise between the warring factions of the monopolies and their political representatives. the postponement of the final vote allows the political staff of capital in the european parliament to negotiate with the council and the tobacco and cigarette industry monopolies in order to continue the profitability of the plutocracy at the expense of the public health.
