in writing. this report is of particular interest to greece as it lists the problems and difficulties faced by regional and local authorities in using european structural funds. it is worth supporting the proposal for flexibility in the release of community resources, in the context of macroeconomic governance, in order to ensure that there are appropriate investments and those which can really contribute to sustainable development at local and regional level. the report, which i voted in favour of, is not binding, but it does show the growing demand for the removal of bureaucratic and formal structures in the eu so that the resources of the structural funds can be used directly in the areas for which they are intended. but this also requires a sound and credible strategy for their use by local, regional and national authorities, which is what our country, especially in the wake of the economic crisis, is called upon to do in view of the new multiannual financial framework (2014-2020).
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the impact of budgetary constraints on regional and local authorities in relation to eu structural fund expenditure in the member states. one of the negative consequences of the economic crisis has been the significant constraints on public finances in the member states. these restrictions are also reflected in the financing of regional and local authorities, at a time when these authorities are called upon to do even more, precisely because of the difficult economic situation in which governments find themselves. at the same time, the eu's next long-term budget is smaller than the current one. the situation is therefore such that the eu regions are in danger of being hit twice, both by the economic crisis at home and by budgetary constraints at european level. in order to safeguard the regions' potential for future development, the maximum possible flexibility of the co-financing of eu programmes and policies should be supported, by exploiting all margins of flexibility in the context of macroeconomic governance and in particular by reviewing the relationship between the stability and growth pact and productive public investment. this will ensure adequate investment to contribute to economic recovery and growth.

in writing. for another year, the european parliament was confronted with the council's refusal to provide information and to answer questions from members of the committee on budgetary control in order to take a reasoned decision on granting discharge. the european parliament therefore decided, for the third consecutive financial year, to refuse to grant discharge to the european council and the council in respect of the council. this attitude of the council is contrary to the spirit of good cooperation which should govern the eu institutions and prevents parliament from exercising its right of review as an authority on the granting of the discharge, which is enshrined in the treaty. a solution must therefore be found, and in this regard it is positive that the presidency of the council has shown a willingness to develop fruitful cooperation between parliament and the council, with the irish presidency proposing the setting up of an interinstitutional working party for negotiations with a view to finding possible solutions to the question of the council's discharge. finally, it should be noted that in order to increase transparency in the financial management and accountability of the two institutions, the budget of the european council should be separated from the budget of the council.
mr. president, to live without fear of violence is a basic human right. it is a fundamental element and expression of european culture, of the european system of principles and values. and yet, more and more often, more and more women in europe are victims of violent behaviour, most often within their own family or wider social environment. so, most of the time, silence covers this sick phenomenon and convictions are rare. the international treaty of istanbul aims to remedy this unacceptable situation by preventing it, by protecting the victims, by prosecuting the offenders, by implementing coordinated policies. as the european parliament, as the member states and as the european union, we must do everything possible to ensure that this treaty is ratified as quickly as possible and that it is implemented effectively. it is unacceptable that only two member states have ratified it. we must strive for a europe free of violence against women and violence within the family.

in writing. this report tackles the issue of misleading not consumers but professionals, traders and businesses. these are marketing policies that include 'covered' charges or charges for services for which the professional's consent was never given (e.g. advertising in a professional directory without a contract). despite the efforts of the eu within the common market to limit these phenomena, the level of protection of businesses still varies considerably from one member state to another. for example, only austria and belgium have included a specific prohibition on misleading listing schemes in their legislation. the present report, which i voted in favour of, is a first attempt to improve cooperation between the member states in the fight against misleading advertising, with a view to gradually converging the legislative frameworks in this direction.

in general, not just in the area of justice - that remain unused in many member states, resources that have not been absorbed and infrastructures that have not been created, which does not allow us to say that we are creating bases at national level and therefore at european level to achieve a better functioning of the justice system through the internet. the conclusion remains that we have the potential, that the next programming framework is very important and ambitious but that a necessary condition for its implementation is the better functioning of justice at national level by the member states.

mr president, when we started the fisheries fund in the european union, the problem was fishing capacity and the modernisation of our fleet, so we gave subsidies to scarce resources. why was that? we needed more ships. the years passed and we were very successful in modernising and increasing our fleet, and now we have over-capacity. for many decades now we have been overfishing. the scarce resource now is fish. this is where we need to give subsidies. if you ask fisherman what the problem is, their problem is that they cannot catch enough fish because there are not enough fish around. this is what a fisherman needs in order to have a sustainable, secure future and to have income. we need funds that result in more fish in the seas, not more boats. our fishermen do not sell boats, they sell fish; this is what they need. i support data collection funds for data collection, and i support preservation measures and increasing gear selectivity – but not more boats.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, it is very unfortunate that, although we have been collecting data for many years at member state level – and have obliged the member states, under european legislation, to collect this data, and have often paid for research papers by europeans – this data, together with any other data that is out there, is not collected, not publicly available and not able to be used for what we need in assessing the situation and helping us to make more accurate policies. so the initiative of the commission’s green paper on marine knowledge was very timely, as was this report by mrs patrão neves that i shadowed for the socialists. we worked very closely with mrs patrão neves, cooperated very well and agreed to the compromise package. so their proposal is that this data will be used, lightly aggregated if necessary but not to an extent that will not allow the full use of the material, so that we can have more data on what is happening in our seas. this is very important in order to protect vulnerable marine ecosystems, to find and protect spawning grounds, to protect nursery grounds, and for so many other operations. it is very closely connected with other legislation. as you know, i am the rapporteur for the deep-sea file. the mapping of the seabed and the deep-sea file are closely interconnected in many other pieces of legislation. i fully support this report, and i am sure it will also help us to achieve what we decided in the basic regulation.

in writing. this decision aims to fill the legal gap in regulation (ec) no 111/2005 on the rules for monitoring trade in drug precursors between the eu and third countries. the term 'drug precursors' means legal substances which are also used in the manufacture of drugs. a typical example is the acetic acid used in the manufacture of heroin. the proposal for an amendment to the regulation, which i voted for, provides for the classification of substances into the following categories: those subject to substantial control procedures at export, import or transit and those subject only to certain formal procedures, exclusively when exported to certain countries. at the same time, new medicinal products are being added to the list of precursors. the european parliament, however, expresses its concern about the grading chosen in any case because it is likely to lead to time-consuming control procedures at the same time as drug traffickers are constantly inventing new ways of using drugs to create drugs.

in writing. i voted in favour of the 2014 eu general budget. for another year, the ep's committee on budgets took a responsible stance by rejecting the council's horizontal cuts that are not in line with the eu's real needs, nor do they reflect the outcome of the mff negotiations, as instead of the forward-looking allocation of funds for the horizon 2020, erasmus+ and cosme programmes in 2014, the council cut the commission's proposal for these programmes that contribute to the fight against youth unemployment. as regards cohesion policy, which is the main and most appropriate investment instrument that can lead the eu to recovery from the crisis, growth and job creation, while precisely addressing the investment needs of the regions, i find the reduction of resources by the council unacceptable, at a time when the latest available estimates seem to indicate that some eur 20 billion in accounts will remain unpaid at the end of 2013 and should be carried over to the 2014 budget. as meps, we have a duty, and we do, to ensure that the various eu policies are adequately funded, particularly those which, like cohesion policy, result in growth and job creation.
in writing. this own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, is a follow-up to the green paper 'knowledge of the sea in 2020: from seabed mapping to ocean forecasts' which the commission adopted almost a year ago. it is intended to make proposals for the mapping and dissemination of data on fishing activity in europe. greece, a maritime country with an increased fishing activity, is particularly interested in the earliest possible implementation of the objective of creating a digital map of the seabed of european waters, which should be completed by 2020. this is an ambitious project, which depends on many factors (institutions, academic research, use of high technology) and increased resources, and whose results will provide valuable information on the marine wealth and our country (adequacy of fish stocks, etc.).
in writing. the report on the european semester is characterized by the spirit of strengthening its democratic legitimacy and introducing a more social dimension. i voted against the report because it remains fully in line with the failed neoliberal policies of the union. the problem with the european semester is not just that it is democratized and that it is simply a matter of assessing its social impact. the problem is structural. it is linked to the anti-social and anti-democratic policies it promotes. the introduction of the european semester paved the way for the imposition of european austerity memoranda throughout europe, a choice that was subsequently validated by the enhanced surveillance and the budgetary pact. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat the problem of unemployment. the member states have lost all possibility of pursuing an alternative economic policy with a development and redistributive role. the disastrous consequences of the european semester and the policies it promotes have ceased to be speculations and have become a sad reality. the peoples of europe are being levelled socially. in greece, the results of the implementation of the ostensible fiscal consolidation are permanent fiscal deficits used as an alibi for more stringent austerity measures, resulting in debt rising to 160% of gdp, unemployment soaring to 30%, poverty rising and zero growth.

the rapporteur. - the mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we have succeeded. i would like to thank everyone who has worked towards this result, and in particular my friend and co-rapporteur, mr ertug, mr riquet and mr ayala sender for our work and cooperation, and of course all the members of the transport committee and all the staff of the commission and the irish presidency who have worked towards today's result. the agreement reached on the trans-european transport networks and the new connecting europe financial instrument paves the way for a modern, environmentally friendly, integrated and interoperable european combined transport network. the new transeuropean transport networks are among the most ambitious and tangible development programmes of the european union for the next 15-20 years because they ensure the unimpeded mobility of goods and european citizens, including those with mobility problems, and because they strengthen the economic, social and geographical cohesion of all regions of the european union. in this way they make a decisive contribution to the growth, competitiveness and innovation of the european economy and to the vital increase in employment for the peoples of europe. to achieve these objectives, a new methodology was adopted in the design of the network, which was strictly observed against narrow national interests and efforts. the new methodology distinguishes between two levels in the network: the 'central network' and the 'integrated network'. the 'core network' is literally the 'blood supply system' of the single market because it links the main european urban centres and economic centres and allows the unimpeded flow of goods and people into the single market. this network aims to respond to the main challenges faced by transport in europe for many years, such as congestion of transport arteries and the absence of critical cross-border links. the new design of the new pan-european transport network takes seriously the need for all member states and their regions to be adequately covered by the network. it also ensures that the greatest effort is focused where the highest european added value is. this ensures that investments are mutually reinforcing. for this reason, it is envisaged that priority and special attention will be given to the major axes and in particular to projects of common interest with high european added value. in this context, particular emphasis was placed on the corridors tool and in particular on the role and responsibilities of the european coordinators. the european coordinators shall acquire substantive powers and become catalysts for assisting and promoting the implementation of projects of common interest along the nine corridors. in addition, there will be clear timetables for the completion of the networks. this will give both the european commission and the member states a clear objective and a clear time horizon for working together. the agreement reached on the connecting europe facility (cef), which provides for the allocation of eur 23 billion for the period 2014-2020 - a figure that is below our initial ambitions - is nevertheless a satisfactory basis for attracting the additional public and private investment that will be required to implement the new trans-european transport networks strategy. the project bonds, which are still at a pilot stage, can provide additional possibilities. ladies and gentlemen, the new trans-european transport networks, together with the new financial instrument 'connecting europe', represent a major qualitative leap forward compared to the past. the new transeuropean transport networks are a realistic and feasible strategic plan. they demonstrate that the european union can produce concrete, tangible results for the benefit of european citizens at a time when europe is 'thirsty' for growth and jobs.
madam president, at a time like the present, when europe is desperately seeking to regain its lost competitiveness and to emerge from recession, an integrated inter-operable combined transport network is a development tool of unique european added value. although the agreed funding is not sufficient for such an ambitious project, the acquisition of the core network alone is sufficient to make the project significantly more efficient. moreover, recognition of the specific nature of island regions will contribute both to strengthening their populations and economies and to their integration into the internal market. today's initial draft may be incomplete but it is certainly the beginning of the realisation of an ambitious vision and for that i want to thank my fellow rapporteurs, the commissioner and all those who have worked for the common cause.
the rapporteur. - the mr president, after two and a half years of hard work, today with our debate here and tomorrow with our vote, we are making a very important qualitative leap for transport in europe. competitiveness, growth and employment are three objectives to which the new transeuropean transport networks can contribute. this is a very important event for all the peoples of europe, and especially for the peoples of the cohesion countries and those countries, like my own country, which are facing a great recession and high unemployment. the legislative stage is indeed coming to an end. now, the next bet that we have before us is the realization and implementation of this grand design. i would like to ask the commission and the commissioner, whom i congratulate once again on the work he and his team have done, to contribute their expertise and their active presence and to support the cohesion countries and the countries whose economies have special needs with their expertise so that these countries can derive as much benefit as possible and as much potential for growth and employment from the new major project, the transeuropean transport networks.

in writing. i would like to congratulate parliament's negotiating team on their methodical and fruitless work. while the agreement on the 2014-2020 mff is not the most ideal and we cannot be fully satisfied that for the first time in history the eu's long-term budget will be reduced, we cannot but acknowledge that this was the best possible outcome that parliament could achieve. we have also succeeded in focusing the mff on growth and job creation, combating youth unemployment, strengthening research and innovation programmes and supporting small and medium-sized enterprises. we have also received assurances that the total budget will be used to the maximum, that there will be more flexibility in payments and commitments and that there will be the possibility of reviewing and revising the budget in the interim. the time is now running backwards for the start of the programmes of the new programming period which are intended to contribute to recovery and development efforts. let us not forget that the eu budget is the union's main investment instrument and acts as a catalyst for economic growth, job creation and the achievement of social, economic and territorial cohesion.

in writing. the 2014-2020 mff constitutes an austerity budget which, in combination with the economic policies implemented by the council, the commission, the ecb and the imf, is in no way able to cope with the consequences of the crisis. the reduced budget will cause further increase in unemployment, reduction in labour rights and cuts in public spending in areas such as health, education, research. the huge cuts in the structural funds and the cohesion fund will further increase economic and social disparities between member states. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the european union budget for the financial year 2000 is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area. europe needed more than ever an increased budget, one based on the idea of solidarity between peoples, aimed at creating growth by securing decent jobs. on the contrary, through opaque and undemocratic procedures on the part of the council, the new mff 2014-2020 reflects the political will to continue austerity and serves the interests of the union's strong economies.
in writing. i voted in favour of the regulation for the mff 2014-2020. after the adoption of the european commission proposal and the subsequent negotiations on the texts of the legislative regulations and on the amount of their funding, we are now at the end of this effort which undoubtedly marked our parliamentary term. negotiations with the council were intense and many times conducted in a negative atmosphere, particularly after the february 2013 summit, when the council refused to further negotiate with parliament on the overall size of the budget and the inclusion of items subject to the co-decision procedure. although the size of the eu's long-term budget is being reduced for the first time in its history, which i cannot be happy about, it is important that we now vote on the mff regulation so that eu programmes can be implemented on time and that continuity and predictability of funding to beneficiaries can be ensured. finally, it should not be forgotten that the flexibility and the revision clause introduced in the mff, following strong pressure from parliament, makes it possible to use eu funding to the full and to make the mff responsive to future economic conditions.
in writing. the agreement of the political representatives of capital, pp-s&d-liberals-nd-pasok-laos-dracis, on the eu budget, the so-called multiannual financial framework 2014-2020 (mff), opens the way for the quick financing of the eu monopoly groups, through the plundering of the people's income. the main lines of the mff 2014-2020 are: targeted capital grants for entrepreneurial action in new high-yield areas such as renewable energy, the digital market, transport, energy and telecommunications networks; the subordination of scientific research to the needs of capital by strengthening the 'centres of excellence' and by directly linking education with the big capitalist companies; the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force. the workers must have no illusions. the eu budget, whether increased or reduced, is designed to boost the profitability of businesses rather than the interest of the people. the only way for the working people is to strengthen the anti-monopoly anti-capitalist struggle of the people's alliance for the overthrow of monopolies, for the disengagement from the eu, for the unilateral cancellation of the debt and for the conquest and construction of workers' power, which is an essential condition for meeting the modern needs of the working people.
in writing. increasing the funds to fight youth unemployment, poverty and social exclusion in each country must be issues of major importance for the future of europe. in the context of dealing with the devastating consequences of the economic crisis for the people, this regulation is legitimate. but while there is this increase in the amount of the european social fund for specific countries, the total amount of the budget for the structural funds is being reduced, while at the same time the blackmailing condition of macroeconomic selectivity is being introduced for countries facing budgetary difficulties. it is necessary to tackle unemployment, poverty and social exclusion in their entirety. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a regulation on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget (dab) no 7 for 2013 covering the increase of commitment appropriations by eur 150 million in title 1b, as it is a result of the final outcome of the negotiations on the mff for the period 2014-2020 and concerns france, italy and spain. the european council of 27 and 28 june 2013 considered that a budgetary solution should be provided for the abovementioned countries. the eur 150 million in commitment appropriations will be covered by the margin below the expenditure ceiling of title 1b (eur 16 million) and by the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument (eur 134 million). it is particularly important to emphasise that in the event of unforeseen situations a corrective budget should be used and funding should be made through new appropriations, without jeopardising the funding of programmes already implemented or for which the eu is legally committed and for which payments should be made.
in writing. i voted in favour of draft amending budget (dab) no 8 for 2013 of eur 3,9 billion, the second instalment of 2013's dab 2, which aimed to increase payment appropriations to enable the european commission to pay the outstanding 2012 accounts in all policy areas of the eu budget, namely under the structural funds and the cohesion fund - payments that could not be made on time due to the lack of sufficient payment appropriations in the 2012 budget. the european parliament had made clear from the outset that there should be no payment shortfall at the end of 2013 and that an unusually high level of unpaid bills should not be carried over to the new financial period. the adoption of spd 8 was therefore one of the conditions for parliament to put the mfn regulation to the vote. by adopting the council position on ipa 8, the european parliament lays the foundations for starting the new 2014-2020 programming period with the smallest possible structural deficit and for the european commission to respect the legal obligations it has assumed towards eu funding recipients.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for spain to support the redundancies in 140 construction materials companies operating in the valencia region. the continuing economic crisis in spain has further highlighted the added value of the egf as a tool for the european union's social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the spanish regions are subject to a process of rationalization and drastic cuts. the eib's support will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of fiscal consolidation of the national and regional budgets of spain.
in writing. the kke group in the european parliament voted against the report on the approval of the eu's conclusion of the revision of the international contracting agreement (icpa) because this agreement seeks to intensify the exploitation of the wealth and peoples of third countries by the monopoly groups. the aim of the gpa, to which the most powerful capitalist countries are signatories, is to open up national public procurement markets. the revision of the convention under approval seeks to identify third countries by the trap of some differentiated treatment arrangements for these countries. however, these arrangements are transitional and of limited duration and do not provide any protection for the markets of these countries, nor do they offer any advantage in terms of access to the markets of large capitalist countries. the sole objective is to open up the markets of these countries so that the eu's monopoly enterprises and other imperialist centres can invade them, intensify the exploitation of workers and plunder the energy resources in particular and other resources of wealth available to these countries. the rapporteur of the report presents the revision of the gatt as a benefit for third countries and tries to cover up the plunder by monopolies which only bring misery to the people with false pretenses of transparency, corruption and so on.
in writing. this report, which i voted in favour of, approved the new erasmus+ programme, which is the continuation of the separate programmes on mobility in education and lifelong learning. the new agreement constitutes an integrated eu strategy for education, training, youth and sport. it increases the choice of young people, giving them the opportunity to develop skills and gain more employment prospects. it also plays a key role in combating unemployment in the eu, particularly in the southern member states. the programme will run from 2014 to 2020 and mobilise a total of around eur 14,8 billion. erasmus+ provides, among other things, the basis for the recognition of non-formal education, provides flexibility and clarity to hitherto fragmented programmes and creates a student loan guarantee mechanism for postgraduate studies abroad. the commission also adopted a communication on the implementation of the european year of the environment, which sets out the main priorities for the future of the european union.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs pack's report. the erasmus programme is the most successful student exchange programme in the world and must be continued. the reduced community resources must not prevent it from continuing, but, as the rapporteur and the commission also suggest, it is important to reduce the overlap and fragmentation found in the existing programmes in the individual education sectors.
in writing. the eu and the member states' civil governments are seeking, through the new erasmus for all programme, which is a follow-up to the eu's erasmus student mobility programme, to promote the strengthening of restructuring in university education and vocational training and the abolition of public and free higher education. they promote the manipulation of consciences and the subordination of students-teachers-researchers to eu values, competitiveness and the capitalist market. in order to provide cheap 'recyclable' labour to monopolies, the programme finances and assesses the degree of implementation of the reactive 'bologna treaty' and promotes 'lifelong learning'. the implementation of erasmus for all leads to the abolition of the free public university system. the programme provides for the provision of guarantees to financial institutions for the 'granting' of loans to students, thus paving the way for the introduction of tuition fees by higher education institutions. the programme creates ideological mechanisms to reproduce and defend eu values - the values of the rotten exploitative system - through funding programmes in universities, departments and centres that propagandize the eu's 'political integration', this anti-people eu construction.
in writing. the resolution on the creative europe programme, which i voted in favour of, supports culture and creativity, protects tangible and intangible cultural heritage and highlights the 'mosaic' of european cultural identity. the dissemination of european values is directly linked to the development and promotion of cultural creation, which is the basis of the programme. creative europe shall support the audiovisual industry, with particular emphasis on smes and start-ups, and shall support creators and artists. it is estimated that more than five million jobs in the eu are related to culture and creativity in general, while the contribution to the union's gdp is estimated at 2.6%. the programme, which is divided into two sections, culture and media, links the audiovisual sector to europe's cultural diversity and takes another step towards addressing new challenges such as the need to adapt to globalisation and the transition to the digital age.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs costa's report. the protection and promotion of creativity and culture are essential to strengthening the foundations and the future of european unity and sustainable development. i agree with the rapporteur's amendments on transnational cooperation and improved cooperation between cultural operators and those on the training of cultural and creative professionals.
in writing. 2013 is the european year of citizens and in may 2014 elections will be held to elect the members of the european parliament. this initiative highlights the importance of citizen participation in european actions and policies. the report on the 'europe for citizens' programme, which i supported, includes a framework for promoting partnerships and twinning and provides for actions to preserve historical memory, raise awareness and mobilise citizens. the new programme highlights the values on which the union was built and developed, born out of the ashes of the world wars, values which we need today, when the danger of the rise of extremism is unfortunately becoming more and more visible and racism and intolerance are dangerously magnified. citizen participation is an added value factor in european policy-making. the new programme promotes initiatives that focus on issues of major concern to citizens themselves and in areas of real vital importance, while ensuring a geographically fair distribution of resources and providing support for sports organisations of particular importance. the european parliament, despite its limited ability to make further amendments to this programme, has adopted an important tool and is making it available to citizens so that they can participate in shaping policies that affect them.
in writing. the kke voted against the eu's anti-communist program called europe for citizens 2014-2020 because it is an even more vulgar version of the previous program of the 2007-2013 period that finances the anti-communist campaign of the eu, the falsification of the historical truth and the unacceptable propaganda of the identification of fascism with communism. as it is stated in its title, the programme finances 'the preservation of the memory of the crimes committed under all kinds of dictatorships in modern european history, such as nazism, fascism and totalitarian communist regimes, including stalinism, with the ultimate aim of preserving peaceful coexistence in europe'. - what ? at the same time that the monopolies, the eu and the bourgeois governments are crushing the lives and rights of the working class and the poor popular strata, lowering wages and pensions, drastically reducing any social spending and dismantling the public social security, health-welfare and education systems, at the same time they can and have a million-dollar package (229 million euros) to spray anti-communist poison on the consciences of the working people and especially the youth. it is thus confirmed that the anti-people attack of capital and the political staff requires raw anti-communism in order to be able to strike the class labour movement and its vanguard, the marxist-leninist communist parties.
in writing. i voted in favour of the regulation, which focuses on projects of high european added value in the areas of transport, energy and telecommunications infrastructure. the aim is to create integrated transeuropean networks to ensure the development and competitiveness of the transport, energy and telecommunications sectors. to achieve these objectives, the regulation provides, in terms of public transport planning, the means for implementing infrastructure projects, encourages research, technology transfer and development (economic and operational), and seeks to create the conditions for the eu to have efficient and reliable transport, energy and telecommunications systems that can contribute to the global competitiveness of its economy.
in writing. i voted in favour of the proposal for a regulation because it makes it easier to link europe. projects such as those covered by this regulation in the areas of infrastructure, transport, energy and telecommunications will bring greater european added value and ensure efficient and effective transport links, which will promote the equal development of all european regions, the economic, social and territorial cohesion of the eu, and the completion of the single market.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. transeuropean transport networks are of great importance for the proper functioning of the common market. the report makes a positive contribution to the mobility of eu citizens, economic, social and territorial cohesion, economic growth and job creation.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr de castro's report. the adoption of delegated acts, and in particular the duration of the period of delegation of power to the commission, should not be open-ended. · i therefore agree with the rapporteur's amendments to limit them to five years, with the possibility of tacit extension for periods of equal duration, unless the european parliament or the council object no later than three months before the end of each period.

the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - the the commission's proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of subsidiarity to the development of the internal market is a very important one. the commission and the council are now once again blackmailing the member states with the macroeconomic conditions to implement the harsh neoliberal policies that lead to the one-way street of austerity and impoverishment. the dependence of regional financing on these macroeconomic conditions means that inequalities between european regions are growing, poverty and unemployment are worsening and the countries of the south are being condemned to permanent underdevelopment. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. they reduce the european social fund funding and exclude from the additional funding countries that are suffering from the crisis. it is regrettable that the european parliament, despite the conditions it had set out in the beginning, now consistently agrees to all these outcomes, namely a pseudo-cohesion policy, a pseudo-development policy and a pseudo-democracy.

the european parliament, has drawn up a very good plan, which the council has tried to weaken. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. it is true that in the past this fund has been accused of environmental damage and has been linked in particular to projects mainly of a construction nature and to waste of resources. now, we can say that this fund is more environmentally friendly, more green and that it will finance projects that are important for the implementation of environmental policy but also for social cohesion. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful substances. we can mention waste management projects based on the principle of the hierarchy of management methods rather than projects mainly for landfilling waste, projects for the management of water resources based on the water framework directive, projects that preserve and do not destroy water resources. it is important that the european cohesion fund will finance projects to promote renewable energy sources and energy saving, both for public buildings and for housing, which is new. this is particularly important in an era of energy poverty and can contribute both to job creation and social cohesion. it also provides for the restoration of ecosystems and special natura sites, the protection of biodiversity and also the promotion of combined transport and multimodal transport systems, in contrast to the projects of the past which concerned only i.x cars. we have insisted strongly on the integration of the rights of persons with disabilities in the design and implementation of projects and on the involvement of the local community and regional authorities in regional planning. we can do many things, and we can especially strengthen the participation of the local community in the planning, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of these projects, so that bureaucracy and waste of taxes and corruption are reduced.

the new cap leaves member states with a great deal of choice and flexibility. this is necessary when designing rural development programmes to take account of the specific characteristics of each region. however, this possibility is now extended to the way in which direct aid is distributed. the common dimension of the cap is being weakened and the potential for widening disparities in the implementation of the cap and in economic and environmental outcomes in each country is being increased. the commission has not answered the question: 'how will the member states affected by the economic crisis make equal use of the rural development programmes? in writing. the agreement on the new multiannual financial framework is undoubtedly a highlight of the current term of office of the european parliament. although the final budget does not meet initial expectations, it does contain some optimistic actions for the future, some of which are new. in total, some €960 billion in appropriations and €908 billion in payments will be allocated to eu actions. of these resources, 373.2 billion (39%) is earmarked for the common agricultural policy, 325.1 billion (34%) will be absorbed by the cohesion policy for growth and employment and 125.6 billion (13.1%) will be channelled to research and technology. it is true that, compared with the current budget (2007-2013), there is a slight reduction in resources, which the member states have cited as the reason for the economic crisis and the budgetary constraints they face. the outcome of the negotiations, as reflected in this report, which i voted in favour of, provides greece with valuable resources for the next seven years, particularly in those areas which can act as a steam engine for the reversal of the economic climate and the achievement of sustainable growth rates, a growth based on the comparative advantages of our country and investment in human resources, research and innovation.
in writing. i voted in favour of the joint conclusions of the conciliation, according to which the level of the eu budget is set for 2014 at eur 142.6 billion for commitments and eur 135.5 billion for payments. the results are positive because they take account of several of parliament's priorities and because they support programmes that contribute to growth and job creation, combat social exclusion and put citizens at the centre of the european construction and promote the eu abroad. however, we cannot be entirely satisfied with the result in payments, despite the fact that parliament managed to add €350 million to the council's position, already considerably reduced both in relation to the draft budget for 2014 and in relation to the budget for 2013. the low level of the 2014 payment ceiling, combined with the accumulation of payments from 2013 carried over to 2014 - despite the approval of eur 11,2 billion - is likely to create a situation in which the agreed amount of payments will not be able to fully cover the needs. the european parliament is therefore ready to use all the flexibility provided for in the mff to ensure that the eu respects its legal obligations towards beneficiaries.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument for the financing of the structural funds for cyprus. although the impact of the economic crisis on the level of prosperity of some member states within the euro area had already been recognised in the structural funds allocation included in the february 2013 european council conclusions, the macro-financial assistance programme for cyprus had not yet been decided. the european parliament and the council have therefore reached the political decision that additional assistance should be granted to cyprus to address the difficult economic situation on the island. for 2014, it is proposed to increase the commitment appropriations by eur 100 million. as this amount exceeds the margin below the expenditure ceiling of title 1b, the commission proposes to mobilise the flexibility instrument at the same time for a total amount of eur 89,33 million. it is important that the eu supports countries facing the negative consequences of the economic crisis, in particular through the investment opportunities offered by the assistance from the structural funds. this is one of the most tangible expressions of solidarity on the part of the union and constitutes a decisive contribution to the economic recovery efforts of these countries.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on draft amending budget (dab) no 9 of 2013 on the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund to deal with the damage caused by the forest fires in romania in summer 2012 and the floods in germany, austria and the czech republic in spring 2013. the commission has also received a request from the united kingdom to provide additional information on the implementation of the 2014 budget. although the council's initial position was to fully finance this eur 400,5 million ppp through reallocation from various budget lines, the final agreement provides for eur 250,5 million to be credited to the 2013 budget and the remaining eur 150 million to be from new appropriations in the 2014 budget. this means that eur 200 million will be available to finance research programmes as specified in the overall transfer proposed by the european commission. it should be noted, however, that the european parliament maintains its consistent position that, in principle, the financing of special instruments such as the solidarity fund should be done with fresh money, above and beyond the ceilings of the mff.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund for the drought and forest fires in romania in the summer of 2012 and the floods in germany, austria and the czech republic in the spring of 2013. priority shall be given to the speedy restoration of normal living conditions for the people affected by the floods and forest fires and to the rapid recovery of economic activity in these areas. the eu solidarity fund is once again demonstrating its importance in responding to major natural disasters by providing financial support to the affected states. however, the fund can and must be mobilised more quickly. the three institutions must therefore work to enable the solidarity fund to intervene with even less delay and with even greater flexibility. in this way we will be able to ensure the real added value of the instrument in question.
in writing. the two european satellite navigation systems, galileo and egnos, are entering their final phase, albeit with considerable delays. galileo will not be able to become fully operational in 2013 as planned, and egnos is not currently ensuring coverage of all member states' territories. i voted in favour of the report, which contains specific proposals to avoid delays and maximise the benefits. to understand the importance of these two programmes, it should be noted that today 6% to 7% of the eu-27's gdp, or €800 billion, depends on the american gps system. the development of european programmes will generate a net gain of eur 68,63 billion for the union over the life cycle of the system, i.e. from 2014 to 2034. it is therefore clear that the timely start-up of these systems is critical and decisive for the european economy as a whole, but also for each member state individually.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr marinescu's report. the european satellite navigation policy is important for improving efficiency in many sectors of the economy as well as in many areas of citizens' daily lives. the existence of a new legal basis for the long-term operation, maintenance and management of radio navigation systems is important.
in writing. i voted against the report on the common provisions of the funds, firstly because it institutes 'macroeconomic selectivity' and secondly because it is based on the failed model of the eu's neoliberal economic governance and the europe 2020 strategy. the dependence of regional financing on the compliance with reactionary and anti-people macroeconomic demands means the permanent condemnation to underdevelopment of the crisis-stricken countries. the last possibility of financing, through the european funds, actions and alternative development policies which promote solidarity, full employment, the reduction of unemployment and poverty is now being lost. overall, the design of cohesion policy and the funds for the new programming period is not linked to the real social and economic problems facing europe's regions and citizens. cohesion policy is losing its role and is becoming a watchdog for neoliberal austerity policies and a punishment for the 'disciplinary' european regions that are facing problems. it is based on statistics and planning of the past five years which do not correspond to the current socioeconomic environment, as shaped by the crisis. at such a critical juncture, esf funding is being cut by 7 billion, while those countries most affected by the crisis and by the memorandum programmes are excluded from additional funding.
in writing. i voted in favour of the regulation on common provisions for the european structural and investment funds, which provides, inter alia, for a strengthened role for local and regional authorities, simplification of rules, the possibility of financing from multiple funds, improved management and control systems, more targeted technical assistance, wider use of innovative financial instruments, ex ante conditionalities that can be objectively assessed to ensure that investments are effective and have added value, measures for youth employment and social inclusion. i would also like to highlight the success of the european parliament's negotiating team in increasing the prefinancing and cofinancing rates and reducing the rate of the reserve to free up resources more quickly. however, we must not forget that compromise is the key word, which means that all sides had to accept issues that they are not fully satisfied with, such as the inclusion of macroeconomic conditions in the general regulation. now everything is in place to start implementing the programmes of the new cohesion policy period, which, with its undoubted value, is the eu's main investment tool and can contribute to recovery and development efforts and to achieving economic, social and territorial cohesion.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because i am satisfied with the agreement between the european commission, the european parliament and the council on the european social fund, the most important fund for promoting employment and combating poverty in europe. it is worth noting that 20% of the fund's allocation to each member state is earmarked for social inclusion and the fight against poverty, which allows greece to obtain significant funds that it can, in this difficult economic period, channel towards vulnerable social groups. the simplification of the procedures for the use of these resources is also important, as it will allow the country to further improve absorption rates in the future to the benefit of citizens. a major effort is being made to integrate the unemployed into society and to ensure their access to resources and services, with special reference to support for young people, for whom the esf is promoting innovative solutions to maximise and improve the quality of employment.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the european regional development fund (erdf) and the investment for growth and jobs goal, which aims to strengthen economic and social cohesion in the eu by correcting regional imbalances in the eu in terms of growth, productivity and employment. it is essential that the four main objectives supported by the european parliament in the negotiations have been achieved, namely greater flexibility for the regions in terms of thematic concentration, a more ecological approach with a higher concentration on low-carbon measures, more opportunities for job creation in enterprises, with a focus on small and medium-sized enterprises, and additional social aspects such as financing for sustainable tourism and culture. in addition, greater power has been given to urban areas in decision-making on sustainable urban development issues and greater responsibilities have been assigned to urban authorities for projects related to the selection of actions. finally, it should be noted that the erdf regulation contains specific provisions concerning exceptions to the rules on 'thematic concentration' (minimum amounts for specific objectives) in island regions and phasing out regions.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the european regional development fund and the european territorial cooperation objective, which is one of the objectives of cohesion policy and provides the framework for the implementation of joint actions and policy exchanges between national, regional and local actors from different member states and third countries. in the course of the negotiations, the european parliament achieved better standardisation and simplification of rules and procedures between member states, inclusive geographical coverage and synergies with macro-regional and sea-basin strategies - a development which is particularly important for islands, while it should be noted that in justified cases, cross-border cooperation for the outermost regions will be possible along maritime borders of more than 150 km. in addition, there will be increased flexibility in the choice of partners, thematic objectives and investment priorities, and a greater emphasis on technical assistance and staff costs, given the specific nature of the european territorial cooperation objective. with these improvements and additions, the european parliament supports all those actions which lead to an effective and efficient cooperation of the parties concerned in order to achieve the objective of territorial cohesion, as provided for in the treaty of lisbon.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the cohesion fund, the main instrument for providing assistance to member states with a gdp per capita of less than 90% of the eu average to create the necessary network of basic infrastructure that would not be possible to build with national investments of the so-called cohesion countries. the european parliament has succeeded in broadening the scope of the fund to include support for energy efficiency and the use of renewable energy sources in the housing sector and to allow support for high-efficiency district heating and cogeneration as an investment priority. it is important that the investment priorities pay particular attention to environmental issues, that it will be possible to invest in the waste and water sectors to meet the needs identified by the member states and that reference is made to the regulation on support for the natura 2000 network and environmentally friendly transport.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the reform of the rules of operation of the european groupings of territorial cooperation (egtcs). in the course of negotiations with the council, the european parliament succeeded in making the establishment of the egtcs easier and faster, simplifying their system and reducing the administrative burden, reviewing their activities, opening up the egtcs to regions outside the eu and clarifying some of their operating rules. the ppas will also be open to all public undertakings providing public services (economic services of general interest), including those governed by private law. this will facilitate territorial cooperation in areas such as public transport, energy, water, health, education and vocational training. the abovementioned improvements will contribute to a better and more efficient functioning of the eeigs, so that these groups can achieve greater added value and can more effectively promote cross-border, transnational and interregional cooperation.
in writing. the "new reform" of the cap for 2014-2020 gives the lucky shot to the poor and middle class farmers because it puts the poor farmer of the family farm and the small clergy in an even more tragic position. the new system reduces subsidies by at least 35%, thus drastically reducing farmers' incomes. in the end, only the big farmers and the capitalist agricultural enterprises will remain, which at lower production costs will absorb most of the subsidies and concentrate the land and production in their hands. the full integration of the cap into the objectives of the eu's anti-people europe 2020 strategy will have beneficial effects on the monopoly groups active in the field of agricultural production and supplies and in the production and distribution of food. the reduction in community funds for agricultural expenditure is accelerating the process of removing small and poor farmers from production and is supporting capitalist restructuring in the field of the agricultural economy, with the result that the living conditions of the rural poor and the working people in general are worsening. market liberalisation will create new risks of large fluctuations in producer prices, which will worsen the position of small producers and working consumers who are confronted with increasing precision and ensure superprofits for monopolies.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on amending council regulation (ec) no 1083/2006 as regards certain provisions on financial management for certain member states experiencing or threatened with serious difficulties with respect to their financial stability and the rules on discharge for certain member states. it is vital to give countries affected by the consequences of the financial crisis the possibility of increasing the rates of community co-financing so that investment from cohesion policy funds can continue unhindered. this will ensure that these countries are not deprived of much-needed investment that can contribute to their efforts to overcome the crisis and to economic recovery and growth.

important tool that will enable innovation partnerships to link up with their respective kics and create synergies with the structural and national funds. this will make the eit stronger and allow the eit innovation model to reach out to less developed regions of the european union. ladies and gentlemen, it is clear to me that the eit's operating model based on the 'knowledge triangle' (education, research, innovation) bridges the gap between the laboratory and the market, the university and the real economy, knowledge and entrepreneurship. the eit brings a new model of innovation that is much closer to european conditions, specificities and values and should be exemplified by all member states in the way it operates.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and i am sure that the commission will take the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. in countries with the biggest problems, like greece, we see seed banks of new businesses, we see startups of people with a passion for work, with inspiration, with ideas, with new proposals, we see new businesses that are in many cases models of organization and operation. here lies the way out of the crisis. we have those capabilities. it is right that what was said by the speakers and what was said by the commissioner, that the cosme and erasmus programmes for young entrepreneurs can be of decisive help. what was not said, however, is that even with the willingness of these people, we have low success rates of new ventures. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial instruments are used to finance the development of the internal market. the conclusion is that we need such expertise for the time being, we need everyone to be informed, we need to make full use of these funds and we need, of course, to avoid the need for any kind of bureaucracy in each member state.

again, after a long time, the challenges facing europe in the field of defence and security. in this context, we must consider how we can work together better and more efficiently in this very difficult period for europe. the european parliament wishes to send a strong message to the heads of state or government who will be discussing the matter at the european council. the message is that europe must move forward in this area too and that coordination and cooperation are the necessary prerequisites for strengthening the european union's role in the world and for making it a credible actor on the international stage. the european union cannot be just one large market. the common defence and security policy is an integral part of the european fabric and of the common european identity. i hope that the council will take the european parliament's position seriously as it will be expressed in the vote tomorrow. our world is changing rapidly and the union now faces new threats and new challenges in the eastern neighbourhood, the middle east, north africa and a zone of instability stretching from the horn of africa to the indian ocean. new threats are being added to the old ones, such as terrorism, cyber threats, the effects of climate change, the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, organised crime, at the same time as emerging economies play an increasingly important role globally in the economic, military and political spheres. the union has a wide range of military and civilian means at its disposal to deal with this complex new environment. the lisbon treaty provides the legal framework, but unfortunately it remains largely inactive. in a time of economic and financial crisis, defence budgets across europe are under great pressure. every euro spent on defence must be fully justified to the taxpayer. the european union has a duty to ensure that the european union is able to play a full part in the international negotiations, and that the european union is able to play a full part in the negotiations with the countries of central and eastern europe. we need cooperation, coordination, but also trust between partners. the report attaches great importance to maritime safety and calls on the high representative to draw up a european maritime strategy. it talks about the importance of energy security and infrastructure security. it refers to telecommunications security, internet security. the scope for action under the common defence and security policy is now much wider than in the past. the union must operate on land, in the air, at sea, in space, in cyberspace. the security and protection of borders is also closely linked to defence policy, at a time when the pressure on the european union, and particularly on certain member states, is increasing day by day. madam president, with this report we are expressing what i believe is a common demand of most political groups and we are calling on the heads of state and government to commit themselves to strengthening their cooperation for a more competitive and more effective european defence. we call on them to decide on the next steps and to set out a road map for the future madam president, i congratulate both rapporteurs on the reports they have presented to us. i believe that these reports will help the december meeting to fill a large gap in security and defence policy at european level. so far, the mutual assistance and solidarity clauses in article 42 (7) of the treaty on european union and article 222 of the treaty of lisbon have remained blank. first of all, we must guarantee, as the european union, the borders of all the member states. i should like to stress that for some countries of the union, defence spending is unconscionable. in greece, they have been 7% of gdp for decades. in 2008, they were capped at 3,5%. the last three years they have exceeded 2.2% of gdp after the cuts that have destroyed wages, pensions and education in greece. according to nato, greece is the second country in military spending after the united states. i would also like to stress that greece, under the threat of attack from turkey, has been exploited by friendly countries, which have promoted the supply of military equipment at exorbitant prices, after first corrupting the country's political system. in fact, traveling officials of high to senior level demanded and enforced the supply of war materials. for those who say that the greek people alone are to blame for greece's downfall, i ask them to look at greece's balance of payments over the last fifteen years to see that the bulk of the loans were issued within the european union.
madam president, i thank the council and commissioner tajani for their comments and for their presence. i would like to congratulate the two rapporteurs, mrs kopp and mr gahler, on their excellent reports, which really express parliament's position in favour of european union. the question is: can there be a union without a common defence? there's no such thing. it would be like talking about a state without a defense system. the truth is that for many years now the effort begun in 2001 has been abandoned, with the result that money has been spent without any impact and the european defence agency has not been used, while at the same time there have been quarrels and conflicts between the member states, which we know about. it is also disappointing that the foreign and defence council has not reached any important conclusions. can we expect anything more important from the european council? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the comments made by commissioner tajani are very important. it is characteristic that europe produces tanks in sixteen lines while the united states produces in one line. it is therefore clear that on the one hand the provisions of the lisbon treaty have not been fully implemented and on the other hand we could have achieved the same and better results with less money, because there is less money today because of the cuts and budgetary policy. the european council, which is the first meeting of the european council, is a forum for the exchange of views on the future of the union.
madam president, the common security and defence policy of the european union, as it is developing and in view of the december summit, is promoting the further militarization of the european union. in the context of the capitalist crisis and competition, the monopoly groups, the institutions of the european union and the bourgeois governments are calling for the establishment of a permanent military headquarters, a european union war budget and increased military capabilities by the creation of a permanent euro-army, which will have upgraded military equipment and infrastructure. they promote the strengthening of eu-nato cooperation and the upgrading of eu combat units, not only to support nato missions but also for independent union combat action, both in its immediate neighbourhood and thousands of kilometres away from europe's borders. these aspirations lead to the adaptation of national defence structures and military equipment to the aggressive imperialist objectives of the common security and defence policy of the european union. at the same time, the concentration of the european union's defence industry and the restructuring that is forcing the dismantling of both the defence industry in greece and sectors related to the country's defence, such as shipbuilding and repair, are being promoted. for these reasons, i believe that the values and interests of the european union which are upheld by the common security and defence policy of the european union are contrary to the interests of the peoples.
i have to say that i am not in favour of this. - the madam president, after this debate it is, i think, absolutely clear that the great majority of members and of the political groups want europe to move forward in the field of defence and security. we've come a long way, putting aside many of our differences and agreeing on common goals. but there is still a lot to be done, and i believe that we should not put a damper on our ambition. we must work to ensure that the common defence and security policy, which is an integral part of a stronger, more autonomous europe at international level,
