in writing. the eu's common security and defence policy, as it develops and in the run-up to the december 2013 summit, is promoting even greater militarisation of the eu. the monopoly groups and the eu, in the context of the capitalist crisis, are becoming even more aggressive, both internally at the expense of its own peoples in the member states and against the peoples of third countries. the political representatives of capital, in their reports to the european parliament, express even more aggressively the needs of the monopoly groups, in the inter-imperialist competitions that are raging, for the division and domination of the markets. they call for the establishment of a 'permanent military operational headquarters', they call for a european union military budget, increased military capabilities to form a permanent eurostrategy, with upgraded military equipment and infrastructure, capable of intervening in every corner of the planet. they are promoting greater eu-nato cooperation and at the same time increasing the eu's military capabilities, not only to support nato missions, but also for autonomous eu war action, in its immediate neighbourhood and thousands of kilometres from europe's borders. it is precisely these aspirations of the eu and governments that require the adaptation of national defence structures and the orientation of war equipment to the aggressive, imperialist objectives of the eu's political, security and defence policy.
mr president - thank you, commissioner - welcome to the house. i have here your communication, a communication which is a first step towards building the social dimension of the european union. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. when unemployment has swept over the younger generation in europe, when poverty is visiting more and more families uninvited, when people have been losing their homes, their jobs, their dignity for at least three years, you speak in your communication, in general and vaguely, of social indicators. we want, we demand here in the european parliament, that the indicators on unemployment, on poverty, on health care should be binding. we want, in other words, to see these social indicators not just on paper but taken into account alongside economic indicators when decisions are to be taken on people's lives in the context of emu. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the tax authorities of the member states are able to provide the necessary information to the tax authorities of the member states. but you will all agree that it is not capable of solving the problem of tax evasion and fraud on its own. i do not mean that the rapporteur and the committee have not done everything possible. i am referring to the fact that the union is avoiding to see the problem of tax evasion and fraud at its root. the commission's report is a good example of the need to ensure that the european union is able to take action in the field of the environment. secondly, the scope is selected and limited and is usually limited to employees, pensioners, ordinary citizens and does not include other areas where the great evasion takes place. thirdly, there is no real will to hit what we call 'the big professional tax evaders', where there is really the big tax evasion throughout the union. on the contrary, commissioner, in many countries, as in my own, citizens are living under austerity policies while at the same time being subjected to an unprecedented tax burden. so to tackle the problem effectively and to achieve the objective, we need a different tax policy, fair, redistributive, which will certainly control international trade activities, the profits of multinationals, intra-group transactions, tax havens and offshore companies.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. the 'horizon 2020' programme is one of the most ambitious and innovative policies introduced under the new multiannual financial framework (2014-2020). for the first time, a single 'common strategic framework for research and innovation' (csfri) brings together elements that have not been integrated into a common perspective. in particular, it is the continuation of the actions of the 7th framework programme and the innovation and competitiveness programme together with the european institute of innovation and technology. this will, within a single programme, bring together the entire innovation chain from basic research to the market, to emphasise the competitiveness of european industry and to create more opportunities for young scientists. at the same time, this point is of particular importance for greece, much attention is paid to social innovation, i.e. to the financing of new, smart and innovative proposals to address social challenges.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs madurell's report. horizon 2020 integrates research and innovation funding for the first time in a single common strategic framework. the financial and economic crisis has hit european industries hard. research and innovation must be strengthened if we are to emerge from this crisis strengthened and get the european economy back on track.
in writing. in order to respond to the multiple challenges facing europe today, it must find ways to develop its capacity for innovation - with the aim of providing viable and effective solutions. in this context, i voted in favour of mr lamberts' report on the strengthening of the european institute of technology (eit), because it is moving in the right direction in terms of strengthening the capacity for innovation at european level. the eit promotes closer cooperation between universities, research centres and businesses, thus enhancing the eu's capacity for innovation.
in writing. the kke meps voted against all the regulations of the eu research and innovation programme horizon 2020 because it strengthens the subordination of university research and public research centres to the needs of european monopoly groups and orients research to new areas of profitability, as defined by the eu's reactive europe 2020 strategy. the eu's strategic research framework is accelerating reactive restructuring at universities and research institutions. the partnerships between universities and businesses are turning over to big business the university and public infrastructure, its researchers and staff. horizon 2020 will not fund research programmes to meet the growing needs of the people, but only those that serve monopoly profits, the strengthening of repressive mechanisms, and for the first time also linked to military purposes. through the 'centres of excellence', which will collect the largest share of funding, the monopolisation of knowledge is increasing and the weakening of public research centres and infrastructures across the eu, as they do not meet the requirements of business competitiveness.
in writing. the debate on the need for reliable and accurate european statistics is not new. european statistics have two main purposes, both of equal importance: the commission's communication on the future of the european union's external relations is based on the following principles: first, it highlights important elements that shape policy decisions and, second, it acts as a barometer for the state of our economy and society. unfortunately, there is a crisis of confidence in european statistics among the public today. the public debt crisis has actually made the situation worse. the present report, which i voted in favour of, therefore comes at an important time. it is clear that there must be a system of checks and balances between the european statistical office (eurostat) and the audit authorities at national level in order to enable all parties to control the production, quality and reliability of statistical data. in addition, a detailed review of their work should be carried out on a semi-annual basis and a statistical code of practice should be drawn up, which should be applied by all to any relevant research.
in writing. this report brings together the progress, eures and the microfinance mechanism programmes into a single programme for social change and innovation, which i supported. these programmes provide practical applications for small enterprises to access microcredit facilities. this is particularly possible through progress. at the same time, eures provides access to cross-border advisory services, which is very important for the greek border regions. in its current form, the programme provides the above tools by reflecting existing labour relations and conditions regarding free movement and cross-border mobility, but in its new form it will be strengthened by setting clear objectives for social progress and employment and will support proposals, ideas and actions driven by a spirit of social innovation and social experimentation. the programme is centred on the fight against unemployment and poverty. in order to maximise the impact within its scope, this strategy will gain new potential, for example by providing funding for specific mobility and cross-border partnership programmes as well as for the training and qualification of eures staff. the programme's greatest effectiveness lies in better coordination between the relevant actors and civil society.
in writing. the life 2014-2020 programme aims primarily at developing and maintaining a european union network of enthusiastic promoters of eu environmental strategies and policies. ngos, research and other institutions, as well as design and technical consultancy firms, are being mobilised to this end, drawing on eu funding. experience of the programme's implementation in the period 2007-2013 in our country shows that the 10 actions financed with eur 14.9 million were distributed among three private companies, three well-known and non-exceptional ngos and two public research foundations. the new version of the programme is innovative in that it includes 'integrated projects', i.e. projects promoting the privatisation of both municipal waste management (with a focus on incineration) and collection, the privatisation of the country's water resources, the development and support, even in times of crisis, of trade in pollutants, the safeguarding of the environmental integrity of the res monopolies, especially wind turbine monopolies, and the penetration of monopolies in the electricity sector. the programme provides for a community contribution of up to 80%, as it constitutes another pillar of support for the monopolies.
in writing. the life programme is considered one of the most successful european environmental action programmes. it should be noted that in the 20 years of the programme's existence, greece has benefited not only from the initiation and implementation of targeted and planned actions but also from the response to emergency needs and natural disasters. the programme's contribution to the regeneration of the national forest of parnitas and to the restoration of the natural environment following the devastating fires in the peloponnese is cited, for example. the role of the natura network in protecting greece's unique natural landscapes and beauties should also be emphasised. life's share of the overall eu budget, however, remains small at 0.3%, which is not justified by the major challenges that require an integrated environmental strategy in europe and the member states. the present report, which i voted in favour of, proposes an increase in the resources allocated to the life programme, while at the same time - and this directly concerns our country - it provides for an increase in the rate of community participation (up to 75%) in the less economically developed european regions.
mr president, i congratulate mrs rodust and mr stevenson on the important work they have done and i acknowledge the excellent contribution of commissioner damannakis. this is the first time that the reform of the fisheries policy has been voted through the co-decision procedure, and this is a victory for the european parliament and the european institutions. the aim of the reform is to restore the viability of fisheries immediately, to combat overfishing and unacceptable practices such as discarding. all of this is now protected and enforced through a regulatory framework. the whole process, the result of many hours of meetings and negotiations, will be meaningless if the member states do not comply with the rules we have voted for and if there is no effective and reliable system of monitoring and control. the report on the organization of the market in fish farming is considered very important and crucial at a time when the european industry is called upon to compete with those of third countries.
rapporteur. − mr president, we are here to discuss a very thorny issue in parliament: the proposal for the north-east atlantic fisheries and deep-sea fisheries. what happened was that our fishermen were fishing in the coastal atlantic waters. once fish stocks were depleted there, they moved further inside the great depths of the atlantic, fishing deeper and deeper into the sea, reaching more and more seabeds. this led to the discovery of new fisheries, fish that were argued over at the beginning, fish that had never been targeted before. but what we soon found was that the fish that live at such great depths are very vulnerable. sometimes they are only able to reproduce once they reach 35; sometimes they live for over 100 years, or even 150. this means that if you catch the big ones their stocks are quickly depleted – and this is what happened. we have cases where fish stocks were depleted in just two years and, 20 years later, they have still to recover. in 2002 we had the first access regime that brought forth some improvements but fish stocks did not return to sustainable levels and the commission has now come up with another proposal to phase out bottom trawling over a two-year period. the debate started in parliament soon after the commission released its proposal but there were severe delays. it was the first case i know of where the procedure was postponed so many times. we had two hearings – not one, but two hearings – on the issue and it was on the verge of not being voted or negotiated, for practical time reasons or simply because of big blocking majorities. members worked very intensely to find common ground within the committee on fisheries and i would like to thank carmen fraga estévez and pat the cope gallagher and all the shadows who helped to bridge the huge gaps. one side wanted very limited things while the other side wanted the commission proposal and even more. the compromise that was reached is a compromise without the commission’s basic proposal – the bottom trawling ban. there was an underlying agreement, however, that this compromise would be supported by the member states in the council. all this month i have tried to talk with the member states involved. i failed to get any commitment that this compromise can be the final outcome of negotiations. the socialist group, just one hour ago, decided to vote in favour of the introduction of the bottom trawling ban.
rapporteur. − madam president, tomorrow this plenary might decide to kill the report; it is a risk that we consciously take. can i explain why? as mr milana said, for 18 months, for three whole presidencies, the council has not even presented this issue, opened, or even started discussing this issue. when we reached this huge compromise we actually gave the industry what it wanted: to avoid the threat of banning deep sea trawling. in exchange we got the protection of the vulnerable marine ecosystems. colleagues, how many of you came to me and told me that this is a compromise which can fly in the council, that this is a compromise which can be endorsed by the council? and yet, one month after we adopted our compromise, the council did not put it on the agenda, did not even present it. i used this time to talk to the member states and the presidencies. the best we could get as a commitment from their side was: ‘vote and we will see’ or ‘we prefer your compromise to the commission proposal, but let us come together for negotiations’. if this compromise is further watered down it will become meaningless. our choice tomorrow is to go for a strong mandate with a ban and go for negotiations, or go for negotiations using our mandate as a starting point and end up with something meaningless. that is why in the socialist group we took this decision to support the reintroduction of the ban.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, tuesday 10 december 2013, at 11.30.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for finland concerning redundancies in nokia plc, nokia siemens networks and 30 suppliers and subcontractors, with the total amount of aid amounting to eur 9.81 million. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and i am therefore very pleased that the commission has taken this step. however, targeted financial support from the european globalisation adjustment fund will be able to provide redundant workers with those opportunities for retraining and training which will undoubtedly prove useful in their search for new employment.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) for denmark to support the redundancies at the vestas group wind turbine manufacturing company. the aid amounts to a total of eur 6 364 643. the group had already had to make large-scale redundancies in 2009-2010, and a new wave of redundancies followed in 2012. this is the third case where the fund is mobilised for the vestas group and the fourth case concerning the wind turbine manufacturing sector. it is clear that the negative effects of globalisation further highlight the added value of the etuc as an eu social policy tool, while the support provided is the practical expression of union solidarity. finally, the case of denmark is the best example of how this financial instrument can be equally useful in helping the reintegration of the displaced into the labour market even in economically strong countries.
in writing. the eu-canada free trade and trade agreement strengthens international monopoly groups, their penetration and free movement in markets in eu member states and canada. for the working class it will mean even greater exploitation of their labour power, for the poor self-employed and peasants even more rapid destruction. the use of names for 'protected designation of origin' (pdo) products is also a characteristic evidence of the objectives and interests served by the eu-canada agreement and its disastrous effects. among other things, the agreement grants canada the right to use the name of products with a protected designation of origin, such as feta and olive trees from the kalamoni, which are characteristic of the rural landscape of our country, in order to serve the interests of business groups. this will have dramatic consequences on the income of poor farmers, producers of these products and the self-employed who are active in the processing sector. the eu, its trade policy, the cap, its overall anti-people policy only cause suffering for the working people. the solution for growth for the benefit of the people is to disengage from the eu and to design the economy from the people's power, the only way to meet the people's needs.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it focuses specifically on ccps and csds as key critical market infrastructures that require strong recovery and resolution rules as a priority. the report also acknowledges that there are many other entities that fall under the non-bank financial institutions, including insurance companies, asset managers and payment systems. however, international work in these areas has either not yet been completed or further discussions and assessments are expected to take place in this regard.
in writing. the kke voted against the report on the eu's space industrial policy because it puts space telecommunications, navigation and geospatial services at the service of the eu's reactionary europe 2020 strategy and the interests of the war industry monopolies. the possibilities offered by space and space services can now be used to meet the needs of workers in the fields of environmental protection, transport, public education, health and culture, and protection against natural disasters. the eu is seeking to use them instead to strengthen its military means and its repressive mechanisms (drone bombers and other new generation weapons systems). the report calls for the use of eu programmes (copernicus, galileo) to further concentrate and monopolise the space industry by fewer monopolistic groups, with their multi-billion euro state subsidies. it promotes the militarization of areas of public administration and the eu research program horizon 2020 to orientate to profitable areas of exit of the over-accumulated capital, which is lagging due to the capitalist crisis, intensifying competition with the us and other imperialist centers, mutual blackmail in key military and non-military sectors.
in writing. the european parliament's final vote in favour of the eu's common fisheries policy (cfp) for 2014-2020 is a crushing blow to poor and middle-class fishermen. the cfp is being revised downwards under the pretext of controlling fish stocks. overfishing has been the eu's pretext for decades for measures that have led to a dramatic reduction in small and medium-sized fishermen and the growth of large and small-scale fish farms. the ban on discards will have a decisive impact on small fishermen, since only the large fishermen can bear the cost. the fleet reduction measures and quotas are moving in exactly the same direction, dealing a blow to the middle and coastal fisheries, with dramatic consequences for island countries such as greece. this pro-monopoly orientation of the cap is complemented by eu maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management, which allocates and delineates areas of activity and exploitation in coastal zones for the benefit of business groups operating in the various sectors linked to all economic activities related to the sea, from fishing and fish farming to tourism and energy production from the sea.
in writing. the present recommendation, which i supported, creates a reform framework capable of addressing issues such as continued overfishing, the biggest problem of the common fisheries policy. the european parliament has therefore called for future decisions on the principle of maximum sustainable yield, a fundamental rule for the sustainable management of fisheries, to be legally binding. the benefits of this initiative are manifold, as it enhances marine biodiversity and fisheries fleets, as the costs of fishing activity are reduced in proportion to the density of the stocks. the new text also raises the issue outside european waters, in line with the acquis of the united nations convention on the law of the sea, on the right to fish in the event of surplus and non-exploitation by third countries. it also provides for safeguards in cases of human rights violations and makes compliance with the rules of the common fisheries policy and the rules to be laid down in the regulation on the european maritime and fisheries fund a precondition for european funding. finally, it should be emphasised how the new strategy enhances transparency by setting ecological, social and economic conditions, with emphasis on the availability of scientific data.
in writing. the kke voted against the report on the eu's civil protection mechanism because it increases the militarization of the eu, since it explicitly provides for the use and mobilization of military forces and means, both in eu member states and in third countries. in this way, the 'civil protection mechanism' is another instrument of eu military intervention, under the pretext of 'human-made disasters' and 'the fight against terrorism'. it is part of the revision of the eu's common security and defence policy, which increases its aggressive character and strengthens its imperialist policy and interventions throughout the world. the eu is seeking to use its so-called 'non-military' missions as 'rapid intervention forces', which it will immediately convert into military missions, depending on the needs of its imperialist interventions. the eu is strengthening its military structures, reforming existing ones and creating new ones in order to make it more effective in attacking peoples, both within itself and in other countries, in the context of its inter-imperialist rivalries with other imperialist powers, for control of the wealth-producing resources and the savage exploitation of the peoples.
in writing. the effects of climate change and the increased frequency of severe natural and man-made disasters in recent years make it extremely important and necessary to have a designed european framework for civil protection. i supported this report because it strengthens the institutional framework for cooperation on civil protection, which remains primarily a national responsibility. the european civil protection mechanism emphasises the design of training programmes and the exchange of experience and good practice, while enhancing the role of volunteers and the transparency of funding. at the same time, it highlights the role of local and regional authorities and emphasises prevention, preparedness and risk assessment, particularly in terms of their financial support. in this case, budgetary synergies between the structural funds, the solidarity fund and any related financial mechanism shall be possible, facilitating better implementation of the civil protection mechanism.
in writing. civil protection in the union is currently governed by two legal acts: one on the community civil protection mechanism and one on the relevant financial instrument covering the period 2013-2020. the establishment of the new multiannual financial framework and the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon is a good time to review the existing legal framework. the number and severity of natural and man-made disasters have increased.
in writing. the report on mortgage loans does introduce some measures to better regulate the mortgage market, but it does not touch on the essence of the problem. at the heart of the problem was the over-hyped mortgage lending in the eu, which failed to replace the welfare state, and the direct linking of housing loans to 'innovative financial products', with the sole aim of making banks profitable. the simple regulation of the market in advance is one part. today the housing problem has another very important dimension: we are also called upon to respond to the subsequent problem of the inability to service loans. european households are suffocating in debt. 70% of their debts are concentrated in mortgage loans and the number of non-performing loans is expected to increase in the coming period in many countries. in greece already more than 20% of housing loans cannot be serviced. european citizens are already suffering from the devastating effects of the crisis and austerity policies; the seizure and housing of the banks will be the final blow. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures.
in writing. volunteering is a prerequisite for encouraging citizens to participate in society. it is the driver of social capital and growth in europe, as well as economic and social cohesion, contributing to the europe 2020 development strategy. the report, which i voted in favour of, sets out the guidelines for the creation of a coordinated policy on volunteering, which is currently fragmented into various services. it promotes values such as solidarity and non-discrimination, and fosters the development of participatory democracy and the promotion of human rights within and outside the eu. although in many member states volunteering is not legally enshrined, the work of the european year of volunteering 2011 has highlighted issues such as strengthening the community framework for the transparency of professional qualifications and competences, the need to establish immediately a pan-european framework for the recognition of non-formal education and a wider mobility network recognising volunteering and social work, the integration of skills acquired through volunteering into the ects system, the creation of a fund for the development of the relevant european centres, with the aim of creating infrastructure, but also supporting the insurance coverage of volunteers.
in writing. i voted for it. volunteering is a key pillar of our democratic and pluralist societies and an expression of commitment, support and active participation in social life. in order to celebrate and promote the commitment of volunteers and organisations working with volunteers and to address the challenges facing the voluntary sector, such as the reduction of the volunteering base or the shift from long-term commitments to specific short-term projects, the initiative to establish a european year of volunteering is welcome.

the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - madam president, in my committee's opinion, the partnership instrument is aimed in particular at partnership and cooperation between the european union and countries which are abandoning development aid and are becoming partners of economic interest to the european union, such as china, russia and latin america. through this regulation, the european union should contribute to the implementation of the europe 2020 strategy, in particular the objectives relating to security of energy supply and improved market access for european businesses. these issues are vital to the recovery of the european union's economy. i believe that special attention should be paid to supporting small and mediumsized enterprises affected by the economic crisis, to the internationalisation of small and mediumsized enterprises in the european union, because they play a very important role in our economy.

mr president, we are talking about the next european council at a time when the people of europe are suffering from the european union's austerity policies, at a time when european democratic and social achievements are being destroyed in the name of competitiveness and profit. mr president, at the next summit the leaders will discuss the economic situation of the union, the economic situation of emu. we shall hear again 'we got a taste' of the banking union, of the european semester, of financial governance. but we will not hear, because we will be ignored once again, about the disastrous effects of these policies. we will not hear, for example, about the one and a half million unemployed in greece, we will not hear that thousands of households in greece today have no electricity, we will not hear that in greece - a country which is under the close supervision of all the institutions of the european union - the poor are getting poorer and the rich richer, while the blackmailing of greek housewives for their auctions and deposits will continue. mr president, such a european union, such a council, such leaders are out of step with developments, they do not respond to the interests of the european citizens. we on the left will fight for radical changes and upheavals so that we can speak of a europe of work, solidarity and democracy.

mr president, it has been ten years since the european security strategy was adopted in 2003 and five years since the debate on its implementation. the european council in december had therefore raised a lot of ambition in the debate on defence. we have sent a strong message to the previous plenary session, by a large majority in the european parliament, that we must also make progress in the field of defence and security, that the common defence and security policy is an integral part of the process of european integration, that if we do not make progress, the union will not be able to play its role as an international actor and provider of security and will remain a single large market. we stressed that the economic crisis had led to necessary cuts in the defence budget of the member states, forcing us to do more with less, and that the solution was coordination, cooperation and confidence-building between partners. we have said that the common defence and security policy must be visible, that it cannot be discussed at the level of the elite but that it needs the participation and support of the citizens. we have proposed concrete measures: the application of the provisions of the treaty of lisbon on permanent structured cooperation, the start-up fund or article 44 to mandate a group of member states to undertake specific tasks. we have asked for the institution of a council of defence ministers, regular discussions at european council level, a road map for the next steps. unfortunately, from what we've seen so far, none of that is going to happen. beyond the adoption of the five pilot programmes, beyond the promise of a reassessment of the situation in june 2015, nothing concrete. general references and paraphrases of well-known slogans on the importance of defence. the lack of political will, the lack of courage, the national egos seem to be prevailing once again and are leading the european council to the lowest possible denominator. it's a shame! the solution is more europe, not less europe, but the member states are cowardly postponing necessary decisions, big decisions, for the future. i repeat, this is a shame!

in writing. the european globalisation adjustment fund, with a budget of €150 million per year, helps young people who have lost their jobs because their company has closed or relocated, to find work and to acquire new skills. the report, which i voted in favour of, states that funding will be provided for measures to help young people find employment, vocational guidance and targeted training, as well as for the provision of counselling services and the promotion of entrepreneurship. it is important that greece finally starts to make use of the possibilities offered by the fund to potential beneficiaries, which will include, under the new regulation, young people not in employment, education or training who live in areas with a particularly high rate of youth unemployment, such as our country, which has the sadly lowest youth unemployment rate in europe.

the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. i would like to thank mrs meissner for her excellent cooperation and congratulate her on the excellent report. the directive on maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management is a key instrument for the implementation of blue development. the new maritime sectors that have emerged in recent years are poised to contribute to much-needed economic growth, to contribute over one and a half million jobs, and even new ones; the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the creation of a european marine research area, the development of marine renewable energy, biotechnology, and advanced forms of tourism. it should be stressed that the directive will simplify and facilitate investment, especially in cross-border maritime areas. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the marine environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the disorderly and simultaneous development of many activities at sea and on the coasts threatens the fragile marine environment, an environment which, at least in the mediterranean, is already under enormous man-made pressures. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the marine environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the report as it stands calls on neighbouring countries to aim at a common vision for the allocation of marine uses in our common sea for reasons of economic efficiency and environmental protection. finally, our proposal ensures that coastal and marine areas are open to the public for recreational and other activities. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the marine environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done in this area.
mr president, an integrated maritime policy and maritime spatial planning can and must be tools for growth, job creation and the achievement of the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. the union, especially in these difficult times of crisis, needs to focus on developing maritime activities, which employ five million people and generate an eu added value of some eur 500 billion. it is very important to coordinate the different actions of the maritime policy at political level in order to optimise the benefits for the member states and european citizens. in this context i welcome the report by mrs meissner on maritime spatial planning and consider it a very important initiative. the existence of a european maritime spatial planning requires the union to move towards the control, stability and security of the coastal and territorial waters, exclusive economic zones, continental shelves, maritime infrastructure and marine resources of the member states of the union.
member of the commission. − mr president, the commission considers that the implementation of maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal management across the eu’s sea basins is key to facilitating the sustainable growth of the european maritime economy. the european parliament has been very supportive of the commission’s proposal and approach and has made constructive contributions. let me just highlight some aspects of the commission’s position on this subsidiarity issue that have been raised during today’s debate. in the commission’s view, eu action in this area is justified given that, to a large extent, maritime and coastal activities and related policies have cross-border, regional, and often eu-wide impacts. risks related to natural hazards and climate change go beyond national borders. as tools, maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal management, when established in a harmonised procedural framework, could significantly contribute to coherent approaches across sea basins. i would also say that the provisions of the proposed directive do not affect town or county planning for integrated coastal management provisions but only impose obligations in relation to the appropriate coordination of relevant policies affecting the coastal area. they do not interfere with member states’ prerogative as regards policy choices related to town and county matters. let me conclude by reiterating the commission’s thanks for the work done on these proposals by the rapporteur, ms meissner, and the shadow rapporteurs, mr pirillo and ms thomas. the commission looks forward to the launch of trialogue negotiations on this file and we are ready to facilitate rapid agreement.
in writing. both maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal zone management are equally important tools if we want to combine maritime and land-based planning and achieve sustainable and coherent use of seas and coasts. although some member states have an exemplary approach, differences between member states are large and a european framework directive is necessary to ensure that marine activities are supported by appropriate coastal infrastructure, that the fragile coastal environment is protected and that citizens and interest groups are representatively and effectively involved in the decision-making process. there is an attempt to weaken significantly the provisions of the directive concerning integrated coastal zone management due to unfounded concerns about unforeseen consequences for member states' land-use planning processes. however, iczm supports the principle of subsidiarity and without the inclusion of the cwfd in this directive the provisions on maritime spatial planning would be much weaker. i therefore call on you to vote against amendment no 21 to article 1 or to support the separate vote to delete the phrase 'on a case-by-case basis' which makes the gats non-binding.
mr president, in this year’s report we have worked on the non-standard monetary operations carried out by the bank, the ltros and the omt, which played a very critical role in averting the contagion of the crisis. however this is not enough to get us out of the woods. at the moment, when the european economy is still desperately struggling for growth, and unemployment is reaching unacceptable levels, the ecb could and should do more. let me give you two examples: in our debates we have consistently raised the issue of the credit crutch in the real economy, along the line that the cheap liquidity you are providing for banks has never reached the real economy but was instead used by banks for profits and capital formation. as you know, the problem is extremely acute in crisis countries like greece where rates have increased, not decreased, and where healthy smes are suffering because of lack of liquidity and large corporations often have to leave the country in order to get access to cheap credit. mr draghi said last week that if he were to do an operation similar to the ltro he would want it to be used for the real economy and this is absolutely the right approach. but i am wondering, mr draghi, what you are waiting for before using this tool and making sure that your monetary policy is transmitted effectively across all the euro area, to help struggling but healthy smes survive and to help them preserve existing jobs and create new ones. finally, a last point about what the ecb could do. one of the conclusions of the independent annual growth survey is that an aggressive monetary policy to reduce sovereign spreads would, together with institutional advances, have an accumulated effect of 3% of gdp in ireland and 8% of gdp in spain and greece, not only helping in the recovery from the crisis but also contributing considerably to the sustainability of public debt. so why does this option remain excluded?
the s&d group is calling for a new approach to the problem of the environment. the resolution which we hope will be adopted today by the european parliament's plenary by a large majority, setting measurable and concrete targets to combat tax evasion and avoidance, is a strong message from the european parliament to the european commission, the council and the governments. the commission's proposal is that the commission should be able to take part in the negotiations on the new agreement, and that the commission should be able to take part in the negotiations on the new agreement. the citizens of the union, the taxpayers, have borne a huge burden during this crisis, largely because we have failed to produce the results we need in the fight against tax evasion and tax avoidance. one trillion euros a year of lost revenue in the european union from tax evasion and tax avoidance. and although i fully acknowledge the effort made by commissioner šemeta, what i also understood from your speech is that there is a ping-pong of responsibility being played between the commission and governments on the backs of european citizens, european taxpayers, who in turn of course turn their backs on europe. parliament wants to put an end to this unacceptable situation. we must give credibility to the commitments to tax justice and to reduce the tax gap, and to do this we must show that we give the fight against tax fraud, tax evasion and tax avoidance the same special weight, commissioner, as we give to fiscal stability. let us not just look at the deficit and debt reduction indicators! let us establish country-by-country indicators! the commission has already made a number of proposals. we must establish indices for each country and for europe as a whole to measure tax evasion! then, set concrete targets to reduce the fiscal gap at european and national level and decide to follow them up in the context of the europe 2020 strategy and the european semester! the member states must do more - you are right, commissioner - but the issue of tax evasion and tax avoidance cannot be tackled effectively at national level alone. it takes a collective effort and commitment from all of us.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i agree with you on the seriousness and the scale of the problem of tax evasion. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in my view, ladies and gentlemen, the problem of tax evasion and avoidance is not simply a disease in the tax system of the european union. it is a condition for the existence and perpetuation of this anti-people, neoliberal model, based on the redistribution of wealth and the profitability of big business. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat tax evasion, and it is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the taxation of companies and the taxation of individuals. on the contrary, we go to the tenant, the pensioner, and we tax them, we put a fine on them and we criminalise, in greece at least, even the right to housing, so that we can save on the necessary taxes. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
mr president, tackling tax evasion and tax fraud is a priority that has not rightly emerged as a major european policy, because tax evasion undermines europe's competitiveness, pushes member states to higher tax rates, discourages investment and is one of the most heinous forms of social injustice. the resolution in question highlights these points, but the increase in tax revenue that it is talking about must not mean an increase in the size of the state. the revenue from tax evasion should be used to drastically reduce tax rates, especially in countries with high levels of unemployment, which need strong incentives for investment and growth. we need less taxation and smaller states in these member states, something which the various troika do not seem to have sufficiently understood. it is positive that the resolution and the commission speak of automatic exchange of information, but this should above all apply within countries by linking cash registers to finance ministries, a measure that resolutely combats vat theft and which, for some mysterious reason, in my own country, greece, the troika does not promote, after passively monitoring the relevant legislation since 2011, the regulation to be postponed indefinitely. the commission often follows different criteria in its broader european policy and in the issues it promotes as a troika. in greece at least ... (the president cut off the speaker)
mr president, as rapporteur for the green group on eco-innovation, i have supported the quality report of a colleague and we have contributed some of our own proposals. green innovation is an opportunity to change our economy and create jobs while protecting the environment and strengthening social cohesion. i think that would be a very smart policy in this period of deep crisis, where we need not cuts in wages and the welfare state but strengthening of those economic activities which are much more sustainable. eco-innovation is combined and linked with technological innovation, involving local communities, and that is why it is very important. we agree that until now - and this is right - there has been a strengthening of the interventions with demonstration programmes, but we believe that it should be gradually extended to all economic activities and all funds, that is to say, we should go further. (the president cut off the speaker)
mr president, as a co-signatory of the resolution on tax avoidance and tax evasion and on behalf of the greek delegation, which has a flight to catch, i would like to kindly ask you, if the chamber agrees, to modify the order of votes and vote for this resolution just after item 11 of our agenda on generalised tariff preferences. i know this is an extraordinary request but the circumstances are extraordinary.
mr president, i think mrs podimata is right. for the same reasons as those given by the commission, i too would ask that the vote on taxes be taken before the resolutions.
i would like to propose, mr president, that a sentence be added to the end of amendment no 36, on which the rapporteur agrees. it's more of an explanation. the text of the amendment is thus: 'marine spatial plans and integrated coastal zone management strategies shall apply an ecosystem-based approach' and the addition is: 'taking economic, social and environmental criteria into account as equals'.
in writing. - the i voted in favour of this report as the arguments put forward by the general court are convincing and the need for the appointment of additional judges is demonstrated. it is extremely important that the general court be able to rule on cases within a reasonable time. the biggest problem, however, is the appointment of judges. the problem was delayed even in the first attempt to solve it. this report makes a specific proposal which does not take account of the nationality of the additional judges. the interests of the member states in the allocation of seats must not undermine the rule of law in the eu.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report. in times of a general economic crisis, a period which particularly threatens our sense of belonging to europe, it is important to create incentives for long-term and manageable cultural initiatives. in this context, a year-long programme of cultural activities should be established through the cooperation of actors from the various sectors involved: from the artistic world, as well as from leaders and protagonists of social volunteering, and those involved in scientific research and environmental protection. for these reasons, the proposal aims to give greater weight to the theme of 'common roots' of europe, both in an abstract or idealised way and in a tangible approach through the protection of cultural heritage sites that embody the heart of european cultural consciousness.
in writing. the joint resolution of the parties of capital and the euro-arabist party on the non-ratification of the eu-ukraine free trade agreement at the recent eastern partnership summit, which was voted for by the european parliament, constitutes a provocative intervention for the penetration of the eu monopolies in ukraine. the political representatives of eu capital are taking an active part in the acute rivalries raging in the region, mainly with russia but also with the other imperialist centres. the conflict has nothing to do with the interests of the people of ukraine and the other peoples of the region. it is a competition between bandits fighting for the spoils: the resources, especially energy resources, the transport routes, the exploitation of the peoples of the wider region. the people must not choose an imperialist. not to enlist under the foreign flag of the bourgeoisie, but to fight under their own flag, to strengthen the anti-capitalist anti-monopoly struggle to overthrow the power of capital, to open the way for workers' popular power, a basic condition for meeting the modern needs of the working people.
in writing. the kke voted against the joint resolution of the "grand alliance" of the people's party, social democrats and liberals on the preparation of the eu summit on 19-20 december 2013, because it promotes and strengthens the eu's aggression against peoples both within itself and in third countries. it called on the summit to speed up the completion of the banking union, to deepen capitalist restructuring in all member states, and to intensify the exploitation of workers to safeguard the profits of business groups. calls for the military arm of the imperialist european union, the common security and defence policy (csdp), to be strengthened so that it can intervene by all means throughout the world, in cooperation with nato and independently; the european union must be able to take action to combat terrorism and to ensure that the european union is able to take action to combat terrorism. the doctrine it promotes is: 'the eu must intervene everywhere'. it promotes the concentration of the military industry in the hands of large monopoly enterprises, the undermining and dismantling of national defence industries that do not meet the needs of the eu's imperialist strategic plans. the eu is the enemy of the people. the fight to weaken and finally dismantle it is the only option that serves the interests of the people.
